- **14.10.2026 - the Uber import asks for Uber's own week, and the period is chosen rather than typed.** Four merges, two per side, and the two halves must stay in step.
  - **The week is Uber's, not the calendar's.** Uber's operating day starts at **04:00 Europe/Berlin**, so its weeks run Monday 04:00 -> next Monday 04:00; his Fleet Hub lists them as `05/10/2026 04:01 AM - 12/10/2026 04:01 AM` and the 04:01/04:02 drift is Uber's own display. We asked for midnights, so our totals could not match the figures he reads there. Reidey PR #215 moved the window with `setTime`, **not** `addHours`, so 04:00 holds on the two DST nights. **Measured, on his data: 5 of 2151 rides in August 2026 start on a Monday before 04:00** - 0.23%, small and not nothing. And matching Uber is the *consistent* choice, not merely the requested one: the weekly rows he types by hand are copied off Uber's screen, so his history already carries Uber's boundary.
  - **The period picker replaced the two date fields** (PR #216). Two tabs, weekly by default: a list of Uber's weeks under month headings, each reading `Woche N [05.10.2026 04:00 - 12.10.2026 04:00]` where **N is the number the books will carry** (`weekNumberOf`, the index inside the business month) rather than a fresh 1,2,3 over the chosen range; and a monthly tab that takes a business month's whole 4 or 5 weeks. Weekly multi-select is **one contiguous run** because the pull takes a single `from`/`to` and would fetch a skipped week anyway. The running week is **first** in its month group with a `läuft` badge, not last as first specified - at the bottom of a 26-week list it would have been unreachable.
  - **The cap had to move on both sides, or the monthly tab was impossible.** A five-week business month spans **35 days** and both caps were **31**, so every such month was refused `range_exceeds_31_days` before it reached Uber. `MAX_RANGE_DAYS` and `MAX_EARNINGS_DAYS` are **38** now (PRs #216 and #217): 35 for the month, room for a partial piece at either end, and - what the number is really about - El-Professor calls Reidey **once per week**, so the cap bounds the outbound calls at six. Reidey's refusal code carries the number (`range_exceeds_<n>_days`), which the browser matched as an exact string and would have shown as a raw code; it reads as `range_too_long` now.
  - **A deploy failed and production was untouched.** Reidey's deploy of `8c3dad0` failed in the Next.js image build on `next/font/google` (`Inter Tight`, module-not-found) - a Google Fonts fetch during the Docker build. Its own script printed *"deploy failed before anything live changed - production is untouched"*. The deploy immediately before it, `ce00cee`, is the one that actually changed `frontend/`, and it succeeded; my commit on top touches one backend constant and its tests. So the frontend bytes were identical across a success and a failure, which is what identified it as transient rather than a regression. A re-run succeeded.
  - **Two sessions in one checkout destroyed work that belonged to neither.** Another Claude session was in `C:\laragon\www\DASHCAM` at the same time; one of its subagents ran `git checkout` on `ElProfessorController.php` and `ElProfessorConnectionTest.php`, discarding about **+42/-3 uncommitted lines** - never staged, so no dangling blob, no stash, and no editor history. Neither session had written them. The tree was then switched back under a running subagent of mine, whose next commit would have landed on the other branch; **stopping it was the urgent act**, ahead of any recovery attempt. Reidey work now happens in `C:\laragon\www\DASHCAM-earnings`, a worktree with its **own** `vendor` - a borrowed one makes PHP resolve `__DIR__` through the junction and test the other tree.

- **13.10.2026 — 173 money rows on PRODUCTION are attributed to a driver name no driver row carries, and 29 of them were caused by a probe.** Measured read-only on both databases; **nothing was written, on either**.
  - **`probe-rename` is a real driver.** `drivers.name` on `0dcb7f15-6c11-4850-907f-04c742d24433` (Y&A Mobility) was overwritten with `probe-rename` and never restored — on **production as well as staging**. It carries **9 trips, all 01.02.2026**, and every one of them has `original_driver_name` *and* `assigned_driver_name` = **`Jehad Al Daher`**. The denormalised name is written at assignment time, so a probe that had *created* a driver and moved trips onto it would have left the probe's name there; `Jehad Al Daher` means the trips were assigned while the row still had its own name, and **only the `name` column moved**. No other driver row on production carries that name, so the row is him. Staging additionally has `ffd94887-…` overwritten the same way, which is **Yassin Asfour** — 1890 trips, a salary and 5 saved Stundennachweise — and that one is staging-only.
  - **Why it costs money and not just a label.** `driver_name` on `income` and `receipts` is **text with no foreign key**, and every read joins on it. Renaming the driver row cut **19 income rows and 10 receipts** (production; the same on staging) loose from any driver: they are not wrong, they are **unattributable**, so they appear under nobody on the Umsätze screen and in the weekly Abrechnung.
  - **And the sweep found three more names that have nothing to do with the probe**, on production: `Jehad Aljolan` (45 receipts + 32 income), `YA` (32 receipts), `Osama Tadmuri` (18 + 17) — **144 rows** whose `driver_name` matches no driver row in the same company. That is ordinary use, not a test: a driver deleted or renamed takes their money's only link with them. **This is the structural finding**, and the probe is one instance of it.
  - **What was deliberately NOT done.** The owner asked for `probe-rename` to be deleted, believing it a test artifact. Deleting it would have destroyed 9 trips' attribution on production and, on staging, 1890 trips, a salary and 5 Stundennachweise. The correct repair is a one-row `UPDATE drivers SET name = 'Jehad Al Daher'`, which is a **production data change and therefore the owner's to authorise**; it is open, with the staging pair beside it, and so is what the other three names are.

- **13.10.2026 — Uber earnings import, all ten note types, two labels, and a staging deploy I broke. Read the Uber bullet about the first live pull before relying on the import.** Four merges on `staging` (`e43c7b9`, `07ae68f`, `6bab75e`; `6c2b324`; `8bb7d42`, `ce3da45`; `183909c`). **Production is untouched**; `staging` is 217 commits ahead of `main`, and promoting is the owner's button.
  - **The Uber earnings import (`20261013120000_income_import_uber.sql`, `uber-earnings-pull`, `UberEarningsImportModal`).** The owner typed every driver's weekly earnings by hand on the Umsätze page; the button pulls them from Reidey instead: a free date range, a **preview he confirms**, then the write. **Automation was dropped by him on 13.10.2026 (*"بدناش اتمتة انساها"*) and REVERSED by him on 15.10.2026** — see the first bullet of this file; the button below is unchanged and the schedule is a second door beside it. **Why the bridge is Reidey:** his own browser-console script works because his browser holds Uber cookies on `fleethub.uber.com`; from `elprofessor.eu` CORS forbids it, and Reidey is the only part of the system holding an Uber session on a server.
  - **The edge function writes nothing.** The rows go in through the RPC `income_import_uber` under the caller's own session and gate, so the database's door (role, company state, a confirmed `partner_driver_links` row, the unique index) stays the only way in. **One call per business week**, because a week belongs to the month containing its **Monday** and one `income` row is written per driver per type per week; `Woche N` goes into `income.description`, which `IncomeManagement` matches back with a regex — it is data, not a label. `driver_name` is written from **our** `drivers` row verbatim, because it is text with no foreign key and every read joins on it.
  - **Replace mode (`20261014100000_income_import_uber_replace.sql`, owner 14.10.2026).** A week pulled twice was refused for good; he chose *replace*, the new figure only (not both rows, not a cancelled flag). `income_import_uber(p_rows, p_replace boolean DEFAULT false)` deletes first and inserts second in one call, keyed on the unique index's own columns with `company_id = get_active_company_id()` and `source = 'uber'` (the function's constant), each driver key only through a confirmed `partner_driver_links` row. Hand-typed rows have `source IS NULL` and cannot match. **Known limit, by the brief:** the key includes `type`, and the overlap check runs after the delete, so replacing a week with a payload that omits a type the old import held (tips that became 0) is refused (`already_imported`) with the old rows intact, not half-done. The RLS kit (section 7 of `tests/rls/income-import-uber.sql`) has not been run by the author; it is the coordinator's to run on the VPS.
  - **Two refusals, and the second is the one to understand.** A repeat is refused by a **partial unique index** on `income`, not by a browser check, so two tabs or a double click cannot get past it. The function also refuses an **overlapping** period, which a start-day key cannot see. An **unlinked driver, or an earner Reidey cannot place, stops the whole import and is named** (the owner's decision). **A support session imports nothing**: it is the customer's act. `tests/rls/income-import-uber.sql` is new and `tests/rls/matrix.sql` moved by 34 lines for it.
  - **The preview warns about typed rows (`07ae68f`).** Every week this owner has was typed by hand, so the first import lands on typed rows: the figures double, **and the salary basis (umsatz + aktionen) with them**, silently. The preview reads the linked drivers' income for the whole range in one query, whatever its source, and tells a **caution** (typed rows: both sets stand and the amounts add up) from a **refusal** (an earlier import overlaps, which the database enforces anyway). A lookup that failed or hit the **1000-row cap** reports itself **incomplete** rather than reporting none.
  - **The first live pull failed (`6bab75e`), and this change does not fix it.** The screen said only *"Reidey refused the request (uber_error)"*. `uber_error` is Reidey's catch-all for "Uber answered, **accepted** the stored session, and returned something other than 2xx, 401 or 403", so a 400 (our request shape), a 429 (rate limited) and a 5xx (theirs) arrive under one word and need three different fixes. Reidey already sent the status in `message` ("Fleet Hub answered 400."); our function read `reason` and dropped it. Now the sentence travels, capped at 200 characters and stripped of control characters, and is shown **only for a reason we have no wording of our own for** — a named refusal (`no_proxy`, `uber_session_rejected`, `too_many_pages`, `range_too_long`, `unreadable_amount`) keeps our sentence, because ours says what to do. **It is proven now, and the 400 was ours.** The query document was byte-for-byte the owner's working console script; the **variables** were not. Two coercion faults, each enough on its own: the millis went as **ints** where the script sends **strings**, and a Unix millisecond stamp is about 1.76e12 while GraphQL's `Int` is a **signed 32-bit** number capped at 2147483647 — so it fails at variable coercion, *before* execution, which is exactly why the answer was a bare 400 with no `errors` array to read; and the first page's `pageToken` went as `null` where the script sends `''`. Fixed in Reidey PR #214, merged and deployed to its production (`3994a3a`, no migrations in it). **The next pull returned real data** for 05.–11.10.2026: nine drivers with amounts, trips and cash, and **`Difference` 0,00 € on every row**, so Uber's own `netOutstanding` reconciles with our `(fare + promotion + tips) − cash` — which measures the classification, not just the connection. **The lesson is the one to keep:** every test on that path was `Http::fake`, which validates nothing about a remote schema, and one of them *pinned the defect* by asserting `=== $start` on an int. It asserts strings now, with a second test stating the overflow as an assertion so a later simplification fails with the reason written out.
  - **All ten note types cross again, and a road incident names its vehicle (`20261013130000_partner_note_all_types_and_vehicle.sql`; Reidey's half is its PR #213, merged).** Owner decisions of 13.10.2026: `sick`, `vacation` and `off` may cross, after he was told that a named employee's sickness becomes data stored in both products. The old 06.10.2026 reason is marked superseded in `20261007110000` where it was written. The allow-list **stays an allow-list**, now of ten, and planning behaviour stays **derived from the type**, so those three exclude the driver. The five types that derive `must_include_in_plan` **must name the vehicle**: the door takes the partner's plate and keeps it **verbatim** on the submission, and the apply resolves it to `cars.id` through a **SQL twin of the importer's `normalise` and `isValidKennzeichen`** (`src/utils/partnerPlateKey.test.ts` pins the twin to the migration's own self-check samples). Equal keys and **exactly one** car in the company, otherwise `car_id` stays NULL **and the note is still recorded** — with no review step left, nothing may be dropped for want of a vehicle link. The kit probes that asserted the three refusals now assert that they cross and derive `exclude_from_plan`.
  - **A sequencing mistake of mine, plainly:** the El-Professor half was merged while the app half was not, so incident notes broke on staging until ridy #213 landed. The owner caught it. A rule that requires a field the sender does not yet send must ship with its sender. **Check in Reidey's dashboard that his fleet has synced vehicles**: without them an incident note cannot be sent at all, because the plate is required and typing one is forbidden.
  - **Two labels, both careful about what must not move.** `8bb7d42`: the cash page reads **Kassenbuch** in the sidebar and the page header, **in both languages**, like `Belege`, `Werkstatt` and `Abrechnung` beside it (he was looking at the English UI). A third place he could not see was renamed too, `users.page.cashBalance` on the Rollen screen, or an administrator would read a different name for the same page. **Three uses stay `Bargeldbestand` on purpose**, because they name the amount and not the book: the Overview's figure, the permission's description and the refusal sentence. The page **key** `cash-balance` is untouched — it is data, matched against `role_permissions` and `public.role_page_keys()`. `ce3da45`: the choice beside `Bargeld` reads **Online**. **The stored value does not move**: `payment_method` stays `bar` | `uberweisung`, because `abrechnung_weekly` filters `payment_method = 'bar'` to decide what a driver owes the company, `belegeExport` formats on `'uberweisung'` and Reidey's validator admits exactly those two words. Renaming it would have broken the weekly Abrechnung silently: figures still produced, just wrong, and a wrong `bar_zuruck` is money a driver is told they owe. Two dictionary entries per language and nothing else; the commit records that neither reaches a statutory document.
  - **The staging deploy broke, and it was mine (`183909c`, PR #212).** I renumbered the notes migration `20261013120000` → `20261013130000` so no two shared a timestamp, swept `tests/` and `supabase/` and **never grepped `src/`**. `src/utils/partnerPlateKey.test.ts` opens that migration by filename, so it died on `ENOENT` and failed the staging deploy of an unrelated PR (#211). **My own local gate had shown it and I read the wrong line:** a suite that fails to load registers no tests, so `Tests 4306 passed` stayed true while `Test Files` read `1 failed | 176 passed`. I grepped the summary instead of reading `BUILD EXIT`. **The exit code is the verdict; the summary line is a courtesy** — the same shape as piping the RLS kit to `tail`, where the pipeline's status is `tail`'s. **Renaming a migration:** this repo reads migrations from browser-side tests to keep a TypeScript rule and a SQL self-check in step — `partnerPlateKey.test.ts`, `planFeatures.test.ts`, `rolePageKeys.test.ts`, `appConfigSeed.test.ts` — so sweep `src/` too. **And the sweep found three more than the deploy did.** Four comments named `20261013120000` where they meant the notes migration — the SUPERSEDED note in `20261007110000_partner_note_intake.sql`, the header of `partnerPlateKey.test.ts`, `partner-contract.test.ts` and `tests/rls/partner-note-intake.sql`. None of them broke anything, and that is why they are worth naming: `20261013120000` is now a real file about something else entirely, so each one sent a reader to the wrong SQL with no error anywhere. They are repointed here. The two that still read `20261013120000` — in `tests/rls/income-import-uber.sql` and `matrix.sql` — are correct: they mean the Uber import.
  - **Measured.** `npm run build` on the fix: exit 0, 177 of 177 test files, 4323 tests, both ratchets at the baseline. The staging deploy succeeded: release `20261013T165014Z-829124d`, `https://app.elprofessor.eu/version.txt` returns `829124d19d83164271a733165c7f387ef7f0060a`, matching `origin/staging`; the functions container was recreated with it and `POST /functions/v1/uber-earnings-pull` answers 401 without a token. Reidey: PRs #209–#213 are merged into its `main` (`64b130a`), its branches are deleted, and they carried no migrations, so its `migrate --force` had nothing to apply. **The driver APK already exists from `main`:** EAS build `a16c6f71`, FINISHED, profile and channel `preview`, app version 1.0.5, commit `64b130a`, `https://expo.dev/artifacts/eas/bcWLm88orMU6wIdbvSnihzam2bhbCH5JnJhDbDOyNWY.apk` — no new build is needed. Measured why an OTA reaches an installed build for JS work: the Expo fingerprint has 161 sources and none is under `app/` or `src/`.
  - **The RLS kit was run for today's three migrations, on the VPS, and the two new files actually ran** — which is the part worth stating, because a kit file that is never reached passes vacuously. `ssh exit 0`, and the run reported *income import uber: all 41 probes matched*, *partner note intake: all 48 probes matched* (the plate probes 41 and 45–52 among them) and *matrix: 340 lines, 0 differ from the recorded baseline, 6720 probes in 11.2 s* with `matrix.uncache=on`, so the plan-cache guard was active and no cell moved. The output was read from a file, never through a pipe: a pipeline's exit status is the last command's, and `tail` has returned 0 over a failed kit in this project before.
  - **Still open.** (1) Nothing is known to pull the nightly `pg_dump` backups off the VPS: they sit on the same disk as the database. Asked repeatedly, unanswered. (2) `note_text` crosses unchanged, so a sickness note can carry explicit medical detail, and `driver_notes` has **no retention period** — an Art. 9 exposure that this change widened and did not narrow. (3) The Uber pull succeeding against Uber (above).

- **13.10.2026 — the review step is gone, and that is the largest reversal this integration has had.** Owner's decision, in his words: *"فش حالة فش مراجعة خلص بتعتمد دائما فاتورة وملاحظة"* and *"كل سائق مسؤول عن اللي برسلو"*. Every partner receipt and note is accepted the moment it arrives.
  - **This reverses his own decision of 06.10.2026** (*"كل وارد بمراجعة إنسان"*), whose stated reason was that a `bar` receipt moves `bar_zuruck` — what the driver owes the company — and that `Tanken` moves the Tankenquote. He was told that again, in those terms, and chose auto-acceptance. **Do not re-argue it; implement it.** The screens, the RPCs and the app all now assume it.
  - **`20261013100000_partner_auto_accept.sql`** is the slice. `app_private.partner_receipt_apply` / `partner_note_apply` write the `receipts` or `driver_notes` row and mark the submission `accepted` in one transaction, and the record RPCs call them. **An unlinked driver is the one case that still waits**: no `driver_id` means no name, and `receipts.driver_name` is the only link this schema has, so such a submission stays `pending` and is applied by `partner_submission_claim_driver` when a human confirms the link.
  - **The Belegnummer could not be drawn the old way.** `public.receipt_number_next` asks `get_active_company_id()` and the caller's page, and the door runs as the service role with no session, so it would have raised `not_authorized` on every arrival. `app_private.partner_receipt_number` is the same draw — same `BEL-<ms>-<4 digits>` shape, same retry, same unique index behind it — without the caller question. It is not a second counter and there is no sequence to desynchronise.
  - **The author is a column, not a repurposed one.** `receipts.submitted_by_name` and `driver_notes.submitted_by_name` snapshot OUR driver row's name at arrival. `created_by` holds a user id everywhere else and the screen resolves it through `user_profiles`; a driver has no user, and the old accept wrote the *accepting administrator*, which is the defect the owner named — the column said "Y&A Mobility Verwaltung" for a receipt a driver photographed. A separate column also survives an edit, where a name parked in `created_by` is one careless `created_by: user.id` away.
  - **One list.** The `Receipts from the app` tab and the notes inbox are deleted with the review they existed for, along with 96 dictionary keys only they used. The seven fields (merchant, city, printed time, litres, unit price, odometer, range) show on the ordinary Belege table — **they were stored and unreachable before**, because the `Receipt` type and `useDatabase`'s mapper carried none of them.
  - **What was verified rather than taken from the agent's report**, because this change writes into customers' books: the kit re-run independently (`ssh exit 0`, matrix 340 lines / 0 differ / 6720 probes); the numbering not forking a second counter; the staging backfill measured as **one receipt and one note**; and a **mutation control** — making the apply prefer `driver_name_submitted` over our own `drivers` row made the kit abort with *"auto-accept self-check 5: the splice lost: receipt_apply reads a submitted name"*. The probes measure.
  - **Two consequences to keep in view.** Confirming a driver link now writes Belege and notes, so the `drivers` page right quietly got wider; and a submission whose link is *rejected* is stranded in the arrival table with no screen to clear it.
  - **No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump, and that was checked rather than assumed.** The migration drops `NOT NULL` on `driver_notes.created_by`. `NotesPage` already declared the field optional and reads `note.created_by ?? ''`, so the previous release tolerates a null; the worst a rollback does is show an empty author for an app Beleg.

- **13.10.2026 — the driver app follows.** `feat/receipt-steps` on Reidey, merged.
  - **The receipt form is four steps** — photograph, check the details, the refuel figures, send — because the screen showed fifteen empty fields before anything had been read, which is the opposite of what the reading is for. The third step exists only on a `Tanken` receipt. **The rules live in `src/lib/receipt-steps.ts` and both the step gate and `submit()` ask them**, so the last wall and the steps cannot disagree about what a valid receipt is; the parity test is what pins that, and its negative control was run.
  - **The list is grouped month → week, each week folding.** The week belongs to the month containing its **Monday**, the same rule El-Professor's whole monthly aggregation uses — group it any other way and the driver and his company disagree about which month a receipt falls in.
  - **Six sentences had become lies** and were rewritten in three languages: *"your company reviews every receipt before it enters the books"*, on the receipt screen, the note screen, the empty state and both success toasts. A promise the product does not keep is worse than no promise.
  - **Nobody is told about a decision** — the bell row, the push and `SubmissionDecisionNotifier` are gone. Safe to do on the same day because **El-Professor production has no partner integration at all** (measured: zero `partner_*` tables, zero partner migrations), so no release out there can reject a receipt and find nobody listening.

- **12.10.2026 — a refuel carries the odometer and the range left, and both figures are read on the phone.** Branches: `feat/odometer-read` on Reidey, `feat/receipt-fields-door` here. Neither is merged.
  - **What ships.** `20261012100000` adds `odometer_km` and `range_km` to `partner_receipt_submissions` **and** to `receipts`, with CHECK constraints (odometer 100–10 000 000, range 0–2000); both functions are spliced from the catalogue as `20261011100000` did; `partner-receipt-core.ts` reads both; `partner-intake` passes them to the RPC. On the app the driver photographs the cluster, `readOdometer` reads it, the two fields fill, and the image stays on the device — the photo is **mandatory** with a `Tanken` Beleg (owner, 11.10.2026).
  - **Why a rule and not a model, measured on 59 photos of this fleet's clusters.** `gemma3:4b` answered all 59 and **abstained zero times**; 15 answers were impossible by magnitude, and on one it returned `691` — the remaining RANGE — where the total read `25041`. Tesseract plus `readOdometer` picked a value on 35 and said "I could not" on 24, and 34 of the 35 were plausible. Nobody downstream can check the figure, because the photo is gone, so the only safe reader is one that admits when it does not know.
  - **Why the range and not the gauge.** The gauge is a bar of lit blocks. The model answered `three_quarters` on 41 of 59 including one visibly at a quarter; nothing on Hugging Face reads a segmented bar; pixel counting failed (the lit span varied fivefold) and a green-badge anchor found 19 of 59. The range beside it is text, reads reliably, and `199 km left` is actionable where "about half" is not.
  - **The trap worth carrying forward: `integer` alone would have lost the figure in silence.** Reidey's payload reaches us **verbatim** (`ElProfessorController::submission()` merges it as it is) and our `text()` reader answers null for anything that is not a string — so a Laravel `integer` rule, which admits `25382` as well as `"25382"`, would have made the one mandatory field read as *absent*, with a 201 there and a 200 here and nothing to look at afterwards. Both ends are pinned now: `['nullable', 'string', 'integer', …]` on theirs, and on ours these two fields **refuse** a present-but-non-string value by name instead of dropping it. `partner-contract.test.ts` holds both halves.
  - **And one claim I had to measure rather than reason about.** With both `string` and `integer` on one Laravel rule, `min`/`max` could size by number or by string length — and if it sized by length, `min:1000` would refuse every real odometer ever sent. It sizes numerically (`getSize()` checks the numeric rules first), and `test_the_odometer_bounds_are_numeric_and_not_string_lengths` is what says so.
  - **Measured.** `npm run test:rls` on the VPS, **ssh exit 0** — and that qualifier is the point: the first run reported exit 0 from `tail` at the end of the pipe, which proves nothing. "partner receipt accept: all 38 probes matched", nine of them new. `20261011100000` had shipped with no kit file at all; section 7 now covers both rounds, all seven extra fields from the door through the accept into the `receipts` row, and probe 34 asks what the edge function cannot: with the TypeScript bound bypassed — which a direct RPC call is — the column still refuses. Ridy: pint clean, 61/61 phpunit, app typecheck clean, 44/44 app tests. Negative controls run on all three new walls, each failing exactly its own probe.
  - **A trap for anyone running Reidey's PHP suite from a git worktree.** The worktree has no `vendor/`, and linking it to the main checkout's with a junction makes the suite pass or fail against the **wrong tree**: PHP resolves `__DIR__` through the junction, so composer's `$baseDir = dirname($vendorDir)` becomes the main checkout and `App\` autoloads from *its* `app/`. My first run reported the new rules absent — they were present, in the tree nothing was loading. Run `composer install` in the worktree, and give it a throwaway `.env` with an `APP_KEY` (`phpunit.xml` supplies the rest, sqlite in memory). Remove such a junction with `cmd //c rmdir <link>`, never `rm -rf` through it.
  - **And a survey of the whole integration found a field stored nowhere.** `city` has been a column on `partner_receipt_submissions` since `20261005140000` and **nothing ever wrote it**: the door parses it, Reidey sends it, `partner-contract.test.ts` asserts it arrives, and `partner_receipt_accept` copied `v_row.city` into `receipts` in good faith — a column that was always NULL. Nothing failed and nothing logged. `p_city` is appended in `20261012100000` (unreleased, and already rewriting the same two bodies), at the **end** of the parameter list so a positional caller keeps its meaning; `tests/fixtures/seed-partner-demo.sql` is such a caller, with fifteen. Probes 31 and 33 now carry it, and they are the argument for a kit file per round — `20261011100000` shipped without one and this is what it cost. Rows submitted on 11.10.2026 keep a NULL city, which the screen shows as a dash rather than inventing one.
  - **The owner asked for the fields on the review screen, and that was the other half of the same gap** (12.10.2026). All eight were stored, copied into `receipts` and **read by nothing**: `PartnerReceiptInbox` selected none of them, so a reviewer judged a refuel on a category and a total. The dialog now carries the merchant, the city and the clock time as claim rows — always rendered, because a dash is an answer and a row that disappears reads as a screen that does not know the field exists — and the four refuel figures as a block shown only when the Beleg has any, since four dashes on a car wash would send a reviewer looking for a number nobody claimed. The unit price takes **three** places (`1,789` is a real pump price; `1,79` is a different number). Under the block, one line says the figures were read by the phone from a photo that stays there: accepting them is accepting something nothing cross-checked, and presenting them like any other stored value invites the assumption that they were.
  - **What the survey also found, and what is NOT in this branch.** `partner-connect` asks `current_role_permits('drivers', true)` while `partner_connections_update`'s policy asks `is_company_admin()` and `20261005130000` says connecting is an administrator's act — so a non-admin with edit on the drivers page can replace the fleet connection through the edge function. `partner_driver_links` and `partner_connections` still carry client UPDATE policies that `20261008160000` closed for the submission tables. The `catchup` branch of `partner-intake` takes its fleet from the body and never reads the caller's session. `partner-notify` returns `partner_submission_push_target`'s reason before comparing the company. None of these is touched here; they are findings to weigh, not conclusions, and three of them would change who may do what.

  - **Still open.** The new preview APK: the OTA fingerprint diverged on the camera work, so an update cannot reach an installed build and a new APK is required.

- **DEMO has a complete December 2026, imported through the UI on 08.10.2026 after #192 and #193.**
  - **The import.** 297 rides, 294 completed and 3 cancelled, carrying all 18 Uber columns including the order transmission time and the position. `fahrtenbuch_imports.source_columns_json` holds 18 columns and the original XLSX is in `fahrtenbuch-files`. That file is the first storage object written since 10.09.2026. The screen reported 0 duplicates.
  - **The data.** It is synthetic and built by the deterministic `scripts/demo/december-2026.mjs`:
    - Anna Beispiel: 103 rides.
    - Emil Normal: 101 rides.
    - Bert Nachtfahrt: 90 rides, Friday and Saturday nights, including rides after midnight.
  - **What the Rückkehrpflicht engine finds in it** (block gap 30): 215 `during_previous`, 21 `at_base`, 6 `away_from_base` and 52 `block_start_at_base`, plus one each of `same_address`, `same_street`, `implausible_speed` and `zero_distance`.
  - **Why a new month.** November's seeded rows cannot carry original columns: they are immutable, and re-importing them is refused as duplicates, correctly.
  - **The specs.** December is now the newest month. `monthly-plan-walk`, `-split-salary` and `-owner-free-time` select November 2026 explicitly; `monthly-plan-save` keeps October.

- **A second live defect, fixed on `fix/storage-cors`: no browser upload has reached storage on either stack since 12.09.2026.**
  - **Symptom.** With the lookup fixed, the December import failed at its last step: "Import blockiert: Original-XLSX-Datei konnte nicht gespeichert werden … (Failed to fetch)". The console showed `blocked by CORS policy: Request header field cache-control is not allowed by Access-Control-Allow-Headers in preflight response`.
  - **Cause.** storage-js adds `cache-control` and `x-upsert` to every upload, and `x-metadata` when metadata is given. The storage route's cors `headers` list in `deploy/kong.yml` named none of the three. That list was written on 12.09.2026 (d8e6172, then bf3bded, which added `X-Supabase-Api-Version` for auth and not these).
  - **Measured read-only.** `storage.objects` holds **no object created after 10.09.2026** on production or staging, and no object at all in `fahrtenbuch-files` or `salary-receipts`. So for four weeks the following all failed in the browser with no server log:
    - every Fahrtenbuch import;
    - every logo upload;
    - every signed Gehaltsquittung copy.
  - **The fix and its guard.** The three headers are added to the storage route. `src/utils/kongStorageCors.test.ts` reads the installed storage-js build and fails when it sends a header the list does not name. Checked against the old `kong.yml`: it fails, listing all three.
  - **Rendering verified locally.** The file was rendered the way the container renders it, by an eval of an echo in double quotes. The only non-comment difference from staging's file is the three header lines.
  - **A trap recorded in the comment itself.** A double quote in a kong.yml comment unbalances that eval for the rest of the file. The first draft of this comment had one, and it was removed before commit.
  - **Production needs this release before anyone can import.**

- **A live defect, fixed on `fix/import-key-lookup`: every Fahrtenbuch import of more than about 50 rides failed, on staging and on production.** It surfaced while importing a 297-ride December into DEMO. The preview step said "Die Vorschau konnte nicht erstellt werden: [object Object]", and the import button then read "0 Fahrten importieren".

  **Mechanism.** The importer's duplicate-ride lookup (`findStoredSourceRowKeys`, `eb68a7f`) asked for 100 64-character keys per GET. **PostgREST echoes the query in its `Content-Location` response header**, and Kong's nginx refuses a response whose headers pass its 4 KB buffer: `upstream sent too big header while reading response header from upstream`, which shows as a 502. Staging's Kong log held 16 such lines from the attempts.

  **Measured through staging's gateway from inside the host, read-only:**
  - 64-character keys: 50–56 pass with the service key. The browser's query, which carries one filter more, failed at 56.
  - uuids: 95 pass and 100 fail.

  **Two more lookups had the same shape at 200 uuids:**
  - the Bargeldbestand's receipt names, which failed for a month with more than about 95 Gehaltsquittungen;
  - the backup's child tables, which failed for a company with that many parent rows.

  **The fix.** All three now take their bound from `src/utils/postgrestLookup.ts`:
  - 25 keys or 40 uuids per lookup, half the measured limits;
  - the key lookups run at most four at a time;
  - its test fails if the old sizes come back (mutation-checked).

  **Production is exposed and unaffected so far:** it has had no import since 20.09.2026. **This must reach production before the next customer import.** Kong's `proxy_buffer_size` was deliberately not raised, because that is a host change and the client fix is enough. The other `.in(…)` lists in `src/` were read one by one. Each is bounded by a small set (one driver's payments, one month's Stundennachweise, a company's drivers), so they were left alone.

- **The whole e2e suite was run on staging after #188 and #189 merged (`b4796f8`).** 19 files, 35 tests. The first pass was 28 passed and 7 failed, and each failure was diagnosed:
  - Four were the local network: `page.goto: net::ERR_TIMED_OUT` and Node's `fetch failed` against Mailpit. They are abrechnung-dialog-fits 1440, abrechnung-mail-all, forgot-password and the second monthly-plan-save test, and all four passed on a rerun.
  - `return-check` failed because the DEMO company has no analysis settings. The page computes nothing until "Mit Testwert 30 Min. ansehen" is pressed, so the spec now presses it. The test value is held in the page and stored nowhere.
  - `abrechnung-mail` asserted a PNG attachment that the owner removed on 04.10.2026 (8b2e208). It now asserts that there is no attachment, so an image coming back unasked fails it.
  - `salary-payment-cash-view` 2 is a **missing fixture**: it needs a cash salary payment with no stored receipt on the DEMO company. It fails rather than skips by design.
  - `google-signin`'s "provider off" test skips itself, correctly, because Google is on.

  **The two staging data changes, after the owner's yes (08.10.2026):**
  1. **The cash-view fixture is in.** It is `salary_payments` `3176e062-8a2a-43ff-9621-f006dcdc1468`: 1,00 € cash, `source_type` NULL, on Emil Normal's salary `b41dfa7d-…` (02.11.–06.12.2026), with a description starting "E2E-Fixture (08.10.2026)". The salary now reads `partially_paid`. The spec then failed on the **view**, not the fixture. It still asserted the 03.10 view, with its "ZAHLUNGSANSICHT" banner and no PDF button, but the owner removed the banner on 04.10 and the view gained Download, Drucken and the issue-a-receipt section. It now asserts the dialog's own sentence instead ("Es ist noch keine Gehaltsquittung gespeichert"), no GQ number, those buttons only, and no write. Both tests pass.
  2. **The November import is NOT changed, and adding its column names would be wrong.** The planned Fahrtenbuch writes every cell verbatim from `raw_data_json`, and these 30 rows hold only `{seed,row}`. The trips are immutable by trigger. So `source_columns_json` alone would produce a sheet of 30 empty rows with only the planned driver filled in: a document that looks like a Fahrtenbuch and is not. The honest fix is a *new*, complete November import of the same rides with all 11 columns, alongside the seeded one, which the fixture rule says is never deleted. That is a different change from the one approved, so it went back to the owner.

- **Fahrtenanalyse has a fourth tab, "Rückkehrpflicht & Adressen" — branch `feat/return-check`, open against `staging`.** The tab places each completed ride's order: during the previous ride, at the Betriebssitz, away from it after the previous ride ended, at a block start, or unverifiable with a reason. It also lists rides whose own data does not add up. The rules are in CLAUDE.md under the Fahrtenbuch section.

  **Measured on staging before building, read-only, counts only:**
  - 13,959 of 14,011 rides carry `Uhrzeit der Auftragsuebermittlung` and a "lat lon" position, and 13,845 of the positions parse.
  - Within blocks (gap under 2 h), 8,455 follow-up orders came after the previous ride ended and 3,991 during it.
  - The base cannot be read off the data reliably. Only 126 of 1,008 block starts of the largest company fall in one kilometre, so it is the operator's entry, prefilled with a proposal.

  **Verified:**
  - 29 engine tests, with nine mutations each caught.
  - 18 screen-helper tests.
  - Two render tests through the real German dictionary. One fails on "Verstoß"; the other proves the operator's base survives a tab switch, with a negative control.
  - `npm run build` green.

  **Not verified:** the tab has not been opened on a real month in a browser.

  **Review fixes made before commit:**
  - The base was lost on every tab switch, because Radix unmounts the inactive tab. It is now held by the page.
  - Unsequenceable rides vanished. They are now counted as `no_ride_times`.
  - The "Wartezeit" column claimed a wait the file does not show. It is now "Abstand zum Ende der vorherigen Fahrt".
  - "Nichts zu prüfen" showed even when nothing could be checked.
  - A place name in front hid a same-address ride.
  - A bare German comma parsed as two coordinates.

  **Open:**
  - ~~Persisting the base~~: done in the same PR. `20261008200000` adds three nullable columns with constraints for inside Germany, both-or-neither, and a radius up to 50 km. Policies are unchanged, so writes still need an administrator. The kit is green: 3,729 checks, the matrix's 310 lines with 0 differences, and 5 new probes in `trip-analysis-settings.sql`. Each probe there runs in a rolled-back subtransaction, so a probe for "half a pair" must set one coordinate on a row that holds none. The first version set one coordinate to NULL on a row it assumed an earlier probe had filled, and failed.
  - The first ride of each vehicle in an import counts as a block start. The info box says so.

- **The shift recommendation is verified on staging, and the five older e2e specs are fixed.** Fixed means brought up to date, and all six tests pass on staging. A new `e2e/tests/return-check.spec.ts` opens the tab and asserts no write and no "Verstoß".

  **A fixture gap, not a product defect.** The DEMO company's November import (`b1c2d3e4-0000-4000-8000-00000000f001`, `scripts/seed-demo-company.sql`) has `source_columns_json = null`, and its rows' `raw_data_json` is only `{seed,row}`. So §10's planned Fahrtenbuch refuses for November ("Die Spalten der Originaldatei konnten nicht ermittelt werden"). `monthly-plan-save` now selects October explicitly. Repairing the November import is a data change on staging and waits for the owner.

  What was stale before:
  - `e2e/tests/shift-recommendation.spec.ts` walks chooser → three steps → back on the demo company and records every PostgREST request. It passed: 27 shifts and no write. Preflights and the shell's read RPCs are excluded.
  - The four monthly-plan specs opened `/monatsplan?art=standard` correctly but then failed on labels changed before #188: "Mindestdauer je Block" (#186), the eight-step walk (#174, where the first "Plan bestätigen" only opens step 6 and the second saves), and the owner free-time sentence.

- **The monthly plan has a second type, the shift recommendation — merged to `staging` as #188.** `/monatsplan` opens on two cards (`?art=standard|schichten`, `MonthlyPlanEntryPage`); the standard plan is `MonthlyPlanPage` with **zero diff**, and the four e2e specs that opened `/monatsplan` now open `/monatsplan?art=standard`. The new type learns revenue per vehicle-hour per weekday and 15-minute slot from the imported months and recommends, for a future month, when each vehicle should be on the road. The rules worth knowing are in CLAUDE.md under *`/monatsplan` opens on a choice*.

  **What it was asked to be, and is not.** The owner pasted an English brief for an "Optimized Operations Plan" that generated rides: addresses, km, fares, tips, cancellations, GPS in the `Standort des Fahrzeugs bei Auftragsuebermittlung` format, and order times placed before the previous ride ends "for the Rückkehrpflicht", calibrated to the cent to a target revenue, with rules so the result would not "look artificial". That part was declined and the legitimate part built instead. A Mietwagen cannot know next month's rides, so a generated ride list is only usable as the record, and the columns it fills are `fahrtenbuchExport.ts`'s. `boundary.test.ts` reads the module's source and fails on a database name or an import outside its allow-list, and `recommend.test.ts` pins the exact keys of a shift. Do not extend it towards a ride.

  **Verified:**
  - 38 engine tests, mutation-checked. Removing any one of these rules fails a test: the gap rule, the holiday mapping, the vehicle wall, the cross-day break, the rotation, the evidence floor, the duration guard. `runWorth <= 0` survives because it is an equivalent mutant of the tie-break.
  - 24 screen-helper tests.
  - A render test that walks imports → rides → profile → plan → result through the real German dictionary. Its negative control is deleting one key, which fails it.
  - `npm run build` is green.

  **Not verified:** it has never been opened in a browser or run on staging data. The "fewer hours at a higher rate" figure (1.716 h → 837 h, 26,91 → 31,92 €/h) was measured on synthetic rides, not on a real month.

  **A review found three majors, all fixed before commit:**
  - A shift after midnight was shown with the next calendar date beside the operating day's weekday.
  - A ride whose end carried a mistyped year would have walked decades slot by slot. Rides of 0 minutes or over 12 hours are now excluded and counted.
  - The vehicle ceiling could be crossed by a shift bridging a full slot. A full slot is now a wall.

  **Open, deliberately:**
  - A vehicle with no rides in the chosen months is not offered; the screen says why.
  - Only the newest import of a month is read; the screen names the others.
  - `loadTripRows` still selects `*`, because it is the Fahrtenanalyse's loader reused.

  **The working tree is shared with another session.** `C:/Users/ahmad/Desktop/Pro` was switched to `feat/partner-intake` while this work was still uncommitted, so the work was moved to its own worktree, `C:/Users/ahmad/Desktop/Pro-shiftrec`. Its node_modules is a junction to the main checkout's. Two sessions in one checkout is how one session's files end up in the other's commit, so give parallel work its own worktree.

- **A table in `public` with RLS off that grants an API role — the live check, because the kit cannot make it.** `20260926100000` revoked `anon` and `authenticated` on `public.tenants`, `public.extensions` and `public.schema_migrations`, which the `supabase/realtime` image creates on boot and Supabase's DEFAULT PRIVILEGES on `public` hand to every API role. Measured 26.09.2026 on both stacks before the fix: RLS off, no policies, all seven privileges for `anon`, and the public API answered 200 on each — `tenants` returning the 108-character Realtime signing secret, `extensions.settings` returning `db_password`, and INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE also held.

  `tests/rls/assertions.sql` now fails any release where such a table exists, but **that rule is vacuous for image-created tables**: the kit's fixture is a `--schema=public` dump and carries none of them. So run this against each live database after any image bump, and after any release that adds a table to `public`:

  ```bash
  ssh elprofessor 'docker exec -i ya-mobility-db-1 psql -U postgres -d postgres -tAc "
    SELECT c.relname || E'''  anon=''' || string_agg(DISTINCT g.privilege_type, ',')
      FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid=c.relnamespace
      JOIN information_schema.role_table_grants g
        ON g.table_schema='public' AND g.table_name=c.relname
     WHERE n.nspname='public' AND c.relkind='r' AND NOT c.relrowsecurity
       AND g.grantee IN ('anon','authenticated') GROUP BY c.relname;"'
  ```

  Empty output is the pass. The DEFAULT PRIVILEGES themselves were deliberately left alone — they are a global setting on a schema the images own, and changing them could break a future image's boot.

- **The Reidey integration has a milestone sheet, and it is the thing to follow: [reidey-integration-plan.html](reidey-integration-plan.html).** Seven milestones, each one "a thing that works"; the fixed owner decisions with their dates; the five constraints that shape everything; and all 46 audit findings written inside the milestone that answers them, by number, so none is lost. Do not keep a second copy of it anywhere.

  **Two of those findings are blockers and one is in code committed the same day.** `GET elprofessor/fleet/drivers` returns drivers and `meta` and no fleet identity, and the endpoint that does return `tenant_id` is outside the confined list — so pasting fleet A's token into company B's Connect succeeds and company B begins ingesting fleet A's drivers. Measured, and not hypothetical for this customer: one address in the partner migration's own measurement owns two companies. The second: **no milestone transported the Beleg photo** — `document_path` appears exactly once in the whole repository, its own column definition — and the verification gate could not see it, because the only subject constraint accepts a row with a category or a description and no photo at all.

  **The ordering changed because of the audit, not around it.** M0 is the legal paper with no code in it, because personal data moves at M1 and there is no Reidey staging, so the first roster pull is real drivers. M3 is **notes with no image at all** — it proves the transport, the idempotency, the claim-on-link-confirm, the review shape and the status round trip without a line of storage work, which is a better first proof than a receipt row with a null photo. The whole image chain is isolated in M4.

- **Two defects in the partner tables I wrote, neither found by reading them — `20261007100000`, on `feat/partner-intake`.** One came out of a kit file written to settle unverified readings, the other out of the five-lens audit. Recording that is the point: a migration whose author reviewed it twice still shipped a sibling pair that disagree.

  **`partner_note_submissions.driver_note_id` had no foreign key** while its sibling `partner_receipt_submissions.receipt_id` had one, with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, and both tables carry the same shape of guard — `CHECK ((status = 'accepted') = (<the reference> IS NOT NULL))`. On the receipt side the chain works: delete the Beleg, the FK nulls the column, the CHECK sees `accepted` beside NULL and refuses. On the note side nothing fires, so the driver deletes, `driver_notes` cascades away, and the submission is left `accepted` pointing at a row that no longer exists. **The CHECK cannot catch it, because a dangling uuid is still non-null.** I expected the refusal and asserted it; the kit refused the expectation. Probe 14 in `tests/rls/driver-deletion.sql` flipped in the same commit. Pre-existing dangling rows are **not deleted** — the reference is cleared and the row marked `rejected` with reason `note_deleted_before_fk (20261007100000)`, because dropping a data row is not this migration's call.

  **Two El-Professor companies could claim the same Reidey fleet.** `partner_connections` had one live row per `(company_id, partner)` and nothing the other way, so the second company silently became the live connection for a fleet the first was also reading. The new partial unique index is on `(partner, external_company_id)` where the status is not revoked — so a revoked connection frees the fleet. `external_company_id` stays nullable and NULLs never collide, which is the one soft edge: a connection recorded before the partner answered with an identity is not covered. It is enforceable at all only because of the Reidey-side fix below; an index on a column nothing fills is decoration.

- **Two owner decisions, 09.10.2026, and one open consequence.**

  **The Beleg photo is kept for ever.** No retention rule, no deletion after a decision, on either side — M4.5 is answered and closed. The disk supports it and that was measured rather than assumed: **788 GB free of 1007** (the plan's "21 GB" was a stale number), against roughly 3 GB a year for a thousand receipts a month.

  **What "for ever" does not yet mean, and it is the one thing left open.** The daily backup (`/etc/cron.daily/ya-mobility-backup`, 06:25, 44 kept) is `pg_dump` over five schemas **including `storage` — which is the rows, not the files**. The bytes live in the `ya-mobility_storage-data` volume and **nothing copies them**. So after a volume loss `storage.objects` would be restored listing every photo, each one answering 404, with `receipts.document_path` pointing at them. Adding a `tar` of the volume beside the dump is cheap and is a **host change, so it waits for the owner's word**.

  **M0 is cancelled** — no legal text, no lawyer. The milestone is no longer a gate and nothing waits on it. Recorded here because the decision was taken after what it covers was laid out: driver names, addresses, phone numbers and receipt photos carrying a place and a date cross between two products with separate databases, and the photos now stay for ever. `Docs/integration/reidey-m0-data-transfer.md` keeps the field-by-field map as reference, with no gate on it.

- **M1–M4 are merged and live on staging** (PR #194, merge commit `b90fddf`, which is the sha `app.elprofessor.eu/version.txt` serves). All eight partner migrations applied, `20261008100000` through `20261008170000`.

  **Two submissions are seeded for *DEMO Monatsplanung GmbH* so the review tab can be tried before Reidey exists**, and they were written **through the real `*_submission_record` doors** rather than by INSERT — a seed that bypasses the door proves the screen and nothing else. `demo-beleg-0001` (41,47 € Tanken, bar, with a readable photo in the `documents` bucket; its signed URL fetched 200 / 46362 bytes / image/jpeg) and `demo-notiz-0001` (`breakdown`), both on the driver `Anna Beispiel`, linked as `demo-41`. Accepting the Beleg writes a `receipts` row with a server-drawn number and moves `bar_zuruck`, which is the thing worth watching. `tests/fixtures/seed-partner-demo.sql` is the script, re-runnable: both doors are idempotent on the external id.

  **The "Eingänge abrufen" button does not appear for DEMO, by design:** it has no `partner_connections` row, so there is nothing to fetch from, and a button that cannot work is worse than none.

  **What is not testable and why.** A real driver submitting needs two things nobody here can do: Reidey's branch deployed to its own server (the owner's act — its `main` is a zero-touch production deploy and is untouched at `d4c0445`), and the camera screen, which is a native module, a store review, and iOS waiting on Apple's DSA check. Everything between those two — the ring, the fetch, the review, the decision and the notification back — is built and tested on both sides.

- **The matrix covers everything for the first time: `UNCOVERED: 0`.** Nine objects had no probe — none of them from the partner work, all of them older, and owing them was recorded twice in memory. `abrechnung_schedules` and `abrechnung_deliveries` now carry ten cells (`company_settings`' shape and `notification_deliveries`' shape respectively), the five Abrechnung functions and the two cash writers carry rpc lines, and both tables are in the generic tenant-table staff rule rather than having hand-written staff cells.

  **Measurement corrected a guess twice, and one of the two is a property of the product.** `record_cash_movement` is **not** an administrator's alone: it asks `current_role_permits('cash-balance', true)`, so an editor and a supervisor book the till and only the viewer is refused — and a platform session is refused in *either* context mode with its own sentence, which is 3.13a's Gehaltsquittung rule applied to the till.

  **The other was a fixture gap presenting as a defect, and the fixture was taught rather than the defect recorded.** `abrechnung_schedule_preview` raised `42703 column u.deleted_at does not exist` for exactly the principal that uses it. Measured read-only on production: the real `auth.users` has `deleted_at`, `banned_until` and `email_confirmed_at`; the kit's does not, because the dump is `--schema=public` and what stands in for GoTrue's table carries only what the foreign keys needed. The three columns are added in `tests/rls/seed.sql` with `IF NOT EXISTS`, so **every probe of a function that filters on an account's state now measures the product instead of the fixture**. Worth knowing for the next probe that looks like a defect.

- **A gap I opened the same day, closed: the Notizen tab had no catch-up.** The button went onto the Belege tab alone, while the catch-up itself ingests notes and Belege together — so the note reviewer was the one left without the recovery for a lost ring, which is worse than neither screen having it: the person who needs it is on the screen where it is missing. It is one hook now (`src/hooks/usePartnerCatchUp.ts`), holding the call, the four outcomes and the reason map once, and the button is on both tabs.

- **The intake direction turned around, and it was a measurement that did it.** `partner-intake`'s door authenticates with the token Reidey issued, and **Sanctum stores only a hash — so Reidey cannot replay it.** Pushing a payload therefore needed a credential nobody holds, for notes as well as Belege: M3's transport was blocked by the same thing and nobody had noticed. The owner chose, on 08.10.2026, of three options, the one that needs no new secret anywhere: **the partner rings and we fetch.**

  Ours: `20261008170000` (`partner_doorbell_resolve`, the events table in `app_private`, 5 self-checks), `_shared/partner-doorbell-core.ts` (39 tests), two branches on `partner-intake` (`doorbell`, `catchup`), `tests/rls/partner-doorbell.sql` (22 probes), and the "Eingänge abrufen" button on the review tab (7 keys). Theirs, on `feat/elprofessor-driver-documents`: the submissions table, the two read endpoints, the driver's door, the ring job and two written-out `CONFINED_ABILITIES` lines (39 tests, 235 assertions). ridy's `main` is untouched at `d4c0445`.

  **A ring carries no credential, so the counters are the whole boundary.** It cannot lie — nothing in it is stored — but it can make us call the partner, and Kong has no rate limiting here. Three counters in the database, and **the third is the one that protects the partner rather than us**: 20 per fleet per minute, 120 per fleet per hour, and **240 per minute across all fleets**, because without that an attacker inventing fleet ids has an unbounded number of per-fleet budgets and a per-fleet limit reads as a limit while bounding nothing. The events table **purges itself on every ring** rather than waiting for a host cron line, which is a deliberate trade: a cron line is the owner's act, and a DELETE of rows past the longest window is cheap and needs nobody.

  **The catch-up is the only recovery, because there is no scheduler.** A ring that never arrives — their queue died, we were down, the gateway dropped it — would be a receipt that silently never came. So a person presses a button that asks the partner what is waiting and ingests up to 25; it is safe to press twice, every submission being idempotent on the partner's own id. Four outcomes are reported distinctly, because "fetched 0" beside "0 waiting" is the only one that is good news.

  **The payload door is kept and is unreachable, which is not the same as unused.** With no `X-Partner-Key` it answers 403 to everyone, Reidey included; it is the generic shape a future partner that *can* hold a key would use. That is materially different from the `partner_*_submissions` UPDATE policy closed earlier the same day: that one was reachable by a company user and let them forge state.

  **Their side needs no secret and no server change.** With no `intake_url`/`anon_key` configured, `RingElProfessor` sends nothing and the submission still arrives through our button — so the feature works before anyone touches ridy's host, and the ring only makes arrival immediate. Filling those two env vars on their server is the owner's act, like every host change.

- **M4 is built: the photo travels, and nothing is accepted without it.** `20261008140000` (`receipts.document_path`, the partner's door, accept and reject, 9 self-checks), `20261008150000` (the sender reaches a Beleg too), `20261008160000` (no client edits a submission), `_shared/partner-receipt-core.ts` (46 tests), `tests/rls/partner-receipt-accept.sql` (29 probes), `partner-status-push.sql` grown from 19 to 32, and the review tab on BelegePage with a photo viewer (42 i18n keys). Build gate green (180 typecheck at baseline, styles at baseline, 4020 tests), kit green, `deno check` clean.

  **The photo is the screen, not a detail of it.** The amount, the category and the date are what the driver *claimed*; the photo is the only evidence. Accepting writes a row that moves money — a `bar` Beleg changes `bar_zuruck`, which is what a driver owes the company, and `Tanken` changes the Tankenquote. So the door refuses a submission with no photo (`document_missing`) before storing anything, the accept refuses one too (for rows written before this release), and the screen **withholds the accept button** while the photo is not on screen, so a reviewer reads why instead of pressing and getting a toast. A broken `<img>` renders as nothing and nothing reads as "there is no receipt", which would have a reviewer reject a Beleg for storage's fault — both the signing call and the image's own `onError` are named.

  **The key is a constraint, not a property of one function.** The edge function derives `<company>/partner/<submission>.<ext>` and takes no path from the request (`send-document`'s rule), but the column is written through a SECURITY DEFINER door, so a later writer — a backfill, a second partner, a repair script — would lose that silently. `partner_receipt_submissions_document_prefix` and `receipts_document_prefix` make the only representable key one inside the submission's own company, which is the folder the bucket's policies key on. The file's type is read from its first bytes and a declared type that disagrees is refused, because the key carries the extension and a browser renders what the extension promises.

  **A deliberate deviation from the plan's M4.2, recorded so it can be overruled:** it asks for a bucket of its own with a size cap, a MIME list and a role gate, like `salary-receipts`. This uses `documents` — its fifteen policies are the ones captured from production and `20260920100000`'s self-check fails a release where a new policy names one of the four buckets. The cost is that `documents` has **no role gate on read**, so any member of the company can read a Beleg photo. That is consistent with the `receipts` row itself being readable by any member (SELECT is never role-gated in this product), but it is a decision, not an oversight.

  **Not covered by M4, by name:** a `request_body max_size` on Caddy's `api.*` blocks (a host change, so it waits for the owner's word); device-side downscaling and EXIF stripping (M5, the camera screen); and how long the photo is kept after a decision (M4.5, the owner's call).

- **The sender was note-only, and a rejected Beleg reached nobody.** `20261008130000` named `partner_note_submissions` in both push functions, so a reviewer who rejected a Beleg made a decision the driver never heard — they keep a photo of a receipt nobody will pay — and the screen could not even report the silence, because the columns that make a lost delivery visible lived on one of the two tables. `20261008150000` teaches both functions the second table, in lookup order notes-then-receipts, and returns the `kind`.

  **Naming the kind is not a nicety.** Reidey's validator takes `kind` in `note,receipt` and **defaults an absent one to `note`**, so a Beleg that travelled without it would be announced in the driver's app as a note. The same answer also decides which page the caller must hold: `partner-notify` asked `current_role_permits('notes', true)` whatever the subject was, so a role holding Notizen and not Belege could announce a Beleg rejection. The gate now follows the kind, and sits after the company comparison so a submission of another company is `submission_not_found` to everyone.

- **The submission tables' UPDATE policy admitted no caller, and it is gone** (`20261008160000`). `20261005140000` wrote it with the comment "Reviewing is the only client write"; that stopped being true inside the same slice, because every judging path was built as a SECURITY DEFINER RPC. Measured: nothing under `src/` and nothing in `supabase/functions/` writes either table through PostgREST.

  **What it allowed, stated precisely after one wrong draft:** rewriting `rejected_reason` after the driver was told; pointing `receipt_id` at any existing Beleg of the company; marking a submission rejected outside the RPC so `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` hold whatever the client sent; and, once `20261008150000` added the column, **stamping `status_pushed_at` by hand**, which makes an undelivered rejection read as delivered and ends the search for it. What it did **not** allow — the first draft of the header claimed it did — is marking a submission accepted with no Beleg: `(status = 'accepted') = (receipt_id IS NOT NULL)` refuses that whoever writes the row. The constraint caught the claim, in the kit, on a fixture that would not insert.

  Eight matrix cells moved with it: `U` and `M` on both tables now read `.` for every principal, which is `fahrtenbuch_trips`' own shape — with no UPDATE policy the statement matches no row and reports success. The view context still reads `T` on `U`, because `refuse_read_only_context_write` is a BEFORE STATEMENT trigger and fires before a policy is consulted.

- **M3's sender is built: a rejection reaches the driver, and a failure stays visible.** `20261008130000` (two columns, two functions, 6 self-checks), `tests/rls/partner-status-push.sql` (19 probes, all matched), `partner-notify/index.ts`, and the screen: the reject sends it, an undelivered rejection is badged, and a resend button appears on exactly those rows (16 i18n keys). Build gate green, kit green, `deno check` clean. **This was blocked until the seal existed** — pushing needs the partner's token replayed and `partner_credentials` held only an HMAC.

  **Two columns on the submission, not a delivery table.** `notification_deliveries` earns a table because one subject owes many notifications over time; a submission owes exactly one push for the one decision a human made about it, so the delivery state is an attribute of that row. A table here would need a foreign key, a unique index, its own policies and both context triggers to say what two columns say.

  **A failure is made visible, never retried.** There is no scheduler in this stack, so `status_pushed_at IS NULL` on a rejected submission *is* "the driver has not been told" — a state the screen shows with a tooltip saying exactly why there is no catch-up. Probe 12 pins it: a failed delivery keeps the reason and leaves the timestamp NULL. If it stamped the time anyway, an undelivered rejection would read as delivered and nobody would look again. Probe 17 pins the mirror: a *later* failure does not un-deliver what was delivered, which is the operator pressing twice while the network drops.

  **What travels and what deliberately does not:** a reason code, the reviewer's own words, the partner's ids. Not the note's text, not who reviewed it, not `driver_note_id`, not `raw_payload` — a driver is told why their submission was refused, not sent their own note back with the company's internal record attached. Probe 9 and self-check 4 both guard it.

  **A guard found while writing it:** the target function runs as the service role, so it would describe any submission to anyone who knows its id. The function therefore compares the submission's company with the **caller's** rather than taking it from the row. Without that line a reviewer in company A could push a rejection to a driver of company B given a submission id.

  **Three small decisions that matter.** A `404` from the partner means "I have no such driver" — the roster moved under us, final for this submission, so it gets its own code instead of counting as an outage. "The driver was already told" is a success (`duplicate: true`), because counting it as a failure would have the operator pressing for ever. And the reject sends the push itself, with the toast saying whether the driver heard: a rejection the driver never hears is not a finished rejection.

- **M1.5 is built: the roster pull, which was the knot.** `20261008120000` (`partner_roster_apply`, `partner_pull_record`, 6 self-checks), `tests/rls/partner-roster-pull.sql` (26 probes, all matched), `partner-pull/index.ts`, and the button plus the departed badge on the proposals tab with 14 i18n keys. Build gate green, kit green, `deno check` clean.

  **Why it was the knot:** M2 and M3 were built and *unusable*. Nothing read the partner's roster, so `partner_driver_links` had no rows, the proposals tab was empty, and a note that arrived could never be accepted because no link existed to confirm.

  **The owner's badge decision is guarded by code, not by a comment.** `partner_removed_at` records what the partner last reported and changes no state; self-check 4 reads the body from the catalogue and fails a release where an UPDATE in the pull assigns `status` or `driver_id`. Probe 11 is the behaviour — a CONFIRMED driver reported as departed keeps both status and driver — and **probe 14 is the control**: a driver who returns has the badge cleared, so the column records the occurrence and not the run. Without 14, an implementation that can only ever set the badge passes 12.

  **Three bounds that are invisible unless named.** `MAX_PAGES` (20 pages of 100): a partner reporting a wrong total would otherwise keep the worker alive until the runtime killed it, and a bound nobody can see is how a loop becomes an outage. A roster longer than one walk is reported as `truncated` rather than left as a short list an operator reads as complete — the "an empty list is a claim" rule applied to an *incomplete* one. And every exit stamps the connection, so a failed pull is visible as a failure rather than as silence.

  **`seal_unreadable` has its own code** because the partner is innocent: the key was rotated or the row was written under another one, and the repair is to connect again. Reporting it as "partner unavailable" would send an operator to check Reidey.

  **Stated plainly: this is built and has never been run against the real Reidey.** The tests cover the database, the endpoint guard and the seal, and `deno check` covers the types, but **no call has ever been made to `reidey.de`**. A first real pull needs a real token from a real company, which is M0's gate and the owner's.

- **M1 is closed on our side: Connect verifies, the token is sealed, and both hosts carry the key.** `20261008110000` (the column and the reader, 7 self-checks), `_shared/partner-token-seal.ts` (26 cases), `_shared/partner-endpoint.ts` (30 cases), `partner-connect`, `PartnerConnectCard` on the settings page with 26 i18n keys. The owner wrote `PARTNER_SEAL_KEY` and `PARTNER_REIDEY_BASE_URL` on staging and production with different keys (`9ab163…` / `eb0645…`), `https://reidey.de`, and a timestamped `.env` backup on each host. No key value was ever printed — only a length and a hash prefix.

  **Why the token is stored at all, measured:** Reidey returns the plaintext ONCE and `issueToken` deletes the previous tokens before minting, so a connection that does not keep it cannot be used again without re-minting — which revokes the link the operator just made. This is the first replayable secret in the database; everything else there is one-way and every replayable secret lives in the host `.env`. The habit is broken for one reason: one token per company, so `.env` can hold the key and not the tokens.

  **Sealed in Deno, never in SQL**, because SQL would put the key and the plaintext into query text, where they reach logs, `pg_stat_statements` and error reports. Self-check 7 fails any function in `public` or `app_private` that starts encrypting.

  **This is the project's first outbound HTTP call**, which makes the destination a security decision: these functions share a Docker network with Reidey's database and ours, so a base URL from a request body is an SSRF into our own network. It comes from the environment, validated before use (https, public host, no credentials, 443, no path) — `http://ridy-backend-1` is a plausible thing to write and exactly what the guard refuses. Three of its tests are controls, not cases: a public IPv4 must be ACCEPTED or "refuses 10.0.3.7" would pass for an implementation refusing every numeric host, and `172.32`/`172.15` must be accepted while `172.31.255.255` is refused, which `a === 172` fails.

  **The verification IS the connection:** `GET /elprofessor/fleet` proves the token, returns the fleet identity the connection is pinned to, and flips Reidey's own card to "connected" because a confined read records `first_used_at` there. Nothing is written before the fleet is known, or it would store the NULL that `partner_connections_live_per_external` deliberately ignores.

  **Three things measurement caught.** `current_role_permits`' argument is `p_need_edit`, not the `p_require_edit` I guessed — a runtime `42883` no test here can see, because it is an RPC across the network. `deno check` rejected `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` as a `BufferSource` while all 26 seal tests passed: a `_shared` module is tested under Node and runs under Deno and must pass both. And `common.retry` does not exist — every screen has its own key.

  **What is NOT built, stated plainly: there is no actual pull.** Connect calls `fleet` to verify and that is the whole of the outbound surface. The roster pull — the thing that produces link proposals — is M1.5: a button, a function that opens the seal, calls `fleet/drivers` and passes each driver through `partner_driver_resolve`. So today a company connects, the identity is pinned and the token is sealed, **and the proposals tab stays empty.**

- **Two owner decisions, 08.10.2026.**

  **The privacy text is dropped as a precondition.** The owner's answer when asked for it was that there is none and the integration is not to wait on one. So M0's gate is now the legal *roles* and the Art. 28 question, and `legal.privacy` stays `{}` — on production as well, where it has been empty since Google sign-in went live on 04.10.2026 ahead of the same page. **Said once and not repeated:** the first roster pull moves real German drivers' names between two products, and a published privacy text is the ordinary legal basis for telling those drivers it happens. That is the owner's call and it is recorded here rather than argued again; what follows from it is that `Docs/integration/reidey-m0-data-transfer.md`'s German drafts stay as drafts and nothing in the product renders them.

  **A departed partner driver is BADGED, not unlinked.** Reidey carries `roster_removed_at` on a driver. When a pull sees it, the link keeps its `linked` status and the proposals tab marks it; the operator decides. Automatic unlinking was the alternative and was refused, which is the right way round: unlinking silently detaches a driver from the submissions and notes already attributed to them, and "this person left the other product" is not the same fact as "this person is not our driver". The code is blocked behind the pull, so what exists today is the decision and the shape it implies: a nullable column on `partner_driver_links` recording what the partner last reported, a badge, and no state change.

- **M2 is complete on our side: a person decides which driver, and the claim rides with the decision.** `20261008100000` (two decisions), `tests/rls/partner-link-review.sql` (25 probes, all matched), the matrix lines for both functions (0 differ first time), `PartnerLinkProposals` as a tab on the Fahrer screen with 37 i18n keys.

  **The confirm claims in its own transaction** and self-check 4 fails a release that splits it. The claim is driven by a person pressing a button, so a lost second call would strand the submissions that arrived before the link — badged "driver not linked" in the inbox, beside a link that says it is confirmed — and nothing retries. Probes 9 and 10 are that property; probe 11 pins that an already-decided submission is left alone, because its decision is a record.

  **A rejected link does not reject the submissions waiting on it**, and the reason is a product one rather than a database one: `rejected_reason` is shown to the driver in their app, so an automatic one is a sentence no person wrote, sent to a person. The reject counts them and returns `stranded`; the screen names the number and the dialog states the consequence *before* the decision. The cost is that those rows stay `pending` for ever and can never be accepted — visible, and cleared by a reviewer in their own words. If the pile ever matters, the answer is a bulk reject with a reason an operator types.

  **Nothing compares a name**, read from the catalogue by self-check 5 and probe 25 rather than trusted. Probe 18 is M2 and M3 meeting: the note that was unacceptable while its driver was unlinked is accepted once a person links them.

  **The waiting count on the screen is a bound and says so.** Two limited queries counted in the browser, and past the bound the column shows `>n` rather than a figure it cannot stand behind. The exact numbers come back from the decisions themselves (`claimed`, `stranded`) and those are what the toast reports — the same discipline as "a figure whose load failed is not shown".

  **A finding that is not this slice's to fix.** `FahrerPage` calls `canEdit(driver.createdBy)` with **no page key**, and AuthContext's `WriteRight` ignores its first argument entirely (`_createdByOrRecord`) and answers `isAdmin || isSuperAdmin` without a page. So editing and deleting a driver on that screen is effectively admin-only, and an editor holding `can_edit` on the Fahrer page cannot. Whether that is intended is the owner's call; the new tab passes `canEdit(undefined, 'drivers')`, which is what both RPCs ask.

  **Still open in M2, and it needs a decision:** the departed driver. Reidey carries `roster_removed_at` and we read nothing, so a confirmed link stays confirmed for a driver who has left their fleet. Unlink automatically when a pull sees it, or badge it and let the operator decide? Blocked in practice behind the token seal, like every pull.

- **M3 is built on our side and the sender is blocked on M1.2 — measured, not preferred.** `20261007110000` (three functions), `tests/rls/partner-note-intake.sql` (37 probes, all matched), `supabase/functions/partner-intake/` with a pure core at 32 vitest cases, `PartnerNoteInbox` on the Notizen screen with 35 i18n keys, and on Reidey's local branch the receiving endpoint plus the driver notification (20 tests, 145 assertions). No image anywhere, deliberately: the chain is proved before any storage work, which stays isolated in M4.

  **`app_private.partner_credentials` holds `key_hash` and nothing else**, so there is no plaintext to replay and **we cannot authenticate a call to Reidey at all**. The push of a rejection therefore waits on M1.2 sealing the token, exactly as the milestone sheet already said. Do not design around it with a browser-side fetch: the token would have to reach the bundle.
