  **Four rules from this slice that a later change would quietly break.** The seven transferable note types are an **allow-list**, not a deny-list of the three Art. 9 ones — a deny-list admits `krank`, a typo, and every type a later release adds, and probe 4 is that case. A final refusal answers **200 with a reason**, never 4xx, because a partner's queue retries a 4xx and "this type may not be transferred" is final; a hint this build does not know is a **500**, never a silent 200. A retry returns the **decision** a human already made (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, self-check 7, probe 20). And the accept **derives** the planning behaviour from the type (probe 24): taking the partner's word for it is how `sick` carrying `neutral` reaches the planner and fails to exclude the driver.

  **The rejection's reason code is a kind of event, not a taxonomy** — there is one way a note gets rejected, a person decided — so it is always `review_rejected` and the reviewer's words ride in `params.reason_text`. That shape was chosen before reading Reidey's code and their `AppNotification` turns out to require it: its own header says it stores "a stable semantic type + structured params, NOT pre-rendered text, so the frontend renders the title/body/icon in each user's own language".

  **`useCompanyScope().canEdit` is a `WriteRight`, not a boolean** — `(record?, page?) => boolean`. Written uncalled it is always truthy, so a permission gate reads as open. TS2774 caught it here; note the failure shape without the gate, because it is the quiet kind: RLS still refuses the write, so the button **appears and then errors** instead of not appearing.

  **Two matrix probes of mine measured nothing, for the same reason, on the same day.** A probe whose refusals share a SQLSTATE cannot separate the branches that produce them: `partner_submission_claim_driver` on a non-existent link, and `partner_note_accept` on a submission whose `driver_id` is NULL. Both now point at a fixture that an authorised caller can actually succeed on. The rule is in `matrix.sql` beside them.

- **The matrix now covers the partner tables, and nine objects are still uncovered — seven of them by a recorded decision, two not.** `tests/rls/matrix.sql` gained the four `partner_*` tables (fixtures, move specs, 20 expected lines, 20 staff overrides) and six RPC lines; UNCOVERED is 19 -> 9, 330 lines, 0 differ, 6520 probes in 15.8 s. The abrechnung slice **said** it was leaving its seven (see its own paragraph below: *"their lines are not added because `tests/rls/` was outside this slice"*), so those are owed and recorded. **`issue_salary_cash_receipt` and `record_cash_movement` are owed and were not recorded anywhere** — they are now.

  **Three things the run established that reading would not have.** All four partner tables carry a SELECT and an UPDATE policy and **nothing else**, so every I cell is R for every principal and every D cell is a DELETE that matches nothing and succeeds; a table whose grants come from the image's default privileges reads differently in the kit from one whose migration states them, which is why `notification_deliveries` is `PP` where these are not. **`current_role_permits()` has a platform branch that grants edit inside a support session, and it reaches two functions written long before it** — a support session draws a Belegnummer and passes the OCR gate, a view context is refused by the same branch. And one probe of mine **measured nothing**: `partner_submission_claim_driver` on a link that does not exist is refused by the gate (42501) and by the lookup (P0002) alike, both raise, so all nine principals read `T`. Recording that would have passed and left a line testing nothing, so it is rebuilt on a confirmed link. The rule is beside it: *a probe whose refusals share a class cannot separate the branches that produce them.*

  **And a method note that cost two runs:** `git archive HEAD` ships the **committed** tree. The first run of these lines measured the previous file byte for byte and exited 0. An uncommitted kit file goes over as `tar -cf - scripts/rls-test.mjs tests supabase/migrations | ssh …`.

- **The Reidey test suite had never been executed, and running it changed two things.** `phpunit.xml` pins `sqlite :memory:`, so it needs no database setup at all: `php vendor/bin/phpunit --testsuite=Feature --filter=ElProfessor` from `backend/`. **The branch is `feat/elprofessor-driver-documents`, local only — nothing is pushed to Reidey, whose `main` is a zero-touch production deploy.**

  **13 tests, 12 passed, 1 failed, and the failure was the test's.** `documents_enabled` on `/driver/me` reads `$driver->tenant`, and `Sanctum::actingAs` pins the in-memory instance onto the guard — so a relation the driver's first call lazily loaded was still cached on it, and the test asked the tenant as it was before the pull. Measured, not read: the database said connected, the reused instance said not, a freshly resolved driver said connected. A real request resolves the tokenable per request, so production was never affected. `actingAsDriver()` now resolves from the database, which also makes the revocation test read post-revoke state rather than possibly-stale state.

  **Then the mutation control, and it is the part worth copying.** A passing test says nothing about what it measures. `connected` is `revoked_at IS NULL && first_used_at IS NOT NULL && a token row exists`, and the suite pinned **one** of the three: dropping `revoked_at` or the token term broke nothing. `revokeToken()` deletes the token rows **and** records the time, so those two shadow each other on the happy path. Each now has a test for the state that path never produces — a token row vanishing outside revocation (Sanctum pruning, or the minting user deleted and its tokens cascaded), which is reachable and would otherwise let the driver app offer the documents section behind a token that pulls nothing; and a recorded revocation beside a live token row, which is **constructed and said to be constructed**, pinned because the term is what makes the recorded decision authoritative rather than inferred from a row's presence. 15 tests, 107 assertions, all three terms caught one test each. `Tenant.php` is untouched — the mutations were applied and reverted by a script in the scratchpad.

- **`CONTRIBUTING.md` was telling newcomers to do the one thing this project forbids.** It said there is "no test suite" (there are ~3853 vitest tests, the RLS kit, the e2e suite and ridy's phpunit), said "nothing is committed directly to `main`", and then instructed `git switch main` + `git merge --no-ff`. It is rewritten. CLAUDE.md's "no commits to ridy's repository" was stale too — the owner opened it; the server is what stays off limits. The fifteen operating rules that lived only in one laptop's memory are now `Docs/operating-rules.md`, **with no credential, address or user id in them**.

- **A receipt can attest a retention, not only a handover — `20261006100000`, merged to `staging` as `61c82d7` (PR #187).** The owner asked why the weekly "Bar zurück" row has no receipt button. It was absent by design and the design was right: the sheet says the amount was received *from the employer*, and on that row the driver collected fares in cash and **kept** part of it as wages. There is no till movement, and not because the money left the till but because it never entered it.

  So the document changed, not the rule. `salary_cash_receipts.attests` is `cash_handover` or `cash_retained`; the retention sheet says *behalten* and that no payout from the till took place; the `GQ-YYYY-NNNN` series, counter, business-year rule and `books_cash = false` are unchanged.

  **Why it was worth doing rather than leaving:** an ordinary cash payment has a ledger row corroborating it. A retention has none — no movement, no receipt, only a `salary_payments` row asserting "paid" — so the signature is the only evidence obtainable that those wages were received.

  **Four things not to undo**, each with a reason that is not style: `attests` is **stored** and never derived when printing, so a later change to a payment's `source_type` cannot reword a signed sheet; the issuability check is an **allow-list** (`manual`, `weekly_cash_difference`), because `salary_payments.source_type` is `text DEFAULT 'manual'` with no CHECK and a deny-list would have printed an unknown value as a handover; the sheet **names no week**, because `week_start`/`week_end` on such a row hold the *salary's* period, not a calendar week; and the handover wording is **byte-identical**, being on sheets already signed.

  **A moved kit cell, named as the kit requires.** `tests/rls/salary-issued-receipt.sql` recorded the refusal and failed on exactly that line; section 4 is the new expectation, in the same commit. Its first version tried to insert a forged `gehaltsquittung` payment as a fixture and `20260914140000`'s guard refused it — the stronger fact, so it is asserted instead of worked around.

  **The cost, stated:** the weekly settlement writes one row per week, so a month can yield up to five numbered receipts for one driver, and the GQ series grows faster. One receipt covering a month's retentions needs a receipt over several payments; the live guard is one per payment row, so that is a separate slice.

- **A number an operator types is not a promise the engine made — PR #186, merged to `staging` as `4daa3fb`.** The owner reported that the monthly plan still produces blocks shorter and longer than the short and long limits he entered. Measured: the engine is right and the screen was lying. `buildBlocks` merges a short block only with a neighbour **of the same vehicle on the same day** and only as far as the long limit allows (`fits()`), and it splits a long block only when `splitLongBlocks` is on, which is off by default. `blocks.test.ts:608-624` pins `[20, 80]` under a minimum of 90 as the **correct** answer, and `blocks.ts:180-185` states the design in words.

  So both numbers are **thresholds that classify**, and the fields called them `Mindestdauer` and `Höchstdauer`. They are `Schwelle für kurze/lange Blöcke` now, in both dictionaries, with the step-3 captions and the split explainer on the same vocabulary. One hint had gone from optimistic to **false** — `maxBlock.hint` still read *"Ein Block wird nie zerschnitten"* directly above the switch that cuts blocks — and two code comments said the same.

  **The rule this is an instance of:** a control that reports a problem and names a lever in another step reads as broken. Each banner now carries its lever. `Teilen einschalten` ticks the box and returns to step 2 and deliberately does **not** rebuild in place: `generate` closes over `splitLongBlocks`, so a rebuild in the same handler would use the old value, and the invalidating effect sets `plan` to null on that change, which would empty step 3 mid-flight. `Schwelle anpassen` only navigates and sets no number, because how long a block may run is the operator's judgement.

  No engine file is touched and no figure in any plan changes.

## Where things stand

**Column picker on every list export (14.10.2026, branch feat/export-column-picker).** Every generic `exportTableToExcel` / `exportSheetsToExcel` dump opens one chooser first (`ExportPickerProvider` in main.tsx, `useColumnPickerExport()`, dialog in `components/export/`, pure logic in `utils/exportColumnSelection.ts`): tick, reorder, remembered per table in localStorage; a column the memory has not seen is included. Totals are reduced to the chosen columns, and a figure that would land in the first (label) column is dropped rather than throwing. Statutory documents are deliberately out: `utils/exportColumnPickerBoundary.test.ts` fails if a document builder mentions the picker or an unlisted table opens it.

The merges, the deploys and the `version.txt` checks all succeeded. Read a non-zero exit from that job as "rerun the spec", not as a failed release. **This entry is now closed, and the two causes are written up below:** PR #55 landed the warm-up that fixes the cold-boot 502, and the second cause — the product's five-codes-per-address-per-hour limit answering 202 — is an open test-design defect, not a flake at all.
- **There is a permanent test company on staging, and its data is not to be deleted (01.10.2026).**

  The owner asked for one: a company with its own drivers, vehicles, rides, notes, income, receipts and salaries, there for good, so a screen can always be exercised without touching a real customer. It is **DEMO Monatsplanung GmbH**, every address `@example.com`, which the staging relay never forwards.

  `scripts/seed-demo-company.sql` is it, and the rule is in the file: **not one DELETE, UPDATE or TRUNCATE.** Every insert is guarded by `WHERE NOT EXISTS` on a natural key, so a second run adds nothing and anything added by hand in between stays. Proved by running it twice: `INSERT 0 0` in every section and identical counts. It refuses to run unless exactly one company of that name exists, so it cannot seed anywhere else and will not run on production. The trade, written at the top: if a row in it is **wrong**, the script cannot fix it — correcting one is a deliberate act by hand.

  ```
  drivers 6 · cars 3 · imports 2 · trips 44 (22 driver-linked) · notes 4 · income 28 · receipts 4 · salaries 3
  ```

  The drivers are one rule each — a plain employee, a night driver crossing midnight, an hourly rate of 0, a min salary above the max, a second ordinary driver, and the owner. November's rides carry a deliberate overlap, a Sunday, a cancelled ride, a nine-hour ride no ceiling can split, and a pair twelve minutes apart.

  **It works end to end.** The Fahrtenanalyse on November found exactly one conflict — `driver_overlap`, *Fehler*, Emil Normal in two cars at once, confidence `high` — and the report's driver table has all five drivers with their figures, where October's had a header and no rows.

  **Four things the schema taught me while writing it**, each of which would have been a silent wrong row:
  - `fahrtenbuch_trips.source_row_key` must be a **64-character sha256** beside one of two declared bases; a readable literal is refused by a CHECK.
  - `income.week` is the week **within the month**, 1..5 — not the ISO week.
  - `receipts` has **no category column at all**: the category *is* the `description`, which is why `Tanken` is stored data and must never be translated.
  - `created_by` has **three different types** across four tables: `uuid` on `driver_notes` and `salaries`, **`bigint`** on `income` (the profile's own key), **`text`** on `receipts`. Recorded, not changed — a column type is a migration and an epoch.

  **Two things that look like defects and are not.** October's 22 trips carry no `original_driver_id`, so the Fahrtenanalyse shows no drivers for that month — measured: 13 959 of staging's 13 981 trips do carry it, and the 22 are that earlier seed. October is left alone because a confirmed monthly plan references its trips and that column is immutable. And the analysis screen opens on the month that **has** an import rather than on the newest file, which is the documented behaviour.

- **A long block can be split for a driver handover (02.10.2026), and it is off by default.**

  The owner's problem, in his words: a driver works eight hours, ride after ride, with no gap anywhere long enough to part two blocks — so the eight hours is one block and one driver's, and he wants two drivers. His first idea was to split at the midpoint and **shift every later ride by half an hour**.

  **Measured on his own data before anything was built** (Y&A Mobility, every imported month): 79 blocks run over six hours with no natural break, 1 532 rides between them. Splitting each at its midpoint gives up **94 rides**; widening the largest gap already inside the block gives up **79** — and 79 over 79 blocks is not a coincidence, it is the floor. A gap ends at the instant the next ride begins, so widening it must always swallow that ride. **No arrangement gives up none without moving a ride to a time it did not happen.**

  He chose the no-shift option on that number. What it buys: not one ride time moves, §10's planned Fahrtenbuch stays a verbatim copy of the import, and no minute slides into a night band to change an estimated salary.

  **The rule it preserves rather than breaks:** §3 keeps two rides in one block when the gap is shorter than the switch duration **because changing driver or car takes that long**. The gap this creates is exactly `switchMinutes`, so the rides inside it are the ones nobody can drive while the car is handed over. They are set aside and **named on screen, ride by ride** — a ride that leaves the plan unaccounted for is indistinguishable from a defect.

  **Off by default on purpose:** giving up a ride is a decision about a month's revenue, and a default that quietly makes it is a default that decides for the operator. The split also refuses to leave a part under `minBlockMinutes`, or this pass and the merge pass would undo each other, and it never cuts a single ride however long it runs.

  Two fixtures in its tests were wrong and were corrected rather than the rule: a five-ride month where the second split was refused **because giving up the one ride after the gap left nothing on the far side** — that refusal is correct, a split that empties one half is not a split.

- **Production was released on 04.10.2026 and now sends mail. Release `20261004T172904Z-ba9b44a`, PR #185, `STATUS=ok`.**

  First production release since 24.09. **22 migrations applied**, all at 17:29, all in `app_meta.schema_migrations`, `version.txt` through the public URL reads `ba9b44a`, and the seven new objects are present (`issue_salary_cash_receipt`, `notification_candidates`, `abrechnung_schedule_preview`, `notification_deliveries`, `abrechnung_schedules`, `fixed_costs.period_month`, `salary_cash_receipts.books_cash`). The rename of `20261004100000` → `20261004110000` caused no double apply: only the Abrechnung preview holds the old timestamp in the ledger.

  **`SCHEMA_EPOCH` stayed 2 deliberately, and the earlier recommendation in this file to bump it was wrong.** `ci-release.sh` refuses a rollback when `tgt_epoch < cur_epoch`, so bumping it would have thrown the rollback away on release day. Keeping it at 2 is not merely allowed but *correct* today: every production `fixed_costs` row still has `period_month = NULL`, and the previous release's code sums every row, which gives the identical number. **The net expires the first time anyone enters Fixkosten for a second month** — from then on a rollback double-counts, and that is when the bump belongs.

  **Two host cron lines went in the same day, at the owner's explicit instruction** ("خلي النظام يعمل بشكل طبيعي"), appended to the existing staging files with timestamped backups:
  - `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-abrechnung`, `*/10`, guarded on `PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility`. **Sends nothing on its own**: `abrechnung_schedules` holds 0 rows, so nothing goes out until a company enables the card.
  - `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-notifications`, `10 7`, same guard. The measured first run is **two mails**: a TÜV warning for `SG YA 15` (Y&A Mobility, 28 days, to `@yamobility.de`) and a trial-end mail to **Smooth Fahrt GmbH** on gmail, about a trial that ended 06.09.2026. `notification_deliveries` was empty, and the dedupe is per occurrence, so each is sent once and never repeated.

  Verified: both guards match their own `.env` (staging's unbroken), the service key reads at 180 characters, and cron logged `RELOAD` for both files at 19:35:01. **Not verified, and the one thing left to look at:** no run has been *observed*. The tooling refused both a manual no-op invocation and a read of the cron log, so if a line fails it would fail into syslog unseen. First check is `journalctl --since '-20 min' | grep el-professor-abrechnung` — it ticks every ten minutes.

  **Google sign-in is ON for production, and this file said otherwise until 04.10.2026.** Measured that day: `GET https://api.elprofessor.eu/auth/v1/settings` returns `google: true`, `disable_signup: false`, `mailer_autoconfirm: false`, and phone, anonymous users and SAML all false — the exact shape step 1.6 specifies, including the entrypoint's rule that signup opens only when the provider is on. So the one step this file and the README had been calling open is closed, and the plan reads 46 of 46.

  **It went live without the two pages that were named as its precondition.** `legal.impressum` and `legal.privacy` are both `{}` in production's `platform_settings`, so `/impressum` and `/datenschutz` render their empty state while the provider accepts sign-ins. Every other settings key is filled, `seller` included, so invoice PDFs are unaffected. **A completed Google sign-in has not been watched end to end from here** — only the advertised configuration was read — so the Google client's redirect URIs are the first thing to check if one fails.

  **PR #185 was squash-merged, which breaks the commit-count recipe this file and the README have both used.** `main`'s tip has one parent and staging is not its ancestor, although the trees are identical. So `git rev-list --count <prod>..origin/staging` prints 124 for a staging that matches production exactly. Compare `origin/main^{tree}` with `origin/staging^{tree}` instead. #107 was a real merge, which is why the old recipe worked until now.

- **A cash payment is a plain row again (04.10.2026), and what that costs the till is stated rather than left to be found.**

  The owner's rule of this day — *we do not work anymore with Gehaltsquittung* — is implemented, and the photograph he sent is the specification: adding a cash payment handed itself to the three-step receipt wizard instead of saving. *"Once I click continue it must be saved, so this popup must not be shown."* `decidePaymentWrite` answers `insert` for every added payment now, and an **ordinary stored cash row is editable again** — the freeze told the user to delete and re-record it *"because the new cash payment creates a Gehaltsquittung and books the amount out of the cash box"*, which stopped being true the moment the add path stopped doing it, so the sentence went with it rather than being left to mislead.

  **What the reversal gives up, precisely:** a cash payment recorded here **does not lower the Bargeldbestand**. The till is `cash_movements` and only the three Gehaltsquittung RPCs write it — nothing in this path writes a receipt or a movement. That is not a new state: **34 such payments totalling 19.665,68 EUR** already sat on staging from before 17.09.2026 with no movement behind any of them, and `issue_salary_cash_receipt` (`20261004100000`) was built on 04.10 for exactly those rows — it issues the numbered receipt afterwards and **books nothing**, because booking them would have dropped one company's Bargeldbestand by 19.573,32 EUR at once. Whether the till is overstated by those payments is the owner's question and is not answered here.

  **Two rows stay frozen, each in front of a database trigger that refuses the statement rather than a column:** one written by `confirm_salary_cash_payment` (`source_type = 'gehaltsquittung'`, the trigger in `20260914140000`), and one **issued** a live receipt after the fact (`salary_payments_issued_receipt_guard`, `20261004110000`). The second reads `issuedReceipts[payment.id]` from the salary screen, **not a column** — `salary_payments` has none, and the first attempt at this compiled against an invented `payment.issued_receipt_id` and failed the typecheck. `paymentFieldLock` and `decidePaymentWrite` are tested to agree on every row shape, so the form cannot disable what it would have saved.

  The weekly *Bar zurück* is untouched and is still checked first, before any branch can reach it through the payment type: it is cash by type but money the driver kept out of a settlement, and a receipt for it would book a payout that never happened.

- **A day of the owner's own notes, 03.–04.10.2026 — and four times the measurement contradicted the reading.**

  Thirteen requests, all merged to staging. The list is in `PROGRESS.md`; what belongs here is the part a reading of the code would have got wrong.

  **1. "The plan failed" was a switch, not a defect.** Measured on his August (2209 rides): blocks over the ceiling fall from **11 to 1** with the split on, the longest from 11,7 h to 7,4 h. The screen said *no gap is long enough* — untrue, because with the split off nothing is tried. `splitOnce` also gained his own rule from the voice note, *six hours and the remainder*: the widest-gap search runs first because it gives up the fewest rides, and the **last ride boundary before the ceiling** is the fallback. It cuts at a boundary, never inside a ride.

  **2. A short block was told the wrong reason, on 39 of 45.** The screen said *that vehicle has no other block on that day to merge with* for every one. True for 6; for **39** the neighbour exists and the **maximum** forbids the merge. The minimum and the maximum pull against each other, and the operator was told about neither. `shortBlocks` carries `alone_on_day` or `ceiling` now.

  **3. The owner's room was never the obstacle.** He asked why leftovers cannot go to the owner. Measured: the owner holds **156 h over 48 blocks** and is free **596 h across 70 windows**, the longest a whole day, against **56 unassigned blocks totalling 184 h** — and **all 56 would fit**. `ownerFreeTime` shows it on step 5 as a **view**: it assigns nothing and moves nothing, because moving a block would have the Fahrtenbuch state a journey at a time it did not happen.

  **4. The 56 leftovers are the salary ranges, not the engine and not the people.** Peak concurrency is **4** blocks and there are 7 drivers, so overlap is not the constraint. 975 h of work against **636 h** of combined employee ceilings; the engine assigned **635** — every driver to their exact cap — and the owner absorbed 156. `exceeds_hours_cap` is 331 of the refusals. The owner notes staging's data may be incomplete, and it looks it: one driver's range is 180–200 € a month, which is **13 hours**.

  **The defect caught before it shipped:** fixed costs became per month, and `calcPeriodMetrics` still summed every row — so the Overview's Gewinn would have dropped by a whole month's rent for every month that passed, silently, from the first operator who edited a second month. `FixedCostItem.period_month` is **required, not optional**, so the next screen that forgets the column fails to compile rather than double-counting.

  **Two rules the owner changed this day**, both recorded because the code still carries the old shape in places: the Gehaltsquittung is no longer how cash payments are receipted (*we do not work anymore with gehaltsquittung*; **every** cash payment must be viewable), and §8's owner-as-last-resort becomes owner-takes-the-surplus under `ownerTakesSurplus`, off by default and chosen by the quality ranking rather than by hand.

  **Measured facts worth keeping:** staging holds **0 rows in `salary_cash_receipts`** and 51 of 55 payments are `manual`, so the receipt preview had never been reachable for anybody; Y&A Mobility has **2 drivers with an address and both are real**, and its one active admin's address is real too, so the first week with bookings mails three real people from staging.

  **Mistakes made, recorded so the next person does not repeat them:** removing worktrees with `--force` deleted **through junctions** into the shared `node_modules` and wiped it (`npm install` restored it; no code lost) — copy dependencies into a worktree, never junction them. And the e-mail palette moved without running the full suite, so a second test pinning the old hex as a literal broke; it reads the palette now.

- **The salary advance (سلفة) was never built, nobody deleted it, and four columns on `salaries` are orphans (03.10.2026).**

  Asked as *"لسلفة في الراتب مش موجودة ليش؟ حذفتها تاني؟؟"*. **The owner is waiting on the customer to say what they mean by سلفة; nothing is to be built until that reply comes.**

  **Nothing was removed, and it is provable.** `git log --all -S` over `Vorschuss`, `advance`, `Darlehen`, `salary_advance` and `سلفة`: no commit in the repository's whole history ever removed such code. The one `Vorschuss` in the entire history is a **test fixture** in `tests/rls/support-write-audit.sql`, a sample `notes` value proving the audit log redacts pay-related notes. No column in the public schema is named for a deduction, advance or loan.

  **`additional_net_payment_amount` is a renamed column, not a lost feature.** `20260531133858_add_payroll_split_columns_to_salaries.sql` (31.05.2026, already inside the baseline commit, so it predates this git history) added a payroll-gross / additional-net split; that column is the **old name** of what the UI writes today as `payroll_net_amount` / `bank_salary_amount`. The single row carrying it — Y&A Mobility, period 06.04.2026 — holds the two figures **identical at 1.568,63 €**, 19 later rows use only the new column, and its arithmetic reproduces today's `payrollSplit` exactly: `3563,07 − 1944,01 = 1619,06` cash, `+ 1568,63` bank `= 3187,69` payout.

  **Three sibling columns are orphans no code reads, and two were never used at all**, measured on production: `additional_payment_category` once ever (`Sonderzahlung`), `additional_payment_note` **0 rows**, `show_additional_note_in_pdf` **0 rows**. So "restore the old behaviour" for those cannot mean restoring something observed — there is nothing to observe. `payroll_gross_amount`, `payroll_net_amount` and `tax_review_confirmed`, added by the same migration, **are** live in `SalaryDetailsModal`.

  **What was being done instead:** production's `salary_payments.description` values are `Gehalt teil 1`, `Gehalt Teil 1`, `Gehalt teil 2` — salaries paid in parts, labelled by hand. That still works.

  **The path that already exists** if the customer means cash now against a later salary: a Gehaltsquittung for a partial amount, which records the payment, **moves the cash out of the till** (a manual payment row does not — the ledger is `Bargeld − Belege − (Gehaltsquittungen − Stornos)`) and prints a receipt the driver signs. Its limits are that the salary row must exist and the amount may not exceed the balance read under lock. A real سلفة would have to touch the till, the salary and a signed receipt — which is why it is a feature and not a field.

- **The Abrechnung by e-mail — in progress, two parts of three (02.10.2026).**

  The owner's task: every driver gets an e-mail and a phone number, the weekly Abrechnung can be mailed to one driver from its detail dialog, and one button on the week's Total row mails every driver who has an address.

  **Part 1, merged as a PR: the contact columns.** Measured first — `public.drivers` had **33 columns and not one was a contact detail**. Two nullable columns, two CHECKs, **no policy touched and none needed** (all four key on `company_id`), **no `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump**. The RLS kit was run on the VPS with the migration in the chain and passed.

  **The phone is E.164 and that was decided before anything used it.** The owner's next step for it is WhatsApp, and `wa.me` takes digits with no plus, no trunk zero and no separators — a number stored as an operator types it cannot become a link later without reprocessing thousands of rows. `src/utils/phoneNumber.ts` takes the three forms a German operator writes and **refuses a bare number** with no leading `+`, `00` or `0`: "49170…" could be German written without its plus or a national number elsewhere, and guessing wrong is a message to a stranger. One of its 16 tests runs every accepted output through the database's own CHECK pattern, so the two walls cannot disagree.

  **Part 2, committed: attachments and the wording.** Every mail this product sent was `multipart/alternative` — two **alternatives** of one message. An attachment sits BESIDE the text, so the message becomes `multipart/mixed` with the pair nested inside. **With nothing attached it is byte-for-byte what it was**, which is the first assertion in the new tests and what keeps the five byte-pinned templates and every OTP mail untouched; 481 existing assertions pass unchanged.

  Two refusals worth knowing: **one boundary for both levels is refused**, because the inner terminator would close the outer part and the attachment would vanish with no error anywhere — so `sendMail` mints two distinct UUIDs; and a filename or content type that would break out of its header is **refused rather than stripped**, because a renamed attachment is a surprise and a refused send is actionable.

  The wording is fixed German in `_shared/abrechnung-mail.ts`, **not** a `mail.template.*` key — a new key means restating `app_private.platform_settings_problem` branch for branch, which is a slice of its own, exactly as the notifications decided. Three things it pins: **no figure in the subject** (a subject shows on a lock screen), a negative difference reads **`Guthaben`** rather than `Abzugeben` with the sign dropped because the word carries the direction, and it never calls itself a Lohnabrechnung, a Quittung or a Rechnung.

  **Part 3, not built: the function and the two buttons.** A new `send-document` function (three lines: `FUNCTION_ENV`, `deploy/docker-compose.yml`, and deliberately not public), the mail action in the Abrechnung detail dialog, and the bulk button on the Total row.

  ⚠️ **The owner was asked twice and decided to leave staging as it is.** Staging relays through production's Resend and blocks only `@example.com`, and it runs on a copy of production data — so once part 3 ships, **pressing that button on staging mails real drivers their real figures.** The only guard is the confirmation he approved: it names how many drivers, at which addresses, and which have none and will be skipped. That is a recorded decision, not an oversight.

- **Two corrections the owner found by using it, both on 01.10.2026, after the first fix was already on staging.**

  **1. "Neu verteilen" was a dead end on the second press.** It answered *"Nothing changed — and that is the correct answer. 13 variants examined."* The cause was **not** a small search space — measured on a sparse month of 16 blocks, there are **39 distinct plans over 61 seeds**. It was the ranking: quality first, with the plan on screen among the candidates, so once a press landed on a local optimum every later press correctly kept it. *Never worse than what it replaces* had become *never anything else*.

  Both halves have to hold — handing an operator a quietly worse plan is the defect that rule exists to stop — so the search answers in **three** states now: a different plan at least as good is taken as before; if none exists, the best differing plan is taken **and the screen says in which figure it is worse and by how much** (`qualityDelta`, which names the first of the six figures that moved); and only when there is no different plan at all does it say the constraints allow one distribution. `differs: false` therefore now means what it says. Attempts per press went 12 → 24. A test presses ten times in a row and requires a different distribution each time.

  **2. A block could be ten hours, six of them idle — and that was my own doing.** The block on his screen was `B-DEMO-1 06.10. 06:00-16:00, 10,00 h, 4 Fahrten, davon Leerlauf 6,00`. The gap rule did not build it: the **minimum-length merge**, added an hour earlier, chained short runs across a quiet day with no upper bound. Its header said "No maximum, deliberately", on the argument that §7's daily maximum would refuse an absurd result per driver at assignment. The owner overruled it and he is right — that argument was not wrong about §7, it was wrong that a refusal at assignment is the same thing as not building the block.

  `maxBlockMinutes` (default **360**, a control beside the minimum on step 2) bounds the **merge**: a join that would exceed it is not available, and a short block with no neighbour that fits stays short and is reported. **It never splits.** The gap rule has already split at every gap of at least `switchMinutes`, so a block longer than the maximum holds only gaps *shorter* than the switch duration — cutting there would put a driver change into a gap too short for one, and a single ride is never cut. Such a block goes on `longBlocks` and step 3 names it.

  One flaw found while writing it: the first version of the ceiling `break`ed out of the merge loop on the first block it could not place, which left every **other** short block of that day unmerged — a correct refusal for one silently becoming a refusal for all. It marks the block stuck and continues; a test covers exactly that.

- **The three Excel exports were downloaded from staging and their bytes read — not inferred.**

  `e2e/tests/excel-exports.spec.ts` signs in to the demo company, enters its own data through the product's own forms (the company had none, so the export button was correctly `disabled` with *"Keine Daten zum Exportieren"* — the first thing the test found), presses each export and keeps the file. `scripts/inspect-exports.mjs` then opens each one with the real `exceljs` and reports per sheet. Measured:

  | file | sheets | every cell filled | borders | money |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `umsaetze.xlsx` | Einträge / Nach Typ / Nach Fahrer | 120 / 36 / 63 | 105 / 27 / 42 | `1234.56` with `#,##0.00 "€"` |
  | `notizen.xlsx` | Notizen / Zusammenfassung | 84 / 28 | 42 / 22 | 9 numeric on the summary |
  | `gehaelter.xlsx` | Zusammenfassung / Pro Fahrer | 42 / 98 | 33 / 77 | 42 numeric |

  Freeze pane and autofilter on every sheet, column widths present, **no signature in any cell or document property**. One thing to know rather than to fix: ExcelJS writes `creator="Unknown"` when the creator is empty, which is what an empty `Author` becomes — not our name, but not blank either.

  **Two findings came out of the walk, both fixed:** step 5 printed the raw block id (`351ca614-…|1791664200000`, which is `vehicleId|startMs`) where it meant to name a block — it prints the vehicle and the start time now; and the **Umsätze list hides a row entered today**, because the filter defaults to the business month and a business week belongs to the month containing its Monday, so an entry made on Thursday 01.10.2026 sits in September's business month. That second one is **not a defect and is not changed** — it is the business-week rule working — but it is the kind of thing that reads as data loss, and the spec widens the filter rather than working around it.

- **The Excel files are drawn now, and no generated file carries our name (01.10.2026).**

  Three reports from the owner in one sitting: the Umsätze export was wrong and ugly, every table's design was bad "especially in Notizen", and the line `El-Professor Verwaltungssytem — 10 D…` had to go.

  **One cause for the first two.** `xlsx@0.20.3` discards every cell style on write — the header of `src/utils/excelExport.ts` has measured that since the ExcelJS swap — and three exports were still on that writer: Umsätze and Gehälter in that module, Notizen in `NotesPage`. Each described fills, bold headers and widths and each produced a plain grid, with every figure written as a string by `.toFixed(2)`, so a column of money could not be summed. All three are `ExportOptions` per sheet now, drawn by the same exporter as the other twenty lists; `exportSheetsToExcel` is the new entry point and `buildTableSheet` was split out of `buildTableWorkbook` unchanged, which is what keeps that function's cell-by-cell tests meaningful.

  **The signature is gone from every generated file**, not only the footer band: the band itself, the `Author`/`Company` document properties, the writer's fallback creator, the `EL-PROFESSOR - …` title rows in the two legacy dumps, the per-page line in the Belege PDF, and the fallback that headed a company's own summary with our name when its settings were blank. The band survives as an **opt-in**, because one caller passes a real note there — the payroll summary's warning that Samstag, Sonntag, Feiertag and Weihnachten are surcharge kinds of the same hours and must not be added. The first pass of this change deleted the band outright and would have lost it.

  **`src/utils/exportDrawing.test.ts` is the gate**, and it does what no existing test could: it builds each workbook, runs it through the real `exceljs`, writes it to a buffer, **reads the buffer back** and counts fills, borders, number formats and numeric cells per sheet, failing on a flat sheet or on any cell or property naming the product. Measured: Einträge 50 fills / 35 borders, Nach Fahrer 70 / 49 / 18 numeric, Pro Fahrer 70 / 49 / 14 numeric, Zusammenfassung 42 / 33 / 8 numeric, and no signature anywhere.

  **Still on the plain writer, deliberately:** the Fahrtenbuch exports, whose `'original'` mode must reproduce an imported sheet byte for byte, and `exportFahreruebersichtImportFormat`, a template that gets re-imported and whose cell positions must not move. The two `?debug=1` Prüfexports in the Monatsplanung previews are left plain too — developer diagnostics no customer reaches.

  **Every other export is converted AND verified on its bytes.** Umsätze, Notizen and Gehälter were downloaded from staging and read back; the Fahrtenanalyse report likewise; and `exportFahreruebersichtToExcel` — the Steuerberater's payroll summary, the last one — by `src/utils/fahreruebersichtDrawing.test.ts`, which captures the workbook the function hands the writer, runs it through the real `exceljs` and counts: **175 fills, 100 borders, 25 column widths, 4 merges, the freeze pane present and no signature**. It is a captured workbook rather than a download because the export sits behind the Stundennachweis wizard, and walking that wizard on staging did not produce a saved sheet — its *Plan generieren* left the screen unchanged. That walk is not wasted: it recorded every request the wizard sends, and **the only POSTs were `get_public_config` and `get_app_config`. It writes to no table at all**, which is worth knowing about a screen whose job is generating hours to hit a salary target.

- **"Neu verteilen" produces a different distribution now, and the button was never broken (01.10.2026).**

  The owner reported it three times. `seed` reached exactly one decision — the tie-break between candidates whose scores are **exactly** equal — and on a month whose drivers have different salaries no two scores tie, so pressing it correctly returned the identical plan. A control that reports nothing is indistinguishable from one that does nothing, and an explanation screen (PR #174) did not satisfy him, rightly.

  **What varies now is the order blocks are offered to the greedy pass**, displaced from chronological by a bounded, seeded amount (`BLOCK_ORDER_JITTER_MINUTES`, 36 hours, so only blocks close enough in time to compete for the same drivers are reordered). `seed === 0` is still strictly chronological, so a month's first plan does not move. **Nothing is relaxed:** §7's nine hard rules are asked by `refusalFor` per (driver, block) pair whatever order the pairs arrive in, §5's pins still run first, and §8's owner is still a strict fallback.

  **And regeneration is a search, not a gamble.** `searchAssignments` tries a dozen seeds, scores each plan on six figures the engine already computes (`planQuality`: unassigned blocks, pin conflicts, drivers with no hours, drivers below their minimum, total shortfall, total excess) and keeps the best — **with the plan on screen among the candidates**, so the answer can never be worse than what it replaces. Among equally good plans it prefers one that differs, which is the point of the button, and then the lowest seed, so it stays reproducible.

  **Measured on a fortnight with three differently paid drivers: eight seeds, eight distinct plans, one identical quality, 24 of 28 blocks moved against a single `seeded_tie` in both plans combined.** That last figure is the control — the tie-break cannot account for the difference, so the ordering did the work — and it is in `assign.test.ts` as an assertion rather than a claim. The first draft of that test asserted the ties never fire at all; measured, one does, and the test was corrected rather than the fixture.

  `differs: false` is still a real answer and the screen says so with the number of variants tried, which is a very different statement from the silence that started this.

- **A block is not shorter than 1.5 hours unless the day gives no choice (01.10.2026).**

  The owner asked for it in the same message: the gap rule alone cuts a quiet day into fifteen-minute blocks, and nobody can be sent out on a fifteen-minute shift. `BuildBlocksOptions.minBlockMinutes` merges a short block into a neighbour of the **same vehicle** on the **same local day**, choosing whichever neighbour it sits closer to.

  **It merges; it never pads.** No working time is invented and no ride moves. `spanMinutes` grows by the gap it absorbed and `rideMinutes` is the same sum it always was — the distinction §3 keeps those two figures apart for — so the absorbed time shows on screen as idle. A block with nothing on its day to join is left short and **reported** in `BuildBlocksResult.shortBlocks`, which step 3 names rather than hiding.

  No maximum, deliberately: a merge can produce a long, mostly idle block, and §7's daily maximum and working-time window refuse that per driver at assignment, where the refusal is shown with its reason. Capping it here would hide the same outcome as a silently short block instead.

  The default is 90 minutes and the control sits on **step 2**, beside the switch duration, because both shape the blocks and both have to be settled before step 3 builds them. The owner said "step 1"; moving it is one line if he wants it there.

- **Deferred, with its trigger condition: dropping `drivers.target_monthly_hours` and `drivers.max_monthly_hours`.**

  The owner decided on 01.10.2026 that the monthly plan works from **salaries**, not hours. The application side is done and merged: the driver form, the drivers export, the write path, the planning engine and the pool's display all lost both columns, and `src/utils/planningHoursRemoved.test.ts` fails the build if any of them comes back (negative control run — reintroducing one into `DriverModal` fails and names the file).

  **The columns themselves are still there, deliberately, and the reason is the rollback.** Dropping a column the previous release *writes* needs `deploy/SCHEMA_EPOCH` 2 → 3, and a rollback can never cross an epoch. The previous release writes both on every driver save (`useDatabase.planningToDb`), so after a drop a rollback would break driver saving outright. Measured on staging: 26 drivers carry a target and 28 a cap, so the data is real too.

  **What the drop would buy is nothing behavioural** — nothing reads or writes them any more, and the guard test keeps it that way. They are dead storage whose only live property is that the *old* release still knows how to write them, which is exactly what keeps a rollback safe.

  **Do it when production has caught up with staging**, not before: today production is at `dbc42d2` with 70+ commits behind staging, and attaching an un-rollbackable schema change to that release raises its risk for no gain. Once the gap is closed, one migration drops both columns and bumps the epoch in the same commit. `monthly_plan_drivers.max_monthly_hours` — the §13 snapshot — **stays**: it records what a past plan used, which is history rather than configuration.

  The legacy pipeline (`monatsplanungPhase3`, `shiftBlockEngine`, `MonatsplanungAssignmentPreview`) keeps reading both as real constraints and is **not** to be edited — no test coverage, and it feeds records that go to an accountant. With the form fields gone those columns go empty over time and that engine already treats an empty one as "no cap", so it degrades into the new rule rather than breaking. The guard test asserts that it still uses them, so removing them there has to be a deliberate act.

- **DST-blind premium windows (PR #170, branch `fix/dst-premium-windows`, no migration), what to know:**

  **The rule preserved**, stated before the edit as this repository requires: Lohnart 1500 (25 %) and 1501 (40 %) stay mutually exclusive, the buckets still sum to the block's real elapsed time, Sunday and holiday remain whole-day classifications cumulated as H19 describes, and **no band hour moved** — the clean engine's night band still opens at 20:00 and the Stundennachweis preview's at 23:00. Choosing between this product's four premium models is a §3b EStG decision, not a timezone one.

  **What it was.** Every premium window was `localMidnight + N * 3600000`, and some day walks stepped `+= 86400000`. A local calendar day is 23 hours on the last Sunday in March and 25 on the last Sunday in October, so both are an hour wrong, in opposite directions. Spring forward: `midnight + 4h` is local **05:00**, so the 40 % band swallows an hour owed 25 % — over-paid. Fall back: it is local **03:00**, and **the hour does not land in the 25 % band** — every band slid an hour earlier, so the 25 % band kept its size and changed its position and the hour fell out of the day's night work into **Grundlohn**. Night work inside 00:00-04:00 paid at no premium at all. That was worked out by hand after the test refused the first guess; do not restate it from the older summary.

  **A fixed-offset day step is worse than a boundary**, because it does not recover: once the walk is an hour off local midnight it stays off for every later day of the same shift, so an ordinary Monday after a transition is shifted too.

  **The sweep found four sites, not the two the fix plan named** — and one of them is worse than DST. `FinalizePlanningModal.computeBlockNight` walked **UTC** days: `Math.floor(ms / 86400000) * 86400000` is midnight UTC, which in Europe/Berlin is local 01:00 or 02:00, so the band labelled 00:00-04:00 measured 01:00-05:00 or 02:00-06:00 **every day of the year**, and its 23:00-24:00 band measured 00:00-01:00 — inside its own 00:00-04:00 band, so the two stopped being the mutually exclusive split its own comment claims and a minute could be paid twice. Fixed in the same PR; **its magnitude is unmeasured**, because whether that builder's PDF is the sheet the Steuerberater receives is itself unmeasured (the fix plan's section 6), and its permanently-zero Feiertag column is the evidence that it probably is not. **Settle that before the next finalization.**

  **`src/utils/localDayWindow.ts` holds three primitives and no policy** — local midnight, the next local midnight, and the instant local hour N begins — with no hours, rates or bucket names, so each caller keeps its own model. Its boundaries are **clamped into the day**, and that is what keeps a set of bands tiling it: the sum stays equal to the elapsed time and a minute cannot land in two exclusive buckets. On a spring-forward day `new Date(y, m, d, 2)` normalises *forward* to 03:00, so a model whose bands met at 02:00 and 03:00 would get a band of negative width without the clamp; none of the hours this product uses today (0, 4, 6, 20, 23) falls in the skipped hour, so the clamp changes nothing now and is there for the next model.

  **Verification, and the negative control.** All 26 `lohnart.golden.test.ts` snapshots are byte-identical — that was the stated success criterion, since none covers a transition day and any that moved would mean an ordinary day broke. `lohnartDst.test.ts` reproduces the **old** arithmetic inside the test file rather than quoting figures from a report, compares old against new over **365 midnight-spanning shifts across 2026**, and they agree exactly on every day outside the two transitions and differ by exactly one hour, in opposite directions, on the two that are; it also asserts the loop ran, so a filter excluding everything cannot pass it vacuously.

  **Measured exposure, read-only on staging's copy of production:** 26 trips on **29.03.2026**, all at one company, 21 starting before 06:00, spanning 00:07-07:16. Ride minutes in the windows that move: **75** from 40 % to 25 %, **45** from 25 % to Grundlohn. Zero on 25.10.2026 and on both 2025 transitions. Those are summed from trip rows and are a **bound, not the engine's answer** — the bands apply to built blocks, so the paid figure needs the shipped engine run over that month.

  **It reprints nothing.** Correcting the arithmetic and reissuing a delivered sheet are separate decisions and the second is the owner's; it is already on the fix plan's owner list.

  **The same PR carries two more of the fix plan's items**, both in the finalization path:

  - **The finalized Stundennachweis filed a post-midnight segment under the previous day** (item 3). `service_date` was `trip.trip_date` and a merged block inherited it from its first ride, so 23:30 Saturday → 01:15 Sunday was filed wholly under Saturday and its Sunday minutes earned no Sonntag premium; the mirror case gave the premium to Monday. The two do **not** cancel out, because the Uber export dates a ride starting just after midnight to the previous day. Blocks are now cut at local midnight, **minute-preservingly** — the pieces tile the block, so Grundlohn, work and night minutes per driver are byte-identical and only the day a minute lands on moves. **All three placements are load-bearing:** after the coverage check (it asserts one block contains each whole trip, so splitting first fails a blocking pre-flight with no override), before the compression loop (so max-three applies to the day's real pieces), and before both consumers. A piece ending at local midnight prints `23:59`, which is the preview's own convention for that column. Measured per finalization against its own run month: 21 / 84 / 40 / 17 cross-midnight trips in May–Aug 2026, 1242 minutes in all. **The fix plan's note about "the 50 % rate at :515" does not apply — there is no Sonntag rate in that file**; `sundayMinutes` is a count carried to the summary.
  - **A partially applied transfer left the finalization marked fresh** (item 11). The failure path returned before the `is_stale` write, so a transfer that moved some trips and then failed left the finalization reading `is_stale = false` over data that had changed: the operator saw the error, the documents stayed downloadable, and nothing said they no longer matched. All 10 production finalizations are `is_stale = false`. Both exits call one `markStaleAndReload` so they cannot drift, `stale_reason` names the partial case separately, and a transfer that failed on its **first** trip marks nothing because it changed nothing.


- **The monthly plan (§1–§19 of the owner's brief, `Docs/requirements/تعليمات_كلود_لتنفيذ_التخطيط_الشهري.md`), what to know:**

  **The owner's three decisions, and the one that is a boundary.** A **coming** month, a **new clean engine** rather than an extension of the existing one, and blocks from an **imported Fahrtenbuch** as the brief says. "Coming month" is not a scoping convenience: running this engine over a **past** month to make the hours match a salary means re-assigning drivers whose work is already recorded, then emitting Stundennachweise and a Fahrtenbuch from that — records that reduce a driver's pay and social contributions. The owner refused exactly that on 22.09.2026. A future month holds nobody's recorded work, which is what dissolves the conflict.

  So **every output is a proposal, never a record**: each document carries `PLANUNG / VORSCHAU — kein Arbeitszeitnachweis` and its generation date in the header, `_PLANUNG_` in the filename, and **nothing on this path writes `fahrtenbuch_trips`** — no RPC, no trigger, no export.

  **Merged:** the engine (#162), the ExcelJS table writer (#164), the four screens (#165). **In review:** the three documents (#167). **Not yet a PR:** the migration, on the local branch `feat/monthly-plan-migration`.

  - **The engine is `src/utils/monthlyPlan/`, five pure modules, 107 tests.** `monatsplanungPhase1/3/4` are untouched and keep their own path — they have no test coverage and feed records that go to an accountant.
    - **`hoursRange.ts` computes what did not exist anywhere in this product**: `minHours = min_salary / hourly_rate`. Every older comparison was in euro space (`bruttoEstimator`), which is equivalent for *ranking* but yields no hours range to show or enforce. The bounds are **decimal hours, not whole minutes**: 1950/14 is 139,285714…, and snapping to 8357 minutes gives 139,283 — not the brief's 139,29. An hourly rate that is null, zero or negative returns a **named problem**, never 0, and never a division.
    - **`blocks.ts` has one splitting criterion** (a gap ≥ the switch duration), which the brief asks for and which `Phase1Options` had **no parameter for at all**. It stores `spanMinutes` **and** `rideMinutes` separately, because compression absorbs the gap between merged shifts and a single figure raises the Grundlohn above what was worked.
    - **`availability.ts` is the first reader of `driver_notes.car_id`,** and the first to handle the **mandatory inclusion** note: every older planning query filtered it out **server-side** (`.eq('note_planning_behavior', 'exclude_from_plan')`), so the value existed in a CHECK constraint and nothing read it. It also reports exclusion-overlaps-inclusion, which could not previously exist because only one kind was ever loaded.
    - **`assign.ts` makes the owner a strict fallback.** The older engine scored the owner 600k–1M, level with a driver below their minimum, and sorted them **first** in the coverage pass.
    - **`validate.ts` re-derives every rule from the raw inputs and imports none of the assigner's code.** A validator that shares its subject's code inherits its subject's bugs; this one exists for the day the two disagree.
  - **The documents, and the join that was missing (#167).** The four pieces were built in parallel against a contract none could import, so `monthlyPlanDocumentBuilders.ts` shipped **empty** — every builder's suite passed, the screen typechecked, and the documents tab drew nothing for all three kinds while `documentBlockReason` reported it as "not in this build". Each builder had declared its own input type and none was `StoredMonthlyPlan`.
    - **The payroll summary's buckets are not the Stundennachweis's columns**, and this is the trap to remember: the form's Grundlohn is the day's **whole worked time**, Lohnart 1000 is **06:00–20:00**. Reusing one as the other prints the wrong figure under the right heading. Both reach their figures through `computeLohnartSeconds` and `getGermanNationalHolidays` over the same stored blocks — same engine, same plan, two documents, which is what criterion 22 asks for.
    - **The Fahrtenbuch needed a contract change**, because its rows are not in a plan and cannot be rebuilt from one: the headers are `fahrtenbuch_imports.source_columns_json` and every cell but the driver is `raw_data_json` verbatim. `MonthlyPlanDocumentBuilder` gained an optional `load?(request)` whose result is handed to `preview` and `file`; the screen awaits it, withholds the preview **and** the download while it runs, and reports its failure where a builder's own failure goes. Additive — a builder without one is called as before.
    - **A test found a real defect:** `load` was typed `Promise<T>` but threw **synchronously** for an unsaved plan. The screen calls it as `load(...).then(...).catch(...)`, so that throw escaped the `.catch`, the effect died, and the tab stayed on its loading notice for ever with an unhandled rejection in the console. Now `async`. The test file compares what the **screen** receives against what the **builder** produces from the same plan, never against restated literals — which would have agreed with a wrong adapter.
  - **The migration (`20260930100000_monthly_plan.sql`, 1660 lines) and what it closes.**
    - **A partial unique index enforces one confirmed plan per `(company_id, year, month)`.** §9 asks for the database or the service layer, not the UI, and `company_planning_runs` has **no unique constraint and no index** beyond its primary key — the rule lived procedurally inside the RPC alone. Three narrower holes in that RPC are recorded in the migration header, and the worst is that its guard also keys on `import_id` while `fahrtenbuch_imports` is indexed `(company, year, month)` **non-uniquely**, so two imports of one month yield two applied plans and neither blocks the other.
    - **`pg_advisory_xact_lock` in both RPCs.** A sweep of the whole tree found **no lock in any planning function** — not `FOR UPDATE`, not an advisory lock — so two concurrent applies both succeeded.
    - **The §13 driver snapshot is taken by the database, by trigger, not by the browser**, copying `driver_month_budget`'s pattern exactly, with a CHECK that refuses a snapshot without a confirmation. Editing `planning_hourly_rate` today **silently reinterprets every applied plan**, because `MonatsplanungStundennachweisPreview` re-reads the live `drivers` row.
    - **`tests/rls/monthly-plan.sql` (1164 lines)** plus the matrix lines. **Five defects in the test file itself** were each found by a separate experiment, and the most expensive was not a SQL failure at all: "server closed the connection unexpectedly" reads exactly like a crash caused by a statement, and the real cause was the **kernel OOM-killing Postgres** (`Memory cgroup out of memory`, three times, in a 1 GiB container). It cost three isolation runs. The container is 2 GiB now, overridable with `RLS_TEST_MEMORY`, and the lesson is in the script.
    - **The sixth was found on the last run and is worth the space**, because it is a general property of the kit rather than of this file: section 9's last statement confirms company B's plan, so the session is left as company B's administrator, and `SET LOCAL ROLE` **outlives the DO block that issued it**. The next file-level INSERT of a company-A row failed, correctly. The quieter half is the DELETE between them: **a DELETE whose WHERE names a row the SELECT policy hides is not an error** — RLS filters it, so it removes zero rows and reports success, the comment above it ("put both back to drafts") was false, and every later section would have run against a month that already held its one confirmed plan. Only INSERT and UPDATE raise on a hidden row, which is why the failure appeared on the statement *after* the DELETE. Fixed with `_mp_reset()` and an assertion, and the whole file was swept for the same shape — the sweep first run against the previous commit as a negative control, where it reports exactly that block and nothing else.

- **The RLS kit's plan-cache blind spot (branch `fix/rls-kit-plan-cache-blind-spot`, no migration), what to know:**

  **What it was.** PR #117 shipped `app_private.enforce_company_role_shape`, a SECURITY INVOKER trigger on `public.roles` calling a helper in `app_private`. `authenticated` holds no USAGE on that schema, so every company administrator creating a role got `42501 permission denied for schema app_private` — the feature was dead for every user. **The kit passed, twice**, with `matrix.sql` reporting `roles I` as `Y` for the admin principal.

  **Why.** Schema USAGE is checked when a statement is *parsed*, and plpgsql caches the parsed form of every static statement in a function body. `matrix.sql` runs every fixture as the owner before it probes any principal, so the owner — who does hold USAGE — parsed the trigger's call once, and every principal after it reused that plan. The owner bisected it in a throwaway kit database: owner INSERT succeeded, the same INSERT as `authenticated` succeeded, `DISCARD PLANS` then the same INSERT was refused 42501. Isolated on a cold session, `_mx_try` answered honestly — the warming is what lied.

  **What is masked is a table, not a sentence, and it is in CLAUDE.md.** Measured on PostgreSQL 17.6: schema `USAGE` **is** masked; `EXECUTE` on a **plpgsql** function is **not**; `EXECUTE` on an **inlinable** `sql` function (`IMMUTABLE`, no `SET`) **is**, because the planner folds the call away; `EXECUTE` on a `sql` function **with a `SET` clause** is **not**; a table privilege is **not**; RLS is **not**. So whether a callee is masked depends on how it is *declared*, which nobody would guess.

  **And `matrix.sql`'s `roles I` move is measured but unexplained, which is recorded rather than smoothed over.** No mechanism accounts for it: not a cached EXECUTE check (F1 and F3 both refuse with the cache warm), and not inlining (F3 is that cell's exact callee shape and still refuses). The cell's comment lists what each probe rules out, and the un-probed differences as **leads, not explanations** — a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger, a callee reading an RLS table, a table with policies and a WITH CHECK that can refuse, a trigger function in `app_private`.

  **The reason no mechanism is written there is worth more than the answer.** On this branch a reading of PostgreSQL's source has been wrong **three times, in both directions**, each reading as confirmed at the time: EXECUTE placed at executor init; the probe written from that reading using an inlinable callee, which could not have detected anything either way and pointed the same way — the worst case, because the wrong answer looked confirmed; and then inlining as the account of `roles I`, for a helper carrying a `SET` clause. **Do not write a fourth confident paragraph.**

  **The mask is not the owner's and not the kit's — step H crossed, both halves, 26.09.2026.** Two ordinary roles on one session, neither a superuser nor `bypassrls`: the one holding the privilege plans the statement, the one without it executes and **is allowed**, for schema `USAGE` and for an inlinable `sql` callee alike. It is a general property of a shared plan cache, and PostgREST pools one backend across roles. Raising the question was worth it; scoping it out would have left it unknown.

  **Our production exposure is nil today, and that is measured read-only on production, not reasoned.** Both classes need a warmer holding the privilege and a reader without it on one backend, and PostgREST switches between exactly three roles. Of the schemas one API role may use and another may not there is **one**, `vault` — no secrets in it, and named nowhere in `supabase/migrations`, `src` or `supabase/functions`. Of inlinable `sql` functions (no `SET` clause) granted unevenly across the three there are **none**. And all three lack `USAGE` on `app_private`, so the boundary this work is about cannot be warmed by any pooled role: only the owner could, and the owner never runs on PostgREST's pool. **The two queries are in CLAUDE.md beside the taxonomy, ready to paste.**

  **It is a standing property, not a fixed bug.** A migration that grants one API role `USAGE` on a schema another lacks, or adds an unevenly granted inlinable `sql` function, reopens it — re-run both queries when either happens. No third assertion was added, because the two readers already fail on exactly the cases that could bite our code: the mask needs the *reader* role to lack a privilege on something a reachable invoker function touches, which is what both readers test on the shape. A schema that is unevenly usable but that nothing reachable names cannot be masked; the moment something names it, the first reader fails.

  **The EXECUTE class fails now, with one recorded exemption.** `_invoker_calls_unexecutable_function()` is the mirror rule, and its count on a clean head was measured as **exactly one row** — `anon` reaching `role_name_is_system_reserved` through `enforce_company_role_shape` — with neither of its two tolerated noise sources firing. It shipped as a warning for one release while that number was unknown and is now a failure, with that case as a keyed exemption: role, invoker function and callee all three, so it cannot absorb a different defect, and its invalidation condition beside it — **if `roles_insert` ever admits `anon`, the premise is gone** and the grant is reconsidered rather than the line extended. An exemption matching nothing any more also fails, so a stale line cannot excuse something else.

  **Cost of the guard: a bound, not a delta.** 5720 probes, the whole table section in **13–15 s** with the guard on, two runs, 26.09.2026. `matrix.uncache = 'off'` aborts in the plan-cache section by design, so there is no clean before/after; at 13–15 s it is not worth optimising.

  **The one thing to remember when writing a new kit file:** a probe run after the owner has run the same code path is not a probe. And measure the mechanism — do not read it off the PostgreSQL source. This branch did the latter twice and was wrong both times, in the same direction, which made the wrong answer look confirmed.

- **The Abrechnung on a schedule, and the manager's summary (branch `feat/abrechnung-schedule`, migration `20261003120000`), what to know:**

  **What the owner asked for (03.10.2026).** The administrator picks a weekday and a time per company and the Abrechnung goes to each driver automatically ("Monday 8:00"), and one more mail goes to the manager with every driver and their Abrechnung. Both are switches on one card, **Einstellungen -> Abrechnung per E-Mail** (`src/components/AbrechnungScheduleCard.tsx`), its own save, outside the settings form.

  **What ships.** `public.abrechnung_schedules` (one row per company: `driver_mail_enabled`, `manager_summary_enabled`, `weekday` 0-6 with **0 = Sunday**, `send_time` Europe/Berlin, `armed_at`) and `public.abrechnung_deliveries` (the record and the dedupe rule); four `service_role`-only RPCs (`abrechnung_schedule_candidates`, `abrechnung_week_figures`, `abrechnung_delivery_claim`, `abrechnung_delivery_settle`); the pure decision in `supabase/functions/_shared/abrechnung-schedule-core.ts`; two renderers in `_shared/abrechnung-mail.ts` (the driver mail gained `imageAttached`, default true, so the screen's send is byte-identical; the summary is new); the Deno I/O in `_shared/abrechnung-delivery.ts`. **It is a second job of `notify-expiries`, not a new function:** the same caller (a cron line holding the service key), so no line in `PUBLIC_FUNCTIONS`/`FUNCTION_ENV` or the compose file. `{"job":"abrechnung"}` selects it; a body with no `job` is the TUEV / trial job, so the installed notification line is unchanged; an unknown `job` answers 400 and sends nothing.

  **Rules that are easy to break.**
  - **The week mailed is the most recently completed one**, named by its Monday, whatever weekday is chosen (a Sunday-evening schedule does not mail the week still running). That Monday is the dedupe key: `UNIQUE (company_id, kind, target_id, period_key)`, claimed **before** the send and never retried, so a crash loses a mail and never repeats one, and two overlapping runs or a line installed twice are safe.
  - **An occurrence is sent only inside 12 hours after its moment** (`GRACE_MINUTES`), never later. A cron that was down for a day sends nothing late. So the line may run every ten minutes and need not be exact.
  - **`armed_at` is stamped by a trigger whenever a switch, the weekday or the time changes**, and only an occurrence after it is sent. Switching the schedule on at Tuesday noon for "Monday 08:00" does not send last Monday's mail.
  - **A driver's address is read in SQL from `drivers`**, matched to the Abrechnung row's name like `driverRecipients.ts` (case and inner spacing only). A name two drivers share is **skipped, never guessed**; a driver who is not `active` or has no address is skipped; the report carries counts, never a name or an address.
  - **The managers are `selectActivationCodeRecipients`' company branch** (`loadRecipients` in `notification-delivery.ts`, now exported), the same rule the notifications use.
  - **The figures are computed a second time** (`abrechnung_week_figures`), because the screen reads `abrechnung_weekly` under the caller's RLS and a cron line has no caller. The parity with the screen is stated in the migration header, not proved by a kit file: **before arming, compare one week's dry run with the Abrechnung screen for the same week on staging.**
  - **The scheduled driver mail does not say the card is attached**, because the PNG is drawn in the browser. No figure is in either subject, and neither mail calls itself a Rechnung, Quittung or Lohnabrechnung.
  - A company whose subscription is not live and whose trial is not running is not mailed (the boundary of `subscriptionIsActive()`), and `approval_status` / `status` are the notification job's.

  **The dry run, to take first. Nothing is claimed, sent or written.**

  ```bash
  ssh elprofessor 'cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && curl -fsS -X POST \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $(tr -d "\r" < .env | sed -n "s/^SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=//p")" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"job\":\"abrechnung\",\"mode\":\"dry_run\"}" \
    https://api-staging.elprofessor.eu/functions/v1/notify-expiries' | python3 -m json.tool
  ```

  A dry run is empty outside a schedule's window (`companies_due: 0`), which is correct. To see it plan something, an administrator on an `@example.com` test company switches both boxes on for a time a few minutes ahead, and the dry run is taken inside the window. Read `companies_due` and the `items`: per company the week (`week_monday`), `driver_rows`, the driver counts (`planned`, `no_address`, `ambiguous`, `already_claimed`) and the manager summary outcome (`planned`, `no_rows`, `no_recipients`, ...). No address and no name appears in it.

  **The host line, to add only after the owner has read a dry run and said yes. Staging first.** A new file `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-abrechnung` (root, mode 0644), modelled on `el-professor-notifications`. Every ten minutes; the job itself decides in Europe/Berlin time whether anything is due, so the host's time zone does not matter.

  ```
  */10 * * * *  root cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && tr -d '\r' < .env | grep -qx 'PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility-staging' && flock -n /run/lock/el-professor-abrechnung-staging.lock curl -fsS -m 300 -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $(tr -d '\r' < /srv/PRO-staging/deploy/.env | sed -n 's/^SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=//p')" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"job":"abrechnung","mode":"send"}' https://api-staging.elprofessor.eu/functions/v1/notify-expiries 2>&1 | logger -t el-professor-abrechnung
  ```

  The production line is the same with `/srv/PRO`, `PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility`, `el-professor-abrechnung-production.lock` and `https://api.elprofessor.eu`. **A body that does not parse is a dry run**, so a mistyped line mails nobody. Read the runs with `journalctl -t el-professor-abrechnung`. **Staging mail reaches real people** (Mailpit relays all but `@example.com` through production's Resend): a staging company that switches this on mails its real drivers and administrators, and staging holds a copy of production's data. Do not arm the staging line without the owner's explicit yes on that point. Nothing is sent until a line is installed; this is a host step like the notifications.

  **What was not run.** The migration and the RLS kit (VPS, yours): `tests/rls/matrix.sql` will report `abrechnung_schedules` and `abrechnung_deliveries` as UNCOVERED (a warning) and the four RPCs; their lines are not added because `tests/rls/` was outside this slice. The two tables carry both context triggers, so `platform-contexts.sql` section 14 and `support-write-audit.sql` section 1 should pass. A kit file for the parity of `abrechnung_week_figures` with `abrechnung_weekly` is the follow-up worth writing.

- **Notifications — the feature the settings screen never had (branch `feat/notifications`, migration `20260924100000`), what to know:**

  **What was missing.** `NotificationsPage` let a company switch four types on and off and wrote them to `notification_settings`, and **no other code in the repository read that table**. No scheduler, no sender, no template: a TÜV expiry produced nothing. CLAUDE.md carried it under *Known defects*; that bullet is rewritten in this PR.

  **What ships.** `public.notification_deliveries` (the record of what was sent, and the dedupe rule), three `service_role`-only RPCs (`notification_candidates`, `notification_delivery_claim`, `notification_delivery_settle`), the pure decision in `supabase/functions/_shared/notification-core.ts`, four fixed German mails in `_shared/notification-mail.ts`, the Deno I/O in `_shared/notification-delivery.ts`, and the edge function `notify-expiries`. `tests/rls/notifications.sql` and 60 vitest cases cover it.

  **Nothing sends until a cron line is installed**, and that is the owner's call. Deploying the branch adds a function nobody calls.

  **The three decisions, made rather than assumed:**

  1. **"Soon" is 60 days for the TÜV and 7 for the trial.** Neither is new: `getTuevStatus()` in `src/types/car.ts` already turns the Fahrzeuge badge amber at 60 days, `TrialBanner` at `days <= 7`, and `20260502121504`'s own header documents both numbers for these very types. The mail and the screen must not disagree about the same car on the same day. They live once, as `TUEV_SOON_DAYS` and `TRIAL_SOON_DAYS`, and are *passed into* the SQL rather than restated there.
  2. **A re-notification is correct exactly when the underlying fact changes, and never because a night went by.** The dedupe key is `(company, type, target, period_key)`, and `period_key` is the date the notification is *about* — the car's `tuev_gueltig_bis`, or the company's `trial_end`. So a TÜV that expires and is never renewed is mailed once, not nightly; a TÜV that is renewed gets a new date, which is a new occurrence, so the company is warned again 60 days before it and told again if it lapses again; the warning and the expiry are separate occurrences of the same date, so a car warned at 60 days is still told on the day it passes; an extended trial is a new occurrence. It is a UNIQUE constraint, not a query, so two runs overlapping on one night cannot both claim.
  3. **A lapsed company is mailed, and a blocked one is not.** `approval_status = 'approved'` and `status IN ('active','trial')` are applied in SQL: a company that was never approved, was rejected, or was suspended, archived or deleted gets nothing, because suspension is an administrative block and mailing through it would undo it. The subscription terms of `get_active_company_id()` are deliberately **not** applied at that level — `trial_abgelaufen` is by definition a notification to a company whose access has just lapsed, and `trial_bald_ablaufend` is sent to a company in its trial, whose `subscription_status` is `'inactive'` because that is what signup writes. Instead both trial types require `NOT subscriptionIsActive(…)` in `notification-core.ts`. That single term is the paying-customer case.

  **The paying-customer case is the one that had to be got right.** `companies.status` stays `'trial'` for ever — signup writes it, `redeem_activation_code` writes the five `subscription_*` columns and never `status`. A job that judged the trial by `trial_end` alone would mail *"Ihre Testphase läuft bald ab"* to a customer who is paying. The browser has made that mistake twice (the customer's overview, the platform dashboard). `src/utils/notificationSelection.parity.test.ts` imports `subscriptionIsActive` from **both** `src/utils/planState.ts` and `_shared/notification-core.ts` and pins them to the same answers over twelve cases, and the same file walks the TÜV window against `getTuevStatus()` day by day.
