A negative control was run: flipping the job's NaN branch fails two rows.

  **Claim before send, and never retry.** The job inserts the delivery row (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), then sends, then settles. If the worker dies in between, the notification is lost and never repeated — deliberately, because a nightly duplicate is the worse failure. A lost one is visible as `mail_sent IS NULL`; a failed send as `mail_sent = false`. A company whose *recipients* could not be looked up is skipped entirely and nothing is claimed for it, so a transient GoTrue failure does not burn a TÜV warning.

  **Who receives it:** `selectActivationCodeRecipients`' company branch, called with no target address — the company's active `admin` profiles that are not platform staff, with a confirmed, undeleted, unbanned account, and nobody when there are none. The rule is not restated; it is one function.

  **The wording is fixed German in `_shared/notification-mail.ts` and is not editable from the panel.** Making it editable means a `mail.template.*` settings key, and a key means restating **`app_private.platform_settings_problem`** with every existing branch carried over character for character, plus a self-check re-asserting what the old branches refuse. That is a slice of its own. The five templates `email-layout.test.ts` pins to their exact bytes are untouched; the new mails go through the same layout.

  **A new edge function, and why it is not a route.** `notify-expiries` costs its line in `FUNCTION_ENV` (`supabase/functions/main/index.ts`) and its comment in `deploy/docker-compose.yml`; both are in this PR. It is not a route on `platform-staff` or `manage-users` because its caller is a **cron line holding the service key**, which has no `auth.uid()` — every platform RPC would refuse it — so a route would have meant adding a branch that skips those functions' own authorization. It is deliberately **not** in `PUBLIC_FUNCTIONS`, and because the router's check only proves a token this stack signed (**every signed-in customer holds one**), the function compares the bearer against `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in constant time itself.

  **The dry run, to take first — staging, at a quiet moment. Nothing is written and nothing is sent.** It reads the same rows, makes the same decision and builds the same mails, so a mail that cannot be rendered fails here.

  ```bash
  ssh elprofessor 'cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && curl -fsS -X POST \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $(tr -d "\r" < .env | sed -n "s/^SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=//p")" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"mode\":\"dry_run\"}" \
    https://api-staging.elprofessor.eu/functions/v1/notify-expiries' | python3 -m json.tool
  ```

  Read `companies_scanned`, `planned`, and the `items` array — each line names the company, the type, the target, the period key and the days left, and carries **no address**. Send the owner those numbers before anything is armed. `{"mode":"send"}` is the real run; **a call with no body, or a body that does not parse, is a dry run**, so a mistyped cron line cannot mail a customer.

  **The host lines, to add only once the owner has seen the dry run's numbers. Staging first, then production.** A new file, `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-notifications` (root, mode 0644). 07:10 and 07:40 Europe/Berlin, after the night's other jobs and early enough that a workshop appointment can still be made that day:

  ```
  10 7 * * *  root cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && tr -d '\r' < .env | grep -qx 'PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility-staging' && flock -n /run/lock/el-professor-notify-staging.lock curl -fsS -m 300 -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $(tr -d '\r' < /srv/PRO-staging/deploy/.env | sed -n 's/^SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=//p')" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"mode":"send"}' https://api-staging.elprofessor.eu/functions/v1/notify-expiries 2>&1 | logger -t el-professor-notify
  40 7 * * *  root cd /srv/PRO/deploy && tr -d '\r' < .env | grep -qx 'PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility' && flock -n /run/lock/el-professor-notify-production.lock curl -fsS -m 300 -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $(tr -d '\r' < /srv/PRO/deploy/.env | sed -n 's/^SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=//p')" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"mode":"send"}' https://api.elprofessor.eu/functions/v1/notify-expiries 2>&1 | logger -t el-professor-notify
  ```

  Each run logs one JSON line; read them with `journalctl -t el-professor-notify`. **Remember what staging mail is since 14.09.2026:** Mailpit relays everything but `@example.com` through production's Resend account, so a staging run mails **real customers** — staging holds a copy of production's data, including real admin addresses. Do not arm the staging line without the owner's explicit yes on that specific point; the dry run is safe and is the thing to show him. Once installed, list the file under *Installed on the host by hand*.

  **One thing the kit caught, and it is worth knowing why.** The first version of the migration gave `notification_deliveries` **neither** context trigger, reasoning that no client can write it — which is true, and is why the job pays nothing for them. `tests/rls/platform-contexts.sql` section 14 refused it: the rule is *every table whose policies name `get_active_company_id()` carries `refuse_read_only_context_write`*, keyed on the **policies**, not on today's grants. That is deliberate — the next person to add an INSERT policy here (the "zuletzt gemeldet" panel below is the obvious candidate) will not re-derive the argument, and without the triggers their write would pass in a view context and leave no audit row in a support session. Both are installed now, because `tests/rls/support-write-audit.sql` section 1 fails a table carrying one without the other in either direction. The alternative — dropping `get_active_company_id()` from the SELECT policy to leave the rule's scope — was rejected: it is only right if a company should never read its own delivery history, and the opposite is the likely future. Section 4b of `tests/rls/notifications.sql` is the new check that the pair does **not** arm for the job: `service_role`'s JWT carries no `sub`, so the refusal trigger returns on its first branch, and without that check the pair would have put one audit row on every notification, every night, with no actor.

  **Two things left over, both small:** nothing in the product *reads* `notification_deliveries` yet (the SELECT policy is the tenant shape, so a "zuletzt gemeldet" panel on the Notifications screen is a UI-only follow-up — and it now has both triggers waiting for it), and `cars.status` is deliberately not filtered on — the column exists in production but has no `CREATE TABLE` in the migration chain, so its domain is unknown; the dedupe bounds an out-of-service car to one mail per expiry date.

- **D14 answered — the money moves, the company list stays (branch `phase3/d14-billing-audited-read`, migration `20260924110000`), what to know:**

  **The owner's decision, 24.09.2026, the recommended middle path.** `companies` stays readable on `companies.read` — it is the list an operator picks a company from in order to open a context on it, so requiring a context to read it is circular, and an audited model for it would write one `read` row every time the panel's home is opened. `company_subscriptions`, `subscription_invoices`, `subscription_payment_audit` and `subscription_coverage` move behind two audited read models, `platform_invoices_list` and `platform_subscriptions_list`, in `20260916140000`'s shape. Their SELECT is the tenant term alone now, which for a company-less staff account is the open context's company.

  **Which capability, and what it costs.** **`billing.manage`** — owner and billing, not `support` and not `read_only`. It is already the capability that *writes* all four tables, and there is no `billing.read` in `app_private.platform_capability_matrix()`; inventing one would decide D2 and D3 by accident. So a `support` or `read_only` account **keeps** the company list and every company's own `status`, `approval_status`, `plan`, `subscription_status`, expiry and trial dates — enough to answer "is this customer's access live, and until when" — and **loses** the invoice list, the amounts, the payment trail and the coverage periods. Inside an open context it reads that company's rows again, like every other table since 3.4a. `AdminSidebar` asks for `billing.manage` on `/admin/billing`, and the page says so rather than showing an empty table.

  The asymmetry this closes, in one sentence: before it, any active staff role — `read_only` included — read every customer's invoices unrecorded, while the same account needed an audited call to see one driver's name.

  **A live defect came out of it, and it is the reason to read the migration header.** The screen asked PostgREST for `companies(name, email, street, house_number, postal_code, city)`. **`public.companies` has no `street` and no `house_number`** — it carries `address`, `city`, `postal_code`, `country`, which is what `platform_company_create` writes and what `CompaniesPage` edits; those two names belong to `company_settings` (`20260402053937`). PostgREST answers that select `42703`, and the page reads `if (invRes.data) setInvoices(...)`, which is `null` on an error — so **the Rechnungen tab has shown "Keine Rechnungen" and five of its six figures as zero for as long as the query has read that way, since the baseline commit**, and the detail dialog's address row never rendered. The read model carries `companies.address` and composes it above `postal_code city`. Worth noting *why* it surfaced: the first draft of the migration copied the two names into the read model, where self-check 6 EXPLAINs the statement and would have failed the release — a silent 400 in a browser became a refused migration.

  **Four cells in `matrix.sql` moved that are not about reading**, and the reason is mechanical rather than a policy change: `company_subscriptions` and `subscription_invoices` keep write policies that name `billing.manage` with no company term, but the matrix's U and D probes read a column in their `WHERE`, so PostgreSQL applies the SELECT policies to the existing row first, and a row the SELECT policy hides cannot be written. `tenant-state.sql` already recorded that rule as measured behaviour for `user_profiles` in 3.4a. **Nothing in the product notices:** every billing write is one of the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs of `20260916130000`, which run as their owner and never through these policies.

  **`generate_invoice_number` had to move with them or invoicing would have broken silently.** It was SECURITY INVOKER and read `subscription_invoices` under the caller's own RLS. Narrowing the policy would have left it answering `INV-<year>-0001` for every call; `invoice_number` is UNIQUE, so the insert fails `23505`, which `GenerateInvoicesModal` maps to "skipped" — every company reported as skipped, nothing created, no error anywhere. It is a definer behind `billing.manage` now and answers NULL to anyone else, which the screen already reports per company.

  **Recorded, not closed:** a *view* context still edits and deletes the supported company's subscription and invoice rows. Those two tables carry no `refuse_read_only_context_write` — they are the account shell, not one of 3.4a's 36 tenant tables — and D14 is a decision about who may *read* the money. It is strictly tighter than before, where both context modes wrote *either* company's rows. Narrowing their writes to the support session, the way 3.13a narrowed `company_settings` and `user_profiles`, is its own slice.

  **What is deliberately not touched:** `companies` (the decision above), the four `codes.manage` reads (an activation code is not a customer's money), `platform_invoice_seller()` (it reads the `seller` *setting*, not a billing table, and stays on `companies.read` so billing and support can build an invoice PDF without holding `settings.read`), and the platform catalogues. `subscription_payment_audit` and `subscription_coverage` get **no** read model: nothing in the product reads either across companies, and a paged model for a list no screen shows would be code with no caller.

  **No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump, and the rollback stated honestly:** the previous release's `SubscriptionBillingPage` reads these tables directly, so after a rollback a platform account sees an empty billing screen until the release is applied again. Nothing is lost and nothing wrong is written. A company reading its own billing rows, the activation flow and every write RPC are unaffected in both directions.

  **One bounded list, named rather than hidden.** The company filter on the invoice tab is one page of `platform_companies_list` — the first 100 companies by name. Paging it would be several audit rows per screen open for a convenience, and a company past the hundredth is still reachable, because the search box filters on the company name in SQL. Production has 19 companies. Give the picker a search of its own before that grows past a hundred.

  **The kit ran on `2f44600` and failed exactly one line, and which one is worth knowing.** Section 12g asserted the paging order through `v_p1 -> 0` — but the read model answers an **object**, and `jsonb -> integer` is the *array* accessor, so every one of those four comparisons was `NULL`. `_rm_ok` refuses `NULL` on the principle that a check which could not be evaluated proved nothing, **and that principle is the only reason this surfaced instead of sitting green for ever.** Do not relax it the next time a `NULL` is inconvenient. The assertion immediately before it passed because it reads `v_p1 -> 'items'`, through the key.

  **The order itself was never wrong.** `ORDER BY invoice_year DESC, invoice_month DESC, created_at DESC, id` is **total**, because `id` is the primary key — so no two rows tie on all four legs and page 2 cannot repeat a row from page 1. The corrected assertions test the whole sequence in one call, name `id` as the leg that makes the order total, and page the list two at a time to prove every row appears exactly once. **`created_at DESC` is the one leg the fixture cannot exercise** (one multi-row INSERT, one transaction timestamp — measured: 1 distinct value across all seven rows), and the comment says so at the line, because an untested `ORDER BY` leg is what someone later deletes as redundant.

  **All three were run before pushing, against the migration's own statement loaded verbatim, on `supabase/postgres:17.6.1.170` — the kit's image, PostgreSQL 17.6** — in a throwaway container with a seven-row stand-in for the fixture, because new SQL whose whole value is that it fails loudly is a claim until it has failed once. Three negative controls, each measured rather than asserted: the leading `ORDER BY` legs reversed makes the sequence `{4,5,6,7,8,9,9}` and fails assertion 1; ignoring the offset makes the four pages collect **8 rows, 2 distinct** and fails assertion 3 — that is the page-2-repeats-page-1 failure, caught; and the old `v_p1 -> 0` wording evaluates to `NULL`, not `false`, and fails on the `NULL`. The scaffolding is deliberately not committed — it answers a one-off question, unlike `tests/tools/plan-cache-repro.sql`, which answers a recurring one.

  **The screen was rendered, and not in the design harness.** `design-harness/` fakes the tenant shell and the signed-out screens; nothing in it renders `AdminLayout`, so there is no harness path for a panel page. `src/pages/SubscriptionBillingPage.render.test.ts` renders the page for real with `react-test-renderer`, the way `SuperAdminContext.test.ts` renders its provider, and covers the refusal, a refused read, a failed read, the flat columns, the six figures and the pager. Two negative controls were run against it. Building it found one thing worth keeping in mind for the next such test: the page's `loadData` is a `useCallback` over `[…, t]`, so a mocked `useTranslation` that returns a fresh `t` per render makes the load effect fire on every render and the test hangs. The real `LanguageContext` memoises its value.

- **Slice 3.13b — staff reads and the legacy routes (branch `phase3/staff-reads-and-legacy-routes-3-13b`), what to know:**

  **The inventory came first, and it moved the slice.** 3.13b was written to close "the direct read" — the concession of 16.09.2026 by which every staff role read a customer's Fahrtenbuch, planning, historical and ledger rows through `platform_reads_company_data()`. **`20260917140000` (plan 3.4a) had already closed it**: both seams are `SELECT false`, and `user_profiles_select`, `roles_select` and `role_permissions_select` read the same false seam, so a platform account reaches those tables only inside an open context. No `HOLE[3.4]` cell was left in `matrix.sql`. CLAUDE.md still described the old state in five places and is corrected in this PR.

  | What a platform account reads | How, today |
  |---|---|
  | every business table, `user_profiles`, `roles`, `role_permissions` | the open context only (`platform_reads_company_data()` = false since 3.4a) |
  | `company_settings` | **the open context only, since `20260923100000` (this slice)** |
  | `company_subscriptions`, `subscription_invoices`, `subscription_payment_audit`, `subscription_coverage` | **the open context, or the two audited read models on `billing.manage`, since `20260924110000` (D14 answered 24.09.2026)** |
  | `companies` | `companies.read`, no company term — **D14 left this one open on purpose**: the list is how a company is chosen to open a context on |
  | `activation_codes`, `activation_code_attempts`, `discount_codes`, `discount_code_redemptions` (platform policy) | `codes.manage`, no company term — not a customer's money, and D14 did not cover it |
  | `subscription_plans`, `document_categories`, `referrals`, `subscription_admin_actions` | platform catalogues, not a company's data |

  **What this slice closes: `company_settings`.** Its SELECT carried `OR platform_can('companies.read')`, which all four staff roles hold, so a `read_only` platform account read every customer's legal name, managing director, VAT and tax number, commercial register, bank name, IBAN, BIC and account holder with no context, no reason and no audit row — wider than `platform_drivers_list` allows for a driver's name, and left behind by `20260921110000`, which moved this table's writes onto the support session and did not touch the read. The predicate is the tenant term alone now; `get_user_company_id()` already answers the open context's company for a company-less staff account, so writing the context helper beside it would say the same thing twice.

  **The one panel reader moved with it.** `SuperAdminDashboard` read `company_settings` across every company for one number, `drivers_per_car_limit`. It is now a key of `platform_companies_list` — one grouped read restricted to the page, and **the only column of that table the model may carry** (self-check 5 and `tests/rls/platform-readmodels.sql`). It is nullable: a company with no settings row stays out of the map and the screen applies its own default of 4, rather than showing an allowance of zero.

  **Per-page inventory of how the panel reads now** (`/admin`):

  | Page | Reads |
  |---|---|
  | `SuperAdminDashboard` | `companies` direct (D14 leaves the list open); counts **and now the driver allowance** from `platform_companies_list`; writes through the `platform_company_*` RPCs |
  | `CompaniesPage` | `companies` direct (D14 leaves the list open) and a direct `DELETE` (D13, deliberate); the user drawer through `platform_users_list`; writes through RPCs; opens its own support session for `manage-users` writes (3.13a) |
  | `SubscriptionBillingPage` | `subscription_plans` direct (a platform catalogue); the invoices and the subscriptions through `platform_invoices_list` and `platform_subscriptions_list`, paged and audited, on `billing.manage` (D14, `20260924110000`); the company picker through `platform_companies_list`; the seller through `platform_invoice_seller` |
  | `PlatformDirectoriesPage` | read models only |
  | `PlatformAuditPage` | `platform_audit_list` + `platform_companies_list` |
  | `PlatformSettingsPage` | settings RPCs; its `AnnouncementsEditor` child reads `companies` for the audience picker (open by D14) |
  | `PlatformTeamPage` | `platform_staff_list` + the staff RPCs |
  | `AbnahmeDiagnosticPage` | `company_planning_runs` and `fahrtenbuch_trips` direct, on a pinned run id — **already context-bound since 3.4a**, and the page says so on screen |
  | `AccountPage` at `/admin/konto` | its own row only |

  Four smaller components read `companies` for a name picker, and D14 leaves every one of them as it is: `DiscountCodesManagement` (:395), `ActivationCodesManagement` (:376, :394), `AssignSubscriptionModal` (:36) and `settings/AnnouncementsEditor` (:130).

  **`/super-admin`: the redirects stay, the links are gone.** There were never any legacy *pages* — the panel has always been registered once, under `/admin`, and `/super-admin/*` has only ever been two redirects (`LegacyAdminRedirect` signed in, `PlatformSignInRedirect` signed out). What existed were six in-app links still naming it, each a round trip and a history entry for nothing: the overview card, the sidebar's way back, the user menu's exit and four `/super-admin/companies` links on the dashboard. All name `ADMIN_BASE` now. The routes, `LEGACY_ADMIN_BASE`, `legacyAdminTarget()` and the e2e spec are kept for bookmarks, and `rememberedPanelPath()` is untouched — `platformReturnPath()` only ever accepted `/admin…`, so it was already legacy-free. The reachability walk (CLAUDE.md's *Dead code*) against `origin/staging` and against the branch returns the same fifteen unreached modules under `src/`: nothing became unreachable, because nothing was deleted. Two of the dashboard's quick actions pointed at the tenant paths `/users` and `/settings`, which a company-less platform account cannot reach at all; they name the panel's own pages now.

  **`manage-users/list` read GoTrue's first 50 accounts of the whole project** and applied the company filter afterwards, so above 50 accounts on the installation a company's own users could be missing from its Benutzer screen — a 200, a short array, no warning, and `auditedPlatformRead` writing the truncated length into the audit row as if it were the whole answer. The company's profiles decide who is in the answer now; `resolveAuthAccounts()` (pure, in `_shared/manage-users-routes.ts`, the GoTrue call injected) pages the admin list at 200 a page and stops as soon as every id is resolved, so an ordinary installation still makes one request and no call issues one per account. A profile whose account is not found keeps its row and its own address; the audit row carries `incomplete`. Thirteen tests, including the exact regression.

  **Gates.** `npx vitest run` from Git Bash with python off `PATH`: 77 files, **2223 passed, 7 skipped**, 0 failed. `npm run build` passes; both ratchets are **exactly at baseline** (typecheck 213, styles 5004/2784 in 68 files) and neither needed updating. **The full RLS kit was run locally** in a throwaway `supabase/postgres:17.6.1.170` — every file passes, `matrix.sql` reports 275 lines with 0 differing, and section 15's 24 checks pass. It found three assertions that had to move (below), which is what it is for. No `deno check`: neither Deno nor a `denoland/deno` image was available on this machine, so `manage-users/index.ts` is reviewed but not type-checked in its own runtime — **run it before merging.**

  **Three assertions moved, each because its read half was `companies.read` answering.** `platform-contexts.sql` section 6 (a support session reading the other company's settings), `support-write-audit.sql` (the no-context owner) and `tenant-state.sql` (the same, widened here to all four staff roles with the company's own administrator beside it). `matrix.sql`'s `company_settings S` moved from `YY` to `..` for `super`, with an override row giving `Y.` in both context modes — reading the legal details is what a view context is for. Every line carries its reason.

  **Still open after this slice, and deliberately not taken here:** D14 was answered on 24.09.2026 and its entry is above; what stays is the write residual `matrix.sql` still tags `3.4c/3.13` — `companies` U and D are `YY` for a staff account with no context, through `companies.lifecycle` and `companies.hard_delete`, which name no company. `companies_delete` must stay (D13: `CompaniesPage` keeps its direct `DELETE`). `companies_update` has no caller in `src/` — every update goes through an audited `platform_company_*` RPC, which is SECURITY DEFINER and bypasses RLS — so dropping that policy is a small change, but it is a *write* narrowing outside this slice's subject and it could reach a host script this machine cannot test (`e2e/setup-staging.sh`). Left for the owner.
- **Fahrtenanalyse slices C and D — the settings tables and the screen (`fahrtenbuch/analysis-screen`, in review), what to know:**
  - **The boundary is the feature.** The screen shows and explains. No control on it reassigns a trip, changes a date, a time, a price or a currency, distributes work to the owner, or fits hours to a salary target. Its only three writes are the company's two analysis settings and, per driver and month, the reserve and the explicit budget confirmation. `design-harness/flows-analysis.mjs` ends **every** one of its 22 checks by asserting that nothing was sent against `fahrtenbuch_trips`, and that guard was proved to catch one: a deliberate trip write added to `confirmBudget` failed the run, and was reverted.
  - **Migration `20260922110000`** adds `public.trip_analysis_settings` (one row per company: `block_gap_minutes`, `travel_margin_minutes`, both nullable — **NULL means "not set", never a default**, because all three settings are the owner's open decisions) and `public.driver_month_budget` (one row per company, driver, year and month: `reserved_hours` nullable, plus the driver's `planning_min_salary` / `planning_max_salary` / `planning_hourly_rate` as they stood when the month was confirmed, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at` and a `recompute_log`). Both carry 20260917130000's business shape exactly — SELECT on `get_active_company_id()` or `platform_reads_company_data()`, every write the same predicate as USING **and** as WITH CHECK plus `is_company_admin()` — and both carry the two triggers every tenant table must carry, `refuse_read_only_context_write` (3.4a) and the gated `record_support_write` (3.4c). `anon` and the service role hold nothing; `authenticated` holds the four DML commands. Seven self-checks. **No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump:** two new tables and two new trigger functions, nothing existing changed, so the previous release runs beside them and simply never reads them.
  - **The pay snapshot is the database's, never the browser's.** `app_private.driver_month_budget_guard` runs BEFORE every INSERT and UPDATE: a row stays with its driver, company, year and month; the driver must belong to the row's company; writing `confirmed_at` is the *request* to confirm, and the trigger then copies the three figures from `public.drivers` as they are now and stamps the time and `auth.uid()`, discarding whatever the client sent; confirming an already-confirmed month appends the previous snapshot to `recompute_log`, as does a change of the reserve on a confirmed month; and a confirmation cannot be taken back by writing NULL — deleting the row is the explicit way to start the month over. One flow check asserts the browser sends no `planning_*`, no `confirmed_by` and no `recompute_log`.
  - **`tests/rls/trip-analysis-settings.sql`** (8 sections, the whole file one transaction rolled back at the end): shape and grants; tenant isolation, including that nobody writes into or moves a row to another company; the administrator gate (a viewer and an editor read but write nothing); company state (a suspended company's administrator reads and writes nothing, as on every business table); the whole budget rule above, with a later salary change proved not to move a confirmed month; a view context that reads the opened company, writes nothing and records nothing; a support session that writes as the company's administrator leaving exactly one audit row per row with the pay figures redacted, and whose write into another company is refused and unrecorded; and a platform owner with no context reaching none of it. `tests/rls/matrix.sql` gains both tables' S/I/U/D/M lines and their move entries. **The kit has not been run on this branch** — there is no Docker on this machine, so run `npm run test:rls` before merging.
  - **The screen is `/fahrtenanalyse`** (`src/pages/TripAnalysisPage.tsx`), on the design system from the start, reached from Dokumente → Fahrtenbuch (a tool card and a button in the sub-view) and from Monatsplanung's header. It is gated `PlanGate feature="dokumente"` on purpose: a new `FeatureKey` would need a migration seeding `plan_features` **and** `app_private.plan_feature_keys()`, and a feature the stored map does not name reads as premium for every plan.
  - **What it reads.** `start_at` / `end_at`, never `trip_date + start_time` — the Uber export dates a ride just after midnight a day early. The engine's `analysisTripFromRow` still derives the times for rows imported before slice A, so an older month analyses. The recorded working time is the saved Stundennachweis: `src/lib/tripAnalysisShifts.ts` turns `rows_json`'s `von`/`bis` pairs into absolute Europe/Berlin intervals, resolving the March gap with the offset in force before the switch and the October repeat to the earlier instant, counting both rather than dropping them. Trips are paged with `fetchAllPaginated`, and the row is read with `*` on purpose: naming a column PostgREST does not have fails the whole request, and this screen must work on either side of slice A.
  - **The month opens on the newest Fahrtenbuch, not on today**, and stops moving as soon as one is chosen by hand, so a reload of the imports cannot pull the reader off the month they are reading. The file is named on the screen whether or not there is a second one to pick.
  - **A failed read is never a zero.** One `loadFailed` state covers both loads; while it is set the screen shows the failure and a retry and nothing else — no counts, no table, no export. A month with no import is a separate state with a link to Dokumente, and settings nobody has decided compute nothing at all until the viewer chooses the clearly labelled 30-minute test value, which is never stored.
  - **Two things the first draft got wrong and the rendered shots caught.** A finding about the whole month (no shift records, a budget that cannot be computed) cites every trip of the driver; drawn as one span from the first to the last it painted every day in between, so days on which nothing happened appeared on the timeline at all. A finding now draws **one mark per piece of evidence**, at that trip's own interval, and a finding citing every analysed trip draws none (`tripAnalysisView.test.ts` pins both halves). And `travel_gap` covers every consecutive pair, so most of its findings are long gaps the engine simply could not check — it is labelled *Zeit zwischen zwei Fahrten*, and only a gap below the margin is described as short.
  - **The Excel report** (`src/utils/tripAnalysisReport.ts`) has four sheets, each opening with *Analyse — kein amtliches Dokument* and a line saying it does not replace the Fahrtenbuch. It satisfies no statutory form, so its headers follow the UI language; each trip's price goes into its cell exactly as stored, beside its currency, and nothing is rounded, converted or summed across currencies.
  - **A design-system finding this screen ran into, worth knowing before the next long table.** `DataTable`'s `scrollClassName` pins the header by putting a `max-height` on an `overflow-auto` container. With 118 findings (8265 px of table) Chrome 141 then reported `document.body.scrollHeight` as the correct 1237 while `document.documentElement.scrollHeight` was 8592 **and the page really scrolled that far** — seven screens of empty background under a table that was already clipped, the sticky header's layout overflow escaping the container. `[contain:paint]` beside the `max-h-` class fixes it (root back to 1237, header still pinned) and is why `TripAnalysisIssues` carries that class. **The same prop is used by `GehaltsquittungList`, `SalariesManagement` and the vehicles preview**, which have too few rows to show it today; folding the containment into `DataTable` itself would fix all four, and was deliberately not done here because three phase-5 batches are in flight against that file.
  - **Harness:** `design-harness/analysis/` (its own fixtures and routes), `design-harness/shots-analysis.mjs`, `design-harness/flows-analysis.mjs`, one registration line each in `design-harness/main.tsx` and `design-harness/fakes/supabase.ts`, and `upsert` added to the fake query builder, since the screen's two writes are upserts. `npm run harness:analysis:capture -- --set after` and `npm run harness:analysis:flows`. Shots in `Docs/design/phase5/analysis/after/`; there is no before set, because the screen is new.
  - **Not built, on purpose:** a routing service (release 2), so every travel check is *nicht prüfbar* rather than passed; any correction flow; and any way to plan future work. The reserve, the block threshold and the travel margin are still the owner's decisions — the database stores NULL for each until he answers, and the screen asks.

- **The three platform staff roles enabled (3.12b, in review), what to know:**
  - **Owner's decision, 23.09.2026.** `platform_staff_add` and `platform_staff_enable` refused every role but `owner` with reason `role_requires_3_3`. The first technical reason went with 3.3 (`20260917130000`, tenant policies onto capabilities) and the last with `20260920100000` (a view context writing the `documents` bucket); what remained was the product decision, which the owner has now taken.
  - **Migration `20260923110000` restates exactly those two bodies** — the `p_role <> 'owner'` block gone, `p_role` inserted, the `v_row.role <> 'owner'` block gone — and its self-check re-asserts every other rule of `20260916130010` from the catalogue, fragment by fragment: the `staff.manage` authorization and its `denied` cap, the four-role argument contract, `account_not_found` / `account_in_company` / `already_staff` / the `unique_violation` race, the row lock, plus the **untouched** `last_owner` branch and advisory lock in `platform_staff_disable`, the `platform_staff_keep_owner` trigger, `platform_rpc_authorize`'s `platform_staff_role_of` call (the password-session rule of `20260919130000`), and the capability matrix.
  - **The capability matrix did not move.** `support` still has no `companies.lifecycle` (D2 open) and `read_only` no `audit.read` (D3 open). `tests/rls/platform-authority.sql` is untouched.
  - **`role_requires_3_3` is now unreachable but not deleted.** `platform_rpc_response` and `platform_rpc_record` still carry a branch for it (their live definition is `20260917140000`'s); restating those two shared helpers to remove one dead line would risk dropping one of the twenty that are not dead, so `tests/rls/platform-rpcs.sql` section 5 asserts instead that nothing produces it. A role outside the four is `invalid`, as it always was.
  - **What each role can do, read off 3.13b's inventory above** — this branch was rebased onto it, so the entry above is the authority for *how* a platform account reads and this one only says who now holds what:

    | | `support` | `billing` | `read_only` |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | capabilities | `companies.read`, `business.read`, `support.session`, `company_users.manage` | `companies.read`, `billing.manage`, `pricing.manage`, `codes.manage`, `settings.read`, `metrics.read` | `companies.read`, `business.read`, `settings.read`, `metrics.read` |
    | the rows left on `companies.read` / `codes.manage` with no company term — since D14 was answered (24.09.2026) that is `companies` and the four code tables, not the billing ones | every company | every company | every company |
    | the audited directories (`platform_vehicles_list`, `_drivers_list`, `_users_list` — the last with every customer's user emails) | every company, one `read` row per call | — (no `business.read`) | every company, one `read` row per call |
    | every business table, `company_settings`, `user_profiles`, `roles`, `role_permissions`, storage | the open context only | **never** — cannot open a context at all | the open context only, `view` mode |
    | writes to a tenant | only in a **support** context, every row audited (3.4c) | none | **none, anywhere** |
    | platform write RPCs | none | billing (6) + codes (6) + `price_book` | none |
    | `staff.manage`, `companies.lifecycle`, `audit.read`, `settings.manage` | no | no | no |

    `metrics.read` grants nothing: no policy, RPC or route asks for it. `billing` **cannot open a context** (`platform_context_open` needs `business.read` for `view` and `support.session` for `support`, and it holds neither), so it reads zero tenant business rows and zero storage objects, ever.
  - **So the honest risk of this merge was D14, not the direct read.** 3.13b landed first and closed `company_settings`; what `support` and `read_only` still read across every company with no context was the set 3.13b's table tags **D14** — `companies`, the subscription tables, the code tables — plus the three audited directories, which read every company by design and leave a row each. Enabling the roles widened *who* can do that from "the owner, who could already do everything" to "three more kinds of account". **D14 was answered on 24.09.2026 and shrank it**: the four billing tables went behind `billing.manage`, which `support` and `read_only` do not hold, so what is left for them with no context is `companies` and the four code tables.
  - **A defect found while measuring, fixed in its own commit (`20260923110010`), droppable on its own.** `roles` and `role_permissions` carried a **write** branch naming `company_users.manage` with no company term — the two tables 3.13a skipped, and the ones 3.13b touched only on the read side. `matrix.sql` reads `..` for those cells and explains it by the row not being *visible*, which is true of a probe carrying a WHERE and only of that: a statement reading no column consults no SELECT policy, so `DELETE FROM public.roles;` is decided by `roles_delete`'s USING alone, which reduces to `is_system = false` — every custom role of every company, no context, no reason, no audit row, `role_permissions` following by cascade. It mattered only once `support` (which holds that capability) became grantable; an owner, the only role that existed, already holds `companies.hard_delete`. The fix is 3.13a's shape applied to those two tables, and loses no legitimate path: inside a support session the first disjunct already granted every one of these writes.
  - **Two `matrix.sql` cells moved with that fix, and the kit caught them:** `roles M` and `role_permissions M`, the `staff_view` column, `T.` → `..`. The mechanism is the one `matrix.sql` already states for the tenant tables and is a step earlier than "the policy refuses the move": the move probe takes the row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` before it issues any UPDATE, and that applies the UPDATE policy's USING. The old capability branch was true for a staff account in any context or none, so the row could be locked, the UPDATE was issued and `pin_roles_company_id` raised; the new branch is NULL in a view context, so there is nothing to lock, no UPDATE is ever issued, and **neither** trigger runs — not the pin one, and not `refuse_read_only_context_write`, which fires on a statement that is issued and none is. A tightening, not a lost refusal. Nothing else moves: I, U and D are unchanged in every column because that trigger fires on the statement and not on a row, and `activation_code_attempts` — the third table behind it — is not touched here and keeps `T.`.
  - **Not run here:** `npm run test:rls` (no Docker on this machine, and the VPS is off limits) and `deno check` (no Deno on this machine). Both are the coordinator's before merge. The kit run on `b498abb` stopped at `matrix.sql`, so `platform-rpcs.sql` §5 and §9 and `platform-contexts.sql` have not been exercised on this branch yet.

- **Fahrtenbuch analysis, slice B — the trip analysis engine (`src/utils/tripAnalysis/`, PR #103), what to know:**
  - The plan is `fahrtenbuch-analysis.md` (22.09.2026, Arabic) over the owner's spec `fahrtenbuch.md` v2. Slice A (import reliability, migration `20260922100000`) and slices C/D (settings table `20260922110000` and the Fahrtenanalyse screen) are built in parallel; D builds against `types.ts`, which is the contract. Nothing imports the folder yet, so it is not in the bundle, and the "every file under `src/` is reachable" claim holds again only once D lands.
  - Pure TypeScript: no database, no UI, no clock (`Date.now()` is never called), so the same input gives the same result. `analyzeTrips(input)` takes a period's trips, the recorded shifts and per-driver budget inputs, and returns blocks, time metrics, findings (`Issue`: type, severity, evidence ids, values, German explanation, confidence, status `passed` / `conflict` / `unverifiable`) and per-check tallies.
  - **It only reads and reports.** Every output trip is the input object itself; no field proposes a new driver, a time or a price; `boundary.test.ts` proves it per id on a deep-frozen input, and fails if the folder ever references `assignRegularDriversToVehicleBlocks`, `assignSurplusBlocksToInhaberWithoutTimeChange`, `shiftOverlappingInhaberBlocks`, `assignBlocksPhase3`, `repairFinalOverlaps` or `extendShiftsForSalary`.
  - The rules: blocks per driver, where a gap **greater** than `blockGapMinutes` splits and an equal one stays, measured from the running maximum end; a vehicle change splits (an unknown vehicle is a value of its own); a midnight trip stays whole. Overlaps are a sweep over half-open intervals, every pair, per driver across vehicles and per vehicle across drivers. Travel between consecutive trips asks an injected estimator: `required = estimate + travelMarginMinutes`; the default has no routing service, so every travel check is `unverifiable`, never `passed`, and a gap below the margin alone is only a weak signal. The budget is exact (BigInt decimals, never a float division) and floored to whole minutes: 1000 / 13.9 → 4316 min, reserve 5 → 4016; `205 / 12.5` is the case where a float floor loses a minute. An unset reserve is "not set", never 0. Past activity is compared with `hoursMax`; the reserve is not deducted from it.
  - Statuses: `completed` is analysed; `cancelled` is kept and counted but never in time metrics (the sample's 44 cancelled rows carry no start or end); `unknown` is kept and on the review list. Rows missing a driver, a start or an end, with an end before the start, or with a DST-ambiguous local time are kept, with their reasons, and listed for review.
  - `analysisTripFromRow` adapts today's `fahrtenbuch_trips` rows through `resolveActualRideWindow` and departs from it in three tested places: a row with only one full date-time keeps that side instead of falling back to `trip_date` (column D, the day-early bug); the `trip_date + HH:MM` fallback adds a calendar day, not 24 h — **the resolver's fixed 24 h is 80 minutes on the March DST Sunday, and that resolver still feeds `FinalizePlanningModal`'s checks for rows without source date-times**; equal HH:MM is a trip under a minute, not 24 hours. It reads slice A's `start_at` / `end_at` / `currency` when a row carries them. **Guessed:** slice A's explicit status column is read as `trip_status`; rename it in the adapter if A names it otherwise. For older rows only `abgeschlossen` and `completed` mean completed; anything else, an empty status included, is `unknown`.
  - Not built here: clipping to the month (a trip across the month boundary counts whole); name-keyed identities for drivers without `original_driver_id` (their rows go to review); reading shifts out of `saved_stundennachweise.rows_json` (the screen converts them to `AnalysisShift`).
  - Tests: 77 in 7 files. The 10,000-trip benchmark prints its measurement on every run; see the PR for the figures and the machine load they were taken under.
- **Company roles (slice B, PR #117, migration `20260923120000`, in review), what to know:**
  - **What it opens.** `roles_name_key` was `UNIQUE (name)` across every company, so two companies could not both call a role `Disponent`. That is why no company has ever created one: production holds four roles, all system rows, and all 29 accounts sit on one of them. It is replaced by two partial unique indexes — one per name over the system rows, one per company, case- and whitespace-insensitive.
  - **Why relaxing it alone would have been a privilege escalation.** `sync_user_profile_role` copies `roles.name` into `user_profiles.role`, and that text is read as an authority by `is_company_admin()`, by `AuthContext.isAdmin` and by the activation-code mail's recipient list. A company role named `admin` would therefore have made its holders administrators **by name**, with no permission row anywhere. `app_private.enforce_company_role_shape` refuses that, plus `is_system` on a company role and any later change of kind; `app_private.enforce_user_profile_role_scope` closes the other half, because the privilege guard says *who* may change `role_id` and never *what* it may be set to.
  - **Two defects the proof found, both fixed here.** (1) `enforce_company_role_shape` and `company_role_save` both run as the **caller**, and both read helpers that were in `app_private`, where `authenticated` holds neither USAGE nor EXECUTE. Measured in a throwaway PostgreSQL 16: a company admin's plain `INSERT INTO roles` died with `42501 permission denied for function role_name_is_system_reserved`, and the RPC caught that same 42501 as `insufficient_privilege` and answered `not_authorized` — the feature could not work for anybody and the message blamed the administrator's own role. Both helpers are in `public` now, granted to `authenticated` and `service_role`. (2) `can_edit` without `can_view` was not refused when `can_view` was simply **absent**: `v_entry -> 'can_view'` is SQL NULL, so the guard's condition was NULL and `IF NULL THEN` never fired. All four comparisons are `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` now.
  - **The screen half was WIP and had a third.** `RoleModal` handed `RolesManagement` a *list* where `permissionPayload()` expects a *map*, so every one of the sixteen lookups came back `undefined` and the role saved with **no permissions at all** — every holder locked out of every page, with a success toast. That was the one TypeScript error above the 213 baseline. `templates` was passed and not declared (the "Als Kopie von" picker is wired now), and a system role offered a Save the RPC answers `not_found` for (it opens read-only now).
  - **What the proof is.** `tests/rls/company-roles.sql`, 167 assertions, dry-run against a stand-in of the live schema with **eight negative controls, every one caught**. Section 2f builds the escalation with the trigger off and shows `is_company_admin()` answering true for an account with no permission row, rather than only asserting the refusal; section 4d separates the two mechanisms behind the one `not_authorized` (a `WITH CHECK` 42501 against a silent `rows 0`), each with the administrator's version beside it. `src/utils/rolePageKeys.test.ts` pins `role_page_keys()` against `PAGE_DEFINITIONS` the way `planFeatures.test.ts` pins the feature map, and it matters for the reason slice C wrote down: `company_role_save` replaces a role's permission rows wholesale, so a stored key this build does not know is dropped the first time anyone edits that role.
  - **matrix.sql will never speak for these two RPCs.** Its uncovered sweep filters on `p.prosecdef` and both are SECURITY INVOKER — correctly, since RLS is meant to stay the boundary. Both are written into the rpc section by hand, with a fixture role of their own, and the header now says that a silent run is not evidence that the next SECURITY INVOKER function is covered.
  - **Not run here:** the RLS kit needs Docker and this machine has none. `tests/rls/company-roles.sql` is new and `matrix.sql` moved; run `npm run test:rls` on the VPS before merging.

- **Permission coverage (slice C, PR #110, migration `20260923130000`, in review), what to know:**
  - **What it closes.** 3.3 gave every tenant table one shape and carried exactly the role gates `20260910120000` had written — ten tables and the `salary-receipts` bucket — and said so in its own table list: *"no role gate today, and none added: a viewer writes these exactly as it does now."* So a `viewer`, the role with `can_edit` on none of the sixteen pages, imported a Fahrtenbuch, applied and finalized a month's planning, edited a historical reconciliation, saved a Stundennachweis and reassigned a trip. `matrix.sql` recorded it under "Recorded without a plan step".
  - **The mapping is to existing page keys only, and that is a decision about the Rollen screen, not about taste.** `RolesManagement.handleSaveRole` saves a role by **deleting every `role_permissions` row it has** and re-inserting one per key in `PAGE_DEFINITIONS`. A key the screen does not know is therefore dropped the first time anyone edits that role — silently, per role. So a new page key seeded by a migration is a landmine, and the slice introduces none. If a future slice does want one (a *Monatsplanung* page is the obvious candidate), it has to ship the `PAGE_DEFINITIONS` entry and the two dictionary keys **in the same release**, and even then the delete-and-reinsert makes the ordering delicate.
  - **Who loses what.** `admin` and `supervisor`: nothing — both hold `can_edit` on all sixteen pages. `editor`: the `documents` group (Fahrtenbuch, planning, historical, Stundennachweis, templates, blocks, variables) and the five planning RPCs, because `editor` has held `can_edit = false` on Dokumente since 20260302065621. `viewer`: all of it, which is the hole. **The query in the PR is how that is measured on a copy of production** — it returns one row per active account affected, and an empty result is the proof.
  - **The RPCs are the other half.** `apply_monthly_planning_run`, `replace_monthly_planning_run`, `delete_or_archive_monthly_planning_run`, `purge_old_monthly_planning_runs` and `reassign_fahrtenbuch_trip` are SECURITY DEFINER and run past RLS, so gating the tables alone closes the door and leaves the window open. Each asks `current_role_permits('documents', true)` **after** its own `caller has no company` branch, so the state refusal still comes first. The three Gehaltsquittung RPCs already asked their page. The four Abrechnung readers need no gate and are not touched: they are SECURITY INVOKER, asserted in the kit rather than assumed.
  - **Two things a later migration must not undo.** Deleting an import or a trip stays `is_company_admin()` alone — replacing that with the page would *widen* it, because a supervisor holds `can_edit` everywhere. And the `imported_by = auth.uid()` / `changed_by = auth.uid()` INSERT clauses sit **beside** the page gate, not instead of it: a page says whether you may write, never as whom.
  - **Not run here:** the RLS kit needs Docker, and the development machine has none. `tests/rls/permission-coverage.sql` is new, and `matrix.sql`, `tenant-state.sql` and `platform-contexts.sql` moved with it; run `npm run test:rls` on the VPS before merging.
- **Fahrtenbuch import reliability (Fahrtenanalyse slice A, PR #104, in review), what to know:**
  - **Additive only.** Migration `20260922100000` adds to `fahrtenbuch_trips` `start_at`/`end_at`/`time_zone`/`time_source`, `trip_status`, `needs_review`/`review_reasons`, `currency`/`currency_source` and a derived `source_row_key` with `source_row_key_basis`; to `fahrtenbuch_imports` `file_sha256`. `trip_date`, `start_time`, `end_time`, `duration_minutes` and every amount keep their meaning. `protect_fahrtenbuch_immutable_fields` is restated with 20260606035519's 21 comparisons carried verbatim and the 11 new columns appended; the migration fires it on a scratch copy of the table (self-check 1b). No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump: every new column is nullable or defaulted, and the previous release's inserts still pass the checks.
  - **The parser is `src/utils/fahrtenbuchImportParser.ts`** (moved out of the modal, 41 tests). Its SQL twin is the `app_private.fahrtenbuch_*` helpers; the parser test reads the migration and fails when the token lists, reject fragments, timestamp patterns, review reasons, time sources or statuses part. On the August 2026 sample the two derived identical `start_at`/`end_at`/`time_source` for all 2209 rows.
  - **Dedup:** the file's SHA-256 is unique per company (`fahrtenbuch_imports_company_file_sha256_key`) and immutable (`fahrtenbuch_imports_keep_file_sha256`); the ride key is unique per company where set (`fahrtenbuch_trips_company_source_row_key`). The key is B + driver + plate when the order-transmission column exists, else start + driver + plate — B alone would refuse the second of two rides sharing a second. Rows stored before the migration have no key, so the index cannot refuse a re-import of an *old* file's rides; the file hash cannot either (old imports have none). Replacing an import with the identical file means deleting it first.
  - **Behaviour the legacy screens now see:** incomplete rows are stored (flagged) instead of dropped, so a month with a ride lacking a start or end now fails `FinalizePlanningModal`'s pre-flight until someone deals with it; an `unknown`-status ride has `is_canceled = false`, so planning treats it as a ride. `reassign_fahrtenbuch_trip` cannot reassign a ride without times (its audit row needs them) — that was already true of cancelled rows.
  - **Measuring the backfill on a production-sized copy** (production itself is never touched): restore the newest nightly `/var/backups/ya-mobility/ya-YYYY-MM-DD.sql.gz` into a throwaway `supabase/postgres:17.6.1.170` container with `--network none`, under the RLS kit's flock, as the 17.09 rehearsal did; then `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --single-transaction` the pending migrations in order, with this file last, wrapped so it ends in `ROLLBACK`, and read the `NOTICE: fahrtenbuch import facts backfilled: {...}` line — rows, per `time_source`, per `trip_status`, needs review — and `\timing` for the lock length. Delete the container. A cheaper read-only estimate on production itself (no function needed): `SELECT count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE raw_data_json ->> 'Uhrzeit des Fahrtbeginns' ~ '^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2} [0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}') FROM fahrtenbuch_trips;` — the second number is roughly the rows that will get `source_start_end`.
  - **Lock:** ADD COLUMN takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `fahrtenbuch_trips` for the whole transaction, backfill included. 100 000 synthetic trips took 1 min 55 s in a one-CPU container under Docker Desktop; the first two designs took 16 and 6 minutes (per-trip column resolution, and a single statement whose join the planner rescanned) — keep the keyed scratch table if you touch it.
  - **The RLS kit ran on the dev machine** for the first time (Docker Desktop, `node scripts/rls-test.mjs`): all files pass, `matrix.sql` unchanged, no `UNCOVERED` line.
- **Two edit defects found by phase 5, fixed (this PR), what to know:**
  - **An Inhaber edited on the Fahrer screen was saved as an employee.** `useDatabase.getDrivers()` mapped every column the edit form reads except `employment_type`, so `DriverModal` opened each driver with `driver.employment_type ?? 'employee'` and `planningToDb` wrote `'employee'` back on the next save of any field; planning then applied the hour limits an Inhaber is exempt from. The list read now goes through `mapDriverRow()` (`src/lib/driverRow.ts`, pinned by `driverRow.test.ts`), which carries it; the create and update paths already did. Production on 22.09.2026: 4 `company_owner` drivers in 2 companies. **Rows flipped before the fix cannot be found** — `drivers` has no `updated_at` — so the owner should check which of his drivers are Inhaber. Batch 1b's `flows-batch1b.mjs` pinned the old behaviour on purpose; move that pin when both are on staging.
  - **Editing a user on Benutzer without a new password always failed.** `UserModal` strips an empty password to `undefined` on an edit, and `handleEditUser` called `userData.password.trim()` on it, so the edit threw before the request and showed "Ein unerwarteter Fehler". Now `password?.trim()`, with the parameter typed optional as `UserModal`'s `onSave` already declares it. Batch 4's `flows-batch4.mjs` pinned the old behaviour; move that pin too.
  - The typecheck count drops from 226 to 224 with these two changes, and the baseline is lowered to match.
- **Gehaltsquittung screens (PR #34), what to know:**
  - The modal issues a receipt from the salary balance, prints a German PDF with the amount in words, lists receipts on Dokumente with void and signed-copy upload, and locks receipt payments in the salary details. No SQL changed; the RPCs come from PR #13.
  - Double booking: the request id is written to the tab sessionStorage the moment the request leaves, reused on retry and after a reload, and cleared on a definitive answer. Closing while the outcome is unknown goes through a confirmation. What it cannot cover: another tab, another browser, or cleared storage. A database-side guard is an open owner question.
  - `react-test-renderer` is a new dev dependency: the repository has no DOM test environment, and the lifecycle fix needed a rendered hook.
  - 4.3b followed in PR #43; see its entry below.
  - Owner questions are listed in the PR and in PROGRESS.md.
- **Overlapping salary rows (PR #71), what to know:**
  - The owner decided on 17.09.2026 that he merges overlapping salary rows by hand before paying, and that `confirm_salary_cash_payment`'s `overlapping_salary` refusal stays. PR #71 makes them findable: an amber badge beside the driver's name on the Gehälter screen, naming each overlapping row's period and amount. No query is added — it is folded out of the rows the screen already loads, in one `useMemo`. `src/utils/salaryPeriodOverlap.ts` is the pure detection, 19 tests.
  - **How they are created, established before proposing to prevent them.** Exactly two paths write a `salaries` row, both a click on the Gehälter screen: `SalariesManagement.tsx:301` (mark paid/unpaid) and `SalaryDetailsModal.tsx:119` (`ensureSalaryExists`, which fires *automatically* when the payments dialog opens on a driver with no row for that period). Nothing else — no import, no planning run, no Abrechnung, no edge function, no trigger, no RPC. Every period the app itself computes is a **business month** (`getBusinessMonthRange`, whose calendar-month fallback is unreachable), and consecutive business months never overlap. The overlap comes from the two free-form date inputs at `SalariesManagement.tsx:449-475`, which write any pair of dates unvalidated, past a `unique_driver_period` that covers only `period_start`. **So overlaps are hand-entry, not a supported flow** — a guard in the write path would break nothing that exists today.
  - ~~**There is no way to merge the rows in the product.**~~ — closed by PR #73, below: the Gehälter screen now **deletes** a salary row, which is the whole merge. Until then, "by hand" meant a direct database edit. #71's own follow-up question, "whether the screen should grow a delete for a salary with no payments", is answered yes and built; editing a period was built, reviewed and **removed**, for the reasons in that entry.
  - **Preventing new ones is proposed, not built**, and written up in PR #71's body for the owner: an application check at the two insert sites first, then a BEFORE INSERT (not UPDATE — merging needs edits) trigger, and **against** an exclusion constraint, which validates the whole table on `ADD CONSTRAINT` and so could not ship while any company still holds an overlap, raises on an inverted period, and would need a `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump.
- **Button label contrast (PR #72), what to know:**
  - **The trap, because it will be walked into again.** `--accent` is the accent *background*; `--accent-text` is accent-coloured *text for a normal background*. The names read as a pair and are not one. In the light theme both hold `#2563eb`, so `background: var(--accent)` with `color: var(--accent-text)` painted the label in the button's own colour — **1.00:1, the text simply absent**; in the dark theme `#22d3ee` on `#06b6d4`, 1.34:1. The owner hit both on staging. The token for a label on a filled background is `--text-inverse`, which `.btn-primary` in `index.css` has always used and which was **the only pairing in the codebase that already passed**.
  - 73 label colours in 40 files now use it: `--accent-text` on `--accent` (9 sites, 1.00/1.34 → 5.17/7.31), white on `--accent` (58, 5.17/2.43 → 5.17/7.31), white on `--danger` (6, 4.83/3.76 → 4.83/4.71). **The light theme does not move** — `--text-inverse` is `#ffffff` there, which 58 of these sites already spelled literally; only the dark theme changes, and only from failing to passing. `--accent-text` keeps its 91 correct uses on neutral surfaces, four of which are now pinned as well.
  - ~~**`--success` and `--warning` are the open half — 18 sites, and no token fixes them.**~~ — closed by PR #76, below. White reached only 3.77:1 on `--success` and 3.19:1 on `--warning` in the light theme, and `--text-inverse` *is* white there, so it changed nothing: the background was too light for any legible label. Both were also failing as text on their own `-subtle` fills (3.58:1 and 3.07:1). The owner approved darkening them on 17.09.2026.
  - **Two guards, and both were proved to fail before being trusted.** `tokens.contrast.test.ts` now parses the legacy hex blocks as well as the Phase-2 RGB ones — hence the two new `/* @legacy-tokens light|dark */` markers in `index.css` — and holds `--text-inverse` on accent/accent-hover/danger to 4.5:1 (breaking `--text-inverse` fails 3 tests). `accentLabel.contrast.test.ts` reads the source and refuses the pairing at the call site, in files the token test never opens (restoring one site names `components/SalaryDetailsModal.tsx:699`). Both carry the reasoning in a header comment so the next reader does not "fix" them; the second also self-checks that it can still see style objects, so a broken parser cannot pass by finding nothing.
  - **How the sites were found, because grep is not enough here.** A scanner parses `index.css` into both theme tables, walks every balanced `{…}` style object skipping strings and comments, splits conditional values per branch, resolves each `var(--x)` per theme, composites translucent values and computes the WCAG ratio. A two-line grep window reported ~7 defects; a 14-line window found 12 candidates of which **3 were false positives** (the importer's step bar carries no text, its label sits on the modal background; the OTP resend link's `--accent-text` branch has no background). Every hit was read in source before being changed. Disabled branches were left alone on purpose — `--text-tertiary` on `--bg-muted` is 2.24:1, but WCAG 1.4.3 exempts inactive controls and "fixing" it makes disabled buttons look enabled.
  - **What it did not do:** no screen was rendered. Every figure is computed from the token values; nothing was checked in a browser or on the VPS. The scan also turned up ~200 **hardcoded hex** colours (e.g. `#b91c1c` on `rgba(220,38,38,0.06)`) that read acceptably in light and badly in dark because they are theme-blind literals — a separate, larger defect class, reported and untouched. **Still true after PR #76, and the owner deferred them to phase 5 on 17.09.2026. Do not pick them up piecemeal.**
- **Darkened `--success` and `--warning` (PR #76), what to know:**
  - **The owner's decision of 17.09.2026**, and the other half of #72. The light values moved `#059669 → #0A7A45` and `#d97706 → #8F5200`; the dark values did **not** move.
  - **One token could not carry both roles, and the reason is arithmetic, not taste.** In the dark theme a white label needs the fill at relative luminance ≤ 0.183, while reading as text on `--bg-surface` (`#111827`) needs the token at ≥ 0.237. The windows do not overlap, at any hue. So the fill got its own token — **`--success-solid` and `--warning-solid`**, named after `--critical-solid`, which is the Phase-2 block's answer to exactly this problem. In the **light** theme both roles want the same darkness, so there the two tokens hold one value; the split exists only where it is forced. `--success` / `--warning` stay the text colour, and that is still their job at ~200 call sites.
  - **The measured ratios**, all now pinned. Light: white on the fill 3.77 → **5.41** (success) and 3.19 → **6.22** (warning); text on `--bg-surface` 3.77 → **5.41** / 3.19 → **6.22**; text on the `-subtle` fill 3.58 → **5.13** / 3.07 → **6.00**; worst light role 5.00 / 5.75. Dark: white on the fill 2.54 → **4.88** and 2.15 → **4.88** (through the new `-solid` tokens `#0B824A` / `#A85F00`, whose lightness is the balance point where the 4.5:1 label margin and the 3:1 shape margin are equal); the text roles were already 5.35–8.93 and are unchanged.
  - **26 call sites, not 18.** #72's list counted only style objects with an explicit `color` entry; nine more spell the label as Tailwind's `text-white` in the className, and a 26th is a **hover handler** (`SalaryDetailsModal.tsx:906`) that assigns the fill imperatively, where no style object holds it. `PlatformLoginPage.tsx:179` from that list is gone — PR #74 deleted the file. Each site changed one token name in the background position only; no className, no layout, no component.
  - **Hue: say plainly what this does and does not fix.** Green still reads as green and amber as amber to normal vision. For **red/green colour blindness it fixes nothing, and nothing could.** Simulating the trio (Viénot 1999) shows today's `--success`/`--danger` already collapse to 1.01:1 under deuteranopia — indistinguishable *before* this change. A 2803 × 2133 search over every green and amber that passes all the contrast roles puts the ceiling at 1.46:1, and that optimum demands a `#532209` that no longer reads as amber at all. The cause is the constraint itself: once all three status colours must be dark enough for a white label they share one narrow lightness band, and lightness is the only channel a dichromat has left. **So the mitigation is that nothing relies on hue**, which was verified site by site: every filled button carries a German label and a lucide icon (`Download` + "Excel", `Plus` + "Neuer Check", `Save`, `CheckCircle`), and every count badge carries its number. The one fill with no text is the discount-code toggle, where the white knob now reaches 5.41:1 against the track instead of 2.54:1.
  - **Why these two values.** They are the Phase-2 `--positive` and `--caution`, which the owner already approved for the prototype and which `tokens.contrast.test.ts` already guards. The legacy and Phase-2 palettes now agree on what success and warning are, so Phase 5 moves a screen across without re-deciding the colour. They were not picked for colour-blind separation, because as shown above no available value buys any.
  - **The guard, proved to fail three ways before being trusted.** `tokens.contrast.test.ts` gained **40** legacy checks (112 → 152 cases) and `accentLabel.contrast.test.ts` two more: `--success`/`--warning` as text on the four `--bg-*` surfaces, the same on their own `-subtle` fills, a **white** label (not `--text-inverse` — no call site uses it) on each `-solid`, and each `-solid` at 3:1 against `--bg-surface` and `--bg-elevated` for SC 1.4.11. It also learned to parse `rgba(…)` and composite it, because the dark `-subtle` fills are translucent and were never measured before. `accentLabel.contrast.test.ts` gained the call-site half: a filled background naming `--success`/`--warning` instead of the `-solid` twin is an offence, in the style-object form and the imperative hover form. Proofs: restoring the old light `--success` fails **8** tests; lightening dark `--success-solid` back to the text value fails the white-label test; pointing two fills back at the text token names `components/SalaryDetailsModal.tsx:906` and `pages/WerkstattPage.tsx:193`. Both new blocks carry their reasoning in a header comment, and the call-site rule self-checks that it can still see a `-solid` fill, so a broken parser cannot pass by finding nothing.
  - **What it did not do:** **no screen was rendered.** Every figure is computed from the token values; nothing was opened in a browser or on the VPS, and the dark theme in particular changes the look of ten filled buttons that nobody has seen. Check `Werkstatt`, `Bargeldbestand`, `Belege` and the admin dashboard's amber count badges on staging after the deploy.
  - ~~**One finding left open, deliberately.** `--danger` on `--danger-subtle` is **4.41:1 light and 3.87:1 dark**.~~ — closed by PR #79, below, on the owner's decision of 18.09.2026.
- **Darkened `--danger` (PR #79), what to know:**
  - **The owner's decision of 18.09.2026**, and the last of the three status colours. Light `#dc2626 → #C0262D`; dark `#ef4444 → #FF8A8A` for the text role, with a new `--danger-solid` at `#D63A40` for the fill. These are the Phase-2 `--critical` and `--critical-solid` unchanged, already approved for the prototype and already guarded, so the legacy and Phase-2 palettes now agree on all three statuses and phase 5 moves a screen across without re-deciding a colour.
  - **The defect was wider than the one finding named it.** #76 measured `--danger` on its own `-subtle` fill at 4.41 light / 3.87 dark. Measuring every surface the token actually renders on found **three more failing dark pairs** that nothing had ever checked: on `--bg-elevated` **4.21**, and on `--danger-subtle` over `--bg-surface` **4.34** and over `--bg-subtle` **4.11**. Eight failing roles, not two.
  - **Where the split was needed, checked rather than assumed.** The dark theme only, as for the other two, and with a wider gap: a white label needs the fill at relative luminance ≤ **0.1833**, while the text role needs ≥ **0.2741** — the binding surface is `--danger-subtle` composited over `--bg-elevated`, not `--bg-surface` as for success and warning. In the **light** theme one value serves both: the fill needs ≤ 0.1833 and the text role ≤ 0.1633, so the text constraint is strictly tighter and `--danger` and `--danger-solid` hold the same `#C0262D`.
  - **The measured ratios.** Light — text on the four `--bg-*` surfaces 4.46/4.83/4.83/4.59 → **5.46/5.90/5.90/5.61**; text on `--danger-subtle` 4.41 → **5.40**; a white label on the fill 4.83 → **5.90**; shape against the surface 4.83 → **5.90**. Dark — text on the surfaces 5.10/4.71/4.21/4.48 → **8.45/7.82/6.99/7.43**; text on `--danger-subtle` over them 4.71/4.34/3.87/4.11 → **7.81/7.20/6.42/6.81**; a white label on `--danger-solid` **4.63**; `--danger-solid` as a shape 3.83 and 3.43, both over the 3:1 of SC 1.4.11. `--danger-subtle` itself does **not** move, in either theme: the dark wash is the same hue (0°) as the new text colour and reaches 6.42–7.81 under it, so repainting some forty badges would buy nothing.
  - **One role gets very slightly worse, and it is the honest trade.** The six destructive buttons that PR #72 had put on `--text-inverse` read 4.71 in the dark theme; as a white label on `--danger-solid` they read **4.63**. Both clear 4.5. What the swap buys is that the *text* role stops failing at all, and that all three statuses now spell a filled button the same way. `--text-inverse` is #111827 in the dark theme and would be **3.83:1** on `--danger-solid`, so the earlier shape is now a defect and the call-site guard refuses it by name.
  - **Nine call sites, and three of them were invisible to #76's scanner.** Six spell the fill directly (`FahrtenbuchImportModal.tsx:1232`, `FahrtenbuchTripsView.tsx:531`, `TrialBanner.tsx:92`, `CompaniesPage.tsx:966` and `:983`, `SuperAdminDashboard.tsx:1581`); three reach it through a **name** — `CompaniesPage.tsx:268` via `btnColor`, `TrialBanner.tsx:59` via a new `fill` const, `ConfirmationModal.tsx:117` via `getConfirmBgColor()`. Those same three sites also carried **three `--warning` fills and one `--success` fill that PR #76 missed** — it read literals only, so its guard reported `[]` while they stood. The scanner now resolves a background written as a bare identifier or a zero-argument call back to its `const` initializer.
  - **Hue.** `#C0262D` is hue 357°, saturation 67%, lightness 45% — a crimson, not a brown or a maroon; `#dc2626` was hue 0°, 72%, 51%. Dark `#D63A40` is hue 358°, 66%, 53%. A destructive button still reads as red. For red/green colour blindness the picture is unchanged and still bad: simulated (Viénot 1999), `--warning` against `--danger` is **1.05:1** under deuteranopia in the dark theme and **1.20:1** under protanopia in the light one. **So hue carries nothing, and this was verified for the nine sites rather than asserted**: all nine carry a German text label; six also carry a lucide icon at all times (`RefreshCw`, `Trash2` ×3, `Zap` ×2), a seventh shows `RefreshCw` while the action runs (`CompaniesPage.tsx:268`), and the two with none — `SuperAdminDashboard.tsx:1581` *Ablehnen* and `ConfirmationModal.tsx:117`, whose label is the caller's — are still words, not colour. **No `--danger` control in the codebase is colour-only**: a sweep for small coloured shapes and for bar widths found none.
  - **The guard, 152 → 170 token cases plus 5 → 7 call-site cases, and proved to fail six ways.** Restoring the old light `--danger` fails **5** (`bg-base` and the four `-subtle` composites, at 4.46 and 4.41); restoring the old dark value fails **5** (`bg-elevated` 4.21, `bg-subtle` 4.48, and three `-subtle` composites); lightening `--danger-solid` to the text value fails the white-label test at **2.27**; pointing a direct site back at `var(--danger)` names `pages/CompaniesPage.tsx:966`; pointing the **indirect** `btnColor` back names `pages/CompaniesPage.tsx:268` — the proof the resolver is doing work; labelling a `-solid` fill with `--text-inverse` names `components/FahrtenbuchTripsView.tsx:531`. The resolver has its own two self-checks: a fixture that pins the ternary and the arrow-body shapes, and a count that it is still wired into the scan of real source.
  - **What left the token test on purpose.** `{ fg: 'text-inverse', bg: 'danger' }` — PR #72's answer to a white label failing on the dark `--danger`, which moved the *label* instead of the *fill*. Nothing pairs those two any more, and an assertion about a combination no screen renders reads as coverage without being any.
  - **What it did not do:** **no screen was rendered.** Every figure is computed from the token values. The light theme moves for the first time in this series — `--danger` is some 157 `color:` call sites, so every red word in the product is slightly darker — and the dark theme changes nine filled buttons and the colour of all that red text. Worth a look on staging at Fahrtenbuch (delete an import), Firmen (the lifecycle dialogs), the admin dashboard's *Ablehnen*, the trial banner, and any toast error.
- **Phase 5 batch 1b: Belege, Fahrer and Umsätze on the design system (PR #100, in review, branch `phase5/batch1b-belege-fahrer-umsaetze`, rebased onto staging after #96–#99 and #102), what to know:**
  - **The rest of batch 1, on batch 1's pattern.** `ReceiptList`, `ReceiptModal`, `BelegePage`, `DriversManagement`, `DriverModal`, `FahrerPage`, `IncomeManagement` and `UmsaetzePage` move onto `src/components/ui`, inside the unchanged `AppLayout`. The forms' selects are logic (`e.target.value`, `required`), so they use `NativeSelect` — batch 3's primitive since the rebase; batch 1b had built the same thing and took theirs, renaming its prop to `containerClassName`.
  - **Look only, proved per file as text.** `node design-harness/identity-proof.mjs . origin/staging <files>` compares each file's logic region (after the imports, before `// ── Presentation (phase 5)`) with the base line by line, and requires every inline `onX={…}` of the old markup and every call it made to something the logic declares, verbatim in the new file. Lines that left a logic region, all of them look: the `hoveredRow` state (ReceiptList, DriversManagement, IncomeManagement), the inline `inputStyle` objects (ReceiptList, ReceiptModal, DriverModal, IncomeManagement), ReceiptList's `SortIcon` and `formatDE` (euros go through `utils/money.ts`), and IncomeManagement's carousel refs and drag handlers, which wrote the carousel's cursor and scroll position and nothing else — the carousel is a per-driver table whose rows are `getWeeklyDataByDriver()`'s answer, as each card was. **Lines added:** one per page, `useDatabase()` also asked for `isConnected` (below), and in IncomeManagement exactly the three the load-failure rule allows. Everything else — every `supabase.from` chain, filter, order, parse, rounding, permission check and export — is the old text.
  - **The Belegnummer is still the system's.** `generateUniqueReceiptNumber()` (`BEL-{timestamp}-{random}`, checked against every loaded number) is untouched and `applyExtracted()` never writes it; `flows-batch1b.mjs` scans a Beleg and checks that the number the insert carries is the one the form generated before the scan. `RECEIPT_CATEGORIES`, `Tanken` included, and the payment-method values are the stored strings. The number field stays editable, as it was; making it read-only would be a behaviour change for its own PR.
  - **A figure whose load failed is not shown.** Belege and Fahrer read through `useDatabase`, which on a failed connection test serves the lists from local storage — which nothing writes — so the screens said "Keine Belege vorhanden" and 0,00 € for a database they never reached; the pages now read `isConnected` and show an error and no figure. Umsätze reads its drivers the same way, and its entries directly: **`IncomeManagement` has a `loadFailed` state** under the rule decided for batch 2 (cleared where `loadIncomes` starts, set in its existing catch), so a failed read of the entries alone withholds the total, the per-driver figures and the export. **Still swallowed:** a `getReceipts()`/`getDrivers()` read that fails after a good connection test falls back to local storage inside `useDatabase`; surfacing it is a change to the hook.
  - **Read contexts and roles.** Every write control asks the scope (`useCompanyScope()`, directly or through the page's `canEdit`/`canDelete`/`canCreate`): a platform `view` context and a viewer are offered no add, edit, delete or status toggle, and the flows check all three screens for both. Controls a caller may not use are hidden rather than disabled.
  - **Defects found by clicking.** (1) **Fixed by #102** while this was in review: `useDatabase.getDrivers()` did not map `employment_type`, so an Inhaber edited in the Fahrer form was saved as an employee; `mapDriverRow()` carries it now, the Fahrer list shows the *Inhaber* badge, and `flows-batch1b.mjs` pins the fixed behaviour (the edit keeps `company_owner`).
