(2) **Open:** the Belege week filter drops the week's Sunday — `getBusinessWeeksForMonth()` ends a week at Sunday 00:00 local time and `ReceiptList` compares `new Date(receipt.date)`, UTC midnight, 02:00 in Berlin — and with it that week's summary PDF; a fix to the filter logic, its own PR, pinned as it is in the flows. (3) **Open, small:** after a Beleg is added the form re-mounts (BelegePage's loading state unmounts it), so `resetForm(true)`'s "keep the driver" never shows.
  - **Rendered: 65 shots before and 65 after**, in `Docs/design/phase5/batch1b/{before,after}/`: the three screens at 375, 768 and 1280 in both themes; at 1280 light and 375 dark the add form, a scanned Beleg with two uncertain fields, both edit forms, the planning section, delete confirmations, the filter panel, the driver limit, empty, a failed connection, a failed read of the Umsätze entries alone, and read-only (a platform `view` context); one English shot per screen. The before set is `origin/staging`'s `src/` through this branch's harness (`--root`); the Fahrer before shots were retaken after the rebase, since #102 put the Inhaber badge on the legacy screen too. `flows-batch1b.mjs`: 27 checks, verified on each write's payload in `window.__harness.writes`. **Staging has not seen these screens.**
  - **Why captures stalled at the first 375 px shot.** Not the touch context. The harness's resolver swapped a real module for its fake by resolved path, and Vite resolves a URL the browser fetches with `<root>/index.html` as the importer — so the URL of a real module a fake re-exports was served the fake itself. The fake re-exported itself, `formatAmountDE` (imported by the Beleg form) was missing, the module graph failed to link, and since `main.tsx` imports every page the harness never booted. Every run's first shot is a 375 px one, hence the pattern. `vite.config.ts` now leaves URL requests alone. **The shared harness after the rebase:** batch 1b's rows live in `fixtures-batch1b.ts` and its shots in `shots-batch1b.mjs`, as batches 2 and 3 keep theirs; `fakes/supabase.ts` gained the write log (`window.__harness.writes`) and three one-line hooks; `capture.mjs` keeps staging's per-checkout cache, DOM-quiet settle and 600 s first page, and adds a warm-up, a hard cap per shot with one retry pass, and taller windows for `tall` dialog shots; `vite.config.ts` pre-bundles `lucide-react` and keeps its own cache per checkout for runs that do not go through `capture.mjs`. **Batches 2 and 3 were re-run on the merged harness** (`flows-batch2.mjs` 29 of 29, `flows-batch3.mjs` 20 of 20).
  - **Ratchets:** style 5871 → **5502** inline styles and 3402 → **3330** colours against staging after batches 2 and 3; the eight files count zero and the ratchet test pins them. Typecheck 221 → **219**: the Beleg modal's invented `style2` prop and `ConfirmationModal`'s `type="warning"` mismatch went with the old markup. **Contrast:** `caution` and `positive` on `field` at 3:1 (a field framed as uncertain or as ready to save; lowest 5.41:1) are new; `positive`, `critical` and `brand-text` as text on `sunken`, which the Beleg form's OCR diagnostics and drop zone render, are batch 2's pairs.
  - **Left for the owner or a later PR:** the Sunday defect; whether the Belegnummer field should become read-only; the Fahrer table's *Erstellt von* column shows the creator's account id, as it always did (resolving names needs a query, as Belege does).
- **Phase 5 batch 1: Fahrzeuge and Fixkosten on the design system (PR #90, merged to staging, branch `phase5/batch1-fahrzeuge-fixkosten`), what to know:**
  - **Two of the batch's five screens, on purpose.** They carry the least payroll logic, so the pattern is judged where a mistake costs least. Belege, Fahrer and Umsätze followed in batch 1b (above) and copy it. `AppLayout` stays; the screens moved inside it.
  - **Look only, and checked as text, not by eye.** Each component's body from its first line through `handleExport` (CarModal: through `handleRenewTuev`) is the old text verbatim; the new markup is appended below a `// ── Presentation (phase 5)` line and only draws. The check is a substring comparison of the old block against the new file, and it passes for all three. Two places mixed presentation into logic and were split rather than rewritten: `TUEV_CONFIG` in `CarsManagement.tsx` held the labels the Excel export reads **and** the colours and icons — the labels stayed, the look moved to `TUEV_PRESENTATION`; and `FixedCostsManagement.tsx` lost only its `hoveredRow` state and its `inputStyle` object. The one piece of logic that sat inside markup, the driver checklist's `isSelected`/`isDisabled`, is kept word for word inside the new rows.
  - **Behaviour that did change, all of it presentation:** `ConfirmationModal` is replaced on these two screens by the new `ConfirmDialog`, which keeps its contract exactly (confirm calls `onConfirm()` then `onClose()`, unawaited) and adds Escape and a click outside as "no". The vehicle form is a `Sheet`: Escape and the close button close it, a click beside it does not (the old overlay ignored both, and a stray click must not throw away a typed form). Fixkosten opens its add form from the page header and focuses the first field. A Fixkosten load that failed with nothing loaded no longer shows "Keine Fixkosten vorhanden" and a total of 0,00 € — the table shows its error state and the total card is hidden. The TÜV filter is a tab row with count badges; sorting a column is new (the initial order is still the database's). The "Excel" buttons read *Nach Excel exportieren*.
  - **Not changed, and worth a follow-up of its own:** the Fixkosten Excel export still writes amounts with `toFixed(2)`, a decimal point. That is what the export writes, so it stayed; it is audit finding 1's class and needs its own look. The Fixkosten debug line (audit finding 6) stays too, set as small print under the list, because Docs/design/README.md leaves removing it to the owner.
  - **Rendered, and how.** No staging: `design-harness/` renders the production `CarsPage` and `FixkostenPage` inside the production `AppLayout` on invented data, by swapping `src/lib/supabase.ts` and five context hooks at module resolution in its own Vite config, so nothing in `src/` knows about it. `npm run harness` serves it (`?screen=fahrzeuge|fixkosten&data=…&role=…&theme=…&lang=…`), `npm run harness:capture -- --set before|after [--root <checkout>]` takes the shots, and `npm run harness:flows` clicks every write and refusal through (15 checks: add with a driver, the required plate, the two-driver lock, edit, delete and cancel, the tabs, add/edit/delete a cost with the total following, the empty-field refusal, the viewer, the failed load, and every row action within a 375 px touch screen). Both shot sets come from the same harness and the same Chrome: the before set from a `git worktree` of the commit before the conversion, via `--root`. 34 each, in `Docs/design/phase5/batch1/{before,after}/`: both screens at 375, 768 and 1280 in both themes, ten states (form, delete, limit, empty, add, inline edit, viewer, missing table) at 1280 light and 375 dark, and one English shot per screen. The 375 shots emulate a touch phone, so the coarse-pointer sizes show.
  - **What rendering found that no test would have.** A CSS grid without `grid-cols-1` sizes its track to the widest child's min-content, so the tab row pushed the whole Fahrzeuge page past 375 px; `grid-cols-1` (`minmax(0,1fr)`) is now on every grid wrapper. At 375 px on a touch screen the icon buttons grow to 40 px and the table scrolled 13 px, hiding the delete button — the approved prototype has the same clipping — so the plate and status columns take tighter padding below `sm`. A text field in a table cell cannot shrink below its 20-character natural width, which pushed the inline edit's save/cancel off a phone; `size={1}` lets it take the cell's width. A Chrome with an English UI shows `mm/dd/yyyy` in a date field whatever the page's locale; the capture launches Chrome with `--lang=de-DE`.
  - **The style ratchet** (`scripts/style-ratchet.mjs`, rules and 16 tests in `style-ratchet-core.mjs` / `style-ratchet.test.ts`) counts, per file under `src/`, inline styles (`style={…}` and imperative `el.style.x =` writes, the legacy hover) and hard-coded colours (hex, `rgb()`/`hsl()` with a number, Tailwind palette classes, quoted `'white'`/`'black'`), after stripping comments. More than `style-baseline.json` fails, fewer fails and asks for `npm run styles:update`, per file so one screen cannot pay for another. It runs in `npm run build` after the typecheck ratchet. **6731 → 6607 inline styles and 3793 → 3774 colours** across 111 → 107 files; the five batch-1 files are at zero and a test pins them there. **Its cost is the typecheck ratchet's:** a PR merged while another is open can move a file the other's baseline records — PR #87 moved `PlatformTeamPage.tsx` from 50 to 67 while this branch was open — so rebase and run `npm run styles:update` before merging, and read the diff it writes. **And new code is still being written the legacy way:** PR #89 (3.10/3.11), merged while this was open, added 91 inline styles and 2 hard-coded colours in five new files (`AnnouncementBanner.tsx` and four under `components/settings/`). Once the ratchet is on staging, a new file like those fails the build; `npm run styles:update` would record it, so a review that sees a new or higher line in `style-baseline.json` should ask why the screen was not built on `src/components/ui` instead.
  - **Contrast pairs added to `tokens.contrast.test.ts`:** `fg` and `fg-muted` on `brand-soft`, `positive-soft`, `caution-soft` and `critical-soft` (Alert bodies, the selected driver row; lowest 6.60:1); `critical` on `sunken` at 3:1 (the delete icon's hover, 5.21:1); and a new block for the Phase-2 foregrounds on the **legacy** `--bg-base`, the page background a converted screen sits on until `AppLayout` moves — `fg`, `fg-muted`, `fg-subtle` and `critical` at 4.5:1 (lowest `fg-subtle` light, 4.96:1) and `brand` and `focus` at 3:1 (lowest `brand` dark, 4.18:1). 202 contrast cases, all passing.
  - **The typecheck ratchet drops 227 → 226** (staging had just reached 227): `CarsPage.tsx`'s unused `React` import went with the page's rewrite.
  - **For the next batch — copy:** the split (logic block untouched and verified by substring, presentation appended below a marker); `ConfirmDialog` for every `ConfirmationModal type="confirm"`; `Alert` for every legacy tinted box; `grid-cols-1` on every grid wrapper; the harness, extended with fixtures and fakes for the tables and hooks the next screens read; before shots from a worktree of the base; a `flows-batchN.mjs` of its own; the overflow check at 375 with a touch pointer. **Avoid:** judging a phone layout with a mouse (coarse-pointer controls are 25 % larger); a `DataTable` column that keeps `px-4` on a phone when the row has two icon actions; an `initialSort` that silently replaces the database's order; and leaving a figure on screen when its load failed.
- **Phase 5 batch 2: Gehälter, Abrechnung and Bargeldbestand on the design system (PR #98, in review, branch `phase5/batch2-gehaelter-abrechnung-bargeld`), what to know:**
  - **Four files converted:** `SalariesPage`, `SalariesManagement`, `AbrechnungPage` and `BargeldbestandPage`, inside the unchanged `AppLayout`, on `PageHeader`, `KpiCard`, `DataTable`, `Alert`, `Field`/`Input`/`Select`, `Tabs` and `Dialog`. Money goes through `formatEuro` / `formatEuroCents` with tabular figures, so the old `1234.56 €`, `0.00` and `50.0%` read `1.234,56 €`, `0,00 €` and `50,0 %`.
  - **Look only, proven as text by `design-harness/prove-batch2.mjs`.** For each file it compares the base's logic block with the new one line by line, after removing exactly the lines it lists as added, and checks that every expression the old markup computed inline is still in the new file verbatim: 34 snippets in `SalariesManagement` (the overlap hint, the transaction dialog's five sums, the netto/amount_due choice and its marker, every handler), 37 in `AbrechnungPage`, 9 in `BargeldbestandPage` and 2 in `SalariesPage`, plus the Abrechnung share card as 195 lines byte for byte. Negative controls: changing the ratio default `?? 0.5` to `0.6`, or one handler's argument, fails it at that line. It defaults to the branch point and passes against `04be061` after the rebase.
  - **The one deliberate behaviour change: a failed read no longer shows a figure** (the coordinator's decision of 22.09.2026). `SalariesManagement`, `BargeldbestandPage` and `AbrechnungPage` had no failure state — the catch showed a toast and left the screen empty (every driver at 0,00 €, a till at 0,00 €) or, in Abrechnung and on a Bargeldbestand month change, **the previous month's rows under the new month's name**. Each now has a `loadFailed` flag, and those are the only lines added to any logic block: its `useState`, a clear where a load starts and a set in the existing catch (3 + 3 + 5 lines; Abrechnung has two loaders). With it set, the screens show an `Alert` with a retry instead of the figures and withhold the export, and Abrechnung also the driver and week choices, which still hold the old month's names. `SalariesPage` already had its own error state and gained no line.
  - **The Excel exports are unchanged, checked as files:** `design-harness/exports-batch2.mjs` downloads each export in the harness and digests its cells. Run against a worktree of the base and against this branch, the four digests (Gehälter; Bargeldbestand for September, for October, and for a billing reader with its caveat) are equal, with only the four export timestamps masked. The one change around them: the button is disabled while a failed load leaves nothing true to export.
  - **Rules checked to survive:** the salary `(Umsatz + Aktionen) × salary_ratio + Trinkgeld` (the flows compare one driver's five figures with the base's); the business-month period from `getBusinessMonthRange`, 0-indexed underneath (September → 07.09.–04.10., October → 05.10.–01.11.); the Abrechnung RPCs with the 1-indexed month and `getBusinessWeeksForMonth(selectedMonth - 1, …)`; Bar zurück as the RPC's `bar_zuruck`, positive meaning the driver owes; the Tankenquote (7,24 % for the harness's Ahmad Haddad in week 4, as before); the till as `Bargeld − Belege − (Gehaltsquittungen − Stornos)` in cents, with a Gehaltsquittung voided on 02.10. lowering September and raising October by 680,00 €; and `cashLedgerInScope()` — a billing reader gets no payout card, the `Bargeld − Belege` label and the caveat on screen and in the dump.
  - **Stayed legacy, and why:** the **Abrechnung share card** (html2canvas renders it off-screen into the PNG a driver is sent, so its look is that document; kept byte for byte with `renderTankenquoteCard`, which is why `AbrechnungPage` keeps 48 inline styles and 121 colours); **`DriverSalarySummaryCard`** (also rasterised into an image); the **status `ConfirmationModal` in `SalariesManagement`** (nothing calls `openStatusModal`, so it never opens); **`SalaryPaymentModal` and `SalaryPeriodsModal`** (write forms); and **`SalaryDetailsModal`**, not attempted: it holds the payment writes, the Gehaltsquittung handoff and the weekly Bar zurück import, and parses amounts inside its JSX handlers — its own step, with its own proof. `GehaltsquittungModal` and `GehaltsquittungList` were already converted (4.3a) and are untouched.
  - **Rendered, and how.** 47 shots before and 47 after in `Docs/design/phase5/batch2/`, from the same harness and Chrome, the before set from a detached worktree at `e2925d5` (its `src/` is the base's) via `--root`: the three screens at 375, 768 and 1280 in both themes; at 1280 light and 375 dark the revenue dialog, a platform view context (read-only), no drivers, a failed salary read, a failed driver list, the monthly view, the detail dialog, an empty month, a failed load, **October with the Storno of a Gehaltsquittung paid in September**, a **billing reader** (the ledger out of scope), an empty till and a failed till; and one English shot per screen. `design-harness/flows-batch2.mjs` clicks 29 checks through, including a new harness scenario, `next-month-fails`, in which September loads and every read of October fails: nothing of September may stay on screen.
  - **What rendering found:** on a phone the old Abrechnung page was 134 px wider than the screen, and the new Gehälter table first was too — four 40 px actions beside a name and a total do not fit 375 px, and Tailwind's `grid-cols-2` (`minmax(0,1fr)`, min-content zero) let the table squeeze the actions column to 18 px. Below `sm` the Gehälter total and status now sit in the driver cell and the actions stand two by two on sized tracks; the Bargeldbestand driver becomes the description's second line. **A pre-existing flash, in the base as here:** when the rows arrive, `SalariesManagement` commits one frame with the new salary rows and the previous `calculations` before its effect recomputes them — every driver with a stored row briefly carries an overlap badge and every figure reads 0,00 €. It is logic, so it stays; `capture.mjs` now waits for the page to stop changing, because a fixed pause on this memory-starved machine caught that frame.
  - **Harness, kept apart from the parallel batches:** batch 2's rows are `fixtures-batch2.ts`, its shot list `shots-batch2.mjs` (which `capture.mjs --batch batch2` loads), and the shared files gained a line or an import each. Two platform roles joined `admin` and `viewer`: `view` (read_only in a view context, business.read) and `billing` (no business.read). The store answers `get_weekly_abrechnung` and `get_monthly_abrechnung` from the same rows.
  - **Ratchets.** Styles **6607 → 6257** inline, **3774 → 3518** colours; `SalariesManagement`, `SalariesPage` and `BargeldbestandPage` at zero, `AbrechnungPage` at 48/121 (all of it the share image and html2canvas's background), and `style-ratchet.test.ts` pins the four. Typecheck **226 → 225**: the old table reported `calc.remaining_balance` as possibly undefined twice, the new cell passes it through `Number()`; every other error is the base's, compared line by line.
  - **Contrast pairs added:** `positive`, `critical` and `brand-text` on `sunken` at 4.5:1 — an amount's colour on a table's hover and totals fill (lowest `positive` light, 4.77:1).
  - **Not checked:** staging. The screens were rendered on invented data only; look at Gehälter, Abrechnung (and its share image) and Bargeldbestand on staging after the merge.
- **Phase 5 batch 3: Dokumente, Monatsplanung and Stundennachweis on the design system, the display only (PR #99, in review, branch `phase5/batch3-dokumente-planung-stundennachweis`), what to know:**
  - **The frame moved, and nothing that computes, plans or builds a document.** Converted: `DocumentsPage` whole (header, the five tool cards, the archive, both delete dialogs, the Fahrtenbuch sub-view's header); `FahrtenbuchTripsView` whole (toolbar, filters, the import list, the summary as `KpiCard`s, the trips as a `DataTable` from `md` up and as cards on a phone, paging, the import delete); `MonatsplanungDriverPool` whole; `CompanyPlannerPage`'s frame (header, the mode choices, Phase 1's month/year/import fields, its four block parameters, the run button, `BlockStatusBanner`); `MonatsplanungAppliedRuns`' list, rows and delete dialog; and `StundennachweisPage`'s frame (header and its three actions, the progress bar, step 1, step 2, the waiting states). One new primitive, `NativeSelect` (the browser's select in the design system's frame: the Radix Select hands its value to `onValueChange`, so swapping it in would rewrite handlers a look-only change must leave alone).
  - **What stays legacy, and why.** No file that builds a document or plans a month was opened: the Stundennachweis PDF (`stundennachweisPdfExport`), the Fahrtenbuch XLSX (`fahrtenbuchExport`, `monatsplanungXlsxExport`, `historicalWorkingHoursFahrtenbuchExport`), `fahreruebersichtExport`, the Trinkgeld-Quittung and Mietwagenauftrag dialogs, the Gehaltsquittung screens (already converted in 4.3a), the clean engine, `monatsplanungPhase*`, `monatsplanungRepairPass`, `shiftBlockEngine`, `weekCalculations`, `historicalWorkingHoursStundennachweisPreview`. Inside the converted files, kept verbatim as legacy markup because they compute as they draw: **StundennachweisPage** — the sheet's summary bar (the salary-range check is an IIFE in the markup, the working-day count a filter in it), the duration-limit list (each date label built in the render loop), the notes carousel, the structured warnings, the `Timesheet` document and its `TimeInput`; **CompanyPlannerPage** — `ResultPanels`, `StatCard`, `Th`, `Td`, the `?debug=1` tables (a render loop that detects cross-midnight blocks and formats averages); **MonatsplanungAppliedRuns** — `RunDetailModal` (the XLSX export dialog, with the `'original'` mode that must reproduce the imported headers), the Stundennachweis preview's error boundary and its "unavailable" fallback. Whole components left legacy: `MonatsplanungAssignmentPreview` (Phases 3–5 and `handleApply`), `MonatsplanungStundennachweisPreview` (Lohnart, premiums, transfers), `FinalizePlanningModal` (the pre-flight checks and document generation), `HistoricalReconciliationWizard`, `HistoricalWorkingHoursWizard` (its dead `AutoShiftPlanView` untouched), `FahrtenbuchImportModal`.
  - **Proven as text, per file, by `design-harness/identity-batch3.mjs`** (`node design-harness/identity-batch3.mjs --base <base commit>`). For each file it compares the base commit's text with the working tree's: (1) every old line outside the declared redrawn ranges appears verbatim, in order, as the same contiguous chunks; (2) every expression the old markup evaluated — handlers, `disabled`/`value`/`min`/`max` guards, the condition in front of a conditional block, the collection a list is drawn from, formatting calls — appears in the new file, whitespace aside; (3) every old line that reads or writes data, calls the clock or `Math`, parses a number, formats a date or an amount, or checks a permission appears too. A loss must be listed as a drop with its reason, a listed drop that is not missing fails as a stale claim, and `mustContain` names where a dropped expression's figures went, so a drop cannot hide one. It also fails the branch if any file outside batch 3's allow-list changed. **Across the six files: 5645 of 7806 old lines verbatim, 668 expressions carried, 440 sensitive lines present, and every drop presentation** (colour maps, a style object, header-key loops, the card list split into `KpiCard`s, and `toLocaleString`/`toFixed` replaced by `formatEuro`). Negative controls: removing the company filter from the group delete, pointing "Öffnen" at the PDF handler, a clamp bound 120 → 121, the run button losing its import guard, the Fahrtenbuch download's year fallback and the admin test on an import's delete each fail it. As first written it had four faults of its own (it walked into style objects, read imports as markup, could not see an insertion, and compared `{…}` with `(…)`); they were fixed before it was trusted.
  - **What a figure says differently, all of it the way it is written:** euro amounts go through `utils/money.ts` — Dokumente's gross (a no-break space before €), the driver pool's salary range (`toFixed` wrote `2100.00 €`), the trips view's totals (`toLocaleString` with only a minimum of two digits printed a float sum with three, `20.990,855 €`) and a phone card's fare (a US decimal point). **Dates were left exactly as they were**, including the archive's `new Date(…).toLocaleDateString('de-DE')`, which writes `4.9.2026` rather than `04.09.2026` — a display rule, but a date call, and this batch does not move date calls; a follow-up.
  - **Behaviour that changed, all of it presentation.** The delete dialogs (a record, a month, a planning run, an import) are Radix dialogs: Escape and a click outside mean Abbrechen, and all four still refuse to close while their delete runs and still show a failure inside themselves — so none is `ConfirmDialog`, which closes on confirm. Dokumente's month header is a button of its own (`aria-expanded`); a click on the row's empty space no longer folds it. The Stundennachweis back button has a name ("Zurück", audit finding 3). Step 2's tabs are Radix tabs driven only by the original `onClick`s (`activationMode="manual"`, no `onValueChange`). A failed driver read in the pool shows its error without seven counts of zero. The trips view keeps a card per trip on a phone, because the table under `md` would have dropped status, plate and route.
  - **Found, not fixed, because each is logic:** Dokumente's archive read ignores its error, so a failed read shows "Keine gespeicherten Stundennachweise vorhanden"; the Stundennachweis driver read does the same (an empty driver list); `useFahrtenbuch.fetchImports` throws a failed read into callers that do not catch it. Each needs its read to keep the error, which a look-only batch does not do.
  - **Rendered, and how.** `design-harness/batch3/` adds a company with payroll on invented data — planning-ready drivers, a month of Fahrtenbuch rides per import, an applied planning run for August 2026 with its rides assigned, older runs finalized and stale, saved Stundennachweise for July and August, notes, two Gehaltsquittungen — kept out of the shared fixtures, with hook lines only in `main.tsx`, `fakes/supabase.ts` and `capture.mjs`. The fake query builder gained what these screens call: every `.order()`, `.range()` that really pages (the `while (true)` loops stop on a short page), counted selects, `gt/gte/lt/lte/is/not/or`, and a record of every write and RPC on a batch 3 screen. `capture.mjs` gained a per-checkout Vite cache (a shared `node_modules/.vite` let one worktree's run reload another's page), longer first-load timeouts, and several `--only` parts; the harness serves Barlow through a junctioned `node_modules`. **51 before and 51 after shots** in `Docs/design/phase5/batch3/`: the three pages at 375, 768 and 1280 in both themes, Phase 1's form in all six, and states at 1280 light and 375 dark — an empty archive, both delete dialogs, the Fahrtenbuch view, the historical choice, Phase 1's blocks with the driver pool and the assignment preview's opening state, a run's delete dialog, the Stundennachweis preview of an applied run, an empty planner, both steps and both tabs, an archived sheet — plus English. **Rendered by the product's own code on invented data:** Phase 1's blocks and the Stundennachweis preview's figures. **Not rendered:** Phase 3's assignment and anything after it, the finalization dialog, the run's export dialog, the two historical wizards, and the Trinkgeld, Mietwagen, Gehaltsquittung and import dialogs — all legacy or already converted, none opened. `node design-harness/flows-batch3.mjs`: 20 checks that only draw — dialogs open and close, tabs and steps switch, the links between the screens, Phase 1's four fields correcting a value as before, Phase 1 and a Stundennachweis generated in memory, every control at least 40 px on a 375 px touch screen — and each ends by proving it sent no insert, update, delete or RPC. Its first run found DataTable's sort buttons at 20 px on a touch screen (every sortable table, batch 1's too); fixed.
  - **Ratchets, against staging after batch 2 and #102.** Style: **6257 → 5871 inline styles and 3518 → 3402 colours** (DocumentsPage 128/13, the driver pool 16/11 and the trips view 77/2 to zero and pinned there by the test; CompanyPlannerPage 96/48 → 29/15, StundennachweisPage 224/91 → 154/71 and AppliedRuns 67/74 → 39/37, the rest being the legacy blocks above). Typecheck **223 → 221**: the trips view's conversion removed an unused type and a `title` that `Lock` does not take; the 17 errors in the two big pages are the same 17.
  - **Contrast pairs added:** `caution` as text on `sunken`, both themes (a run's exception note on a row's hover; 5.49:1 light, 9.86:1 dark). Its other two words on `sunken` — the delete labels in `critical`, a reassigned driver in `brand-text` — are batch 2's pairs, which landed first.
  - **For the next batch — copy:** the proof script (copy it, give it your files and ranges, and run its negative control); `NativeSelect` wherever a select's `onChange` carries logic; a dialog that must stay open while it works is a `Dialog`, never `ConfirmDialog`; keep a figure's formatting call verbatim unless moving it to `utils/money.ts` is the point, and then list it. **Avoid:** a flow selector that matches a name as a substring (a row that is a button contains its actions' names); trusting a proof before a negative control.
- **Phase 5 batch 4: the sign-in and account screens, Benutzer, Rollen, Einstellungen and Abonnement on the design system (PR #101, in review, branch `phase5/batch4-benutzer-rollen-settings-abo-login`), what to know:**
  - **What moved.** `LoginForm`, forgot password, verify email, set password, `/einladung`, signup with `PlanSelection`, the Google registration screen, `/auth/callback`, the four takeovers (blocked, pending, rejected, suspended — one frame now, `components/auth/AccountStatusPage`), `UsersPage` + `UserManagement` + `BackupManagement`, `RolesPage` + `RolesManagement` + `RoleModal`, `SettingsPage`, `ActivationPage` (`/abo-aktivieren`, `/aktivierung`) and `CompanyBillingPage`, with the shared auth pieces (`AuthShell`, `NoticeBox`, `OtpChallenge`, `OtpCodeInput`, `GoogleButton`, the auth class names) and a token variant of `LanguageSwitcher`. New primitive: `MessageDialog` (the legacy modal's success/error/info). **The signed-out screens follow the theme now** (owner's answer, 22.09); App.tsx's own spinner and "profile unavailable" screens are still the old dark look, because App.tsx was not touched.
  - **Look only, proved by a script.** `node design-harness/batch4/logic-identity.mjs` passes for all 28 converted files that hold logic (the new frame and class files hold none): App.tsx, the four contexts, `src/lib` and `src/hooks` untouched; every listed logic block verbatim; every call the old file made (`signIn`, `requestOtp`, `completeSignIn`, every `manage-users` fetch with its body, `redeem_activation_code`, `navigate`…) present with the same arguments; every behaving attribute (`required`, `disabled={…}`, `onClick={…}`, `type`, `value`…) present, and the few that moved named with their reason (the takeovers' support link into their shared frame, the confirm type into ConfirmDialog, two hand-painted focus colours, two `stopPropagation`s Radix does itself). Proved to fail on a changed call, a changed handler and a lost `required`.
  - **Presentation changes, named.** In a platform read context Einstellungen is a disabled fieldset under a note (owner's answer, 22.09) — it was an editable form whose save the database refuses since 3.13a; it reads `companyScope.readOnly`, which the file already read. Benutzer's desktop table offers exactly what it did, and the phone cards what they did: reset password and the "Passwortänderung erforderlich" badge stay phone-only (owner's answer: adding them to the desktop is a function, not a look — see *Waiting on the owner*). The Einstellungen labels, the activation screen's sentences and the status word's "Aktiv"/"Inaktiv" come from the dictionaries now. RoleModal's permission boxes are real checkboxes a keyboard reaches (they were hidden inputs under painted squares). Every icon button names its row.
  - **One behaviour kept on purpose, and a defect.** PlanSelection's "Anwenden" and remove-code buttons named no `type`, so inside the Google registration form they are submit buttons: pressing "Anwenden" there has always submitted the registration too. The design-system Button defaults to `type="button"`, so the conversion asks for `type="submit"` explicitly to change nothing. Fixing it is the owner's (below).
  - **e2e selectors, all kept** (the suite runs on staging after the merge): on `/login` one `input[type="email"]`, one `input[type="password"]`, one `form button[type="submit"]`, one heading named "Anmelden" (the page's h1 now, it was the card's h2), one button named exactly "Anmelden", "Mit Google anmelden" rendered only when GoTrue says so; on forgot password the button "Code senden" with no other button containing those words on that step, the notice "Falls ein Konto zu dieser Adresse existiert", the boxes labelled "Ziffer 1 von 6", the refusal "Noch N Versuche."; on set password two password fields in the order new, confirm, the button "Passwort speichern" and the heading "Passwort gespeichert"; the staging banner untouched. `flows-batch4.mjs` checks each on the harness.
  - **Rendered, and how.** `design-harness/batch4/`, a harness of its own (the shared one was being extended by other batches): `npm run harness:batch4`, `harness:batch4:capture -- --set before|after`, `harness:batch4:flows`. Before and after shots in `Docs/design/phase5/batch4/{before,after}/`. The before set was taken in this branch before any screen changed, from the same harness and Chrome.
  - **Rebased onto the merged staging (`9d7b052`), and what that meant.** The two raw `<select>`s this batch had styled by hand — Benutzer's role, Einstellungen' drivers per car — are batch 3's `NativeSelect` now; the element, its `onChange` and its ids are unchanged, and `Field` labels it like an `Input`. #97's Impressum and Datenschutz links stay on `/login` and `/signup`, in the `AuthShell` footer, and take `LegalLinks`' default `theme` tone instead of `onDark`, because these screens follow the theme since the owner's answer of 22.09 — `onDark` paints dark-theme text on a light page. The proof script gained a check that the element is still rendered (with a negative control) and lists the tone change as declared. #102's `handleEditUser` fix arrived inside a block this batch keeps verbatim, so the merge carried it and the proof reads 564 of 1268 base lines. Batch 4 keeps its own harness under `design-harness/batch4/`, so batch 1b's fix to the shared resolver does not replace its copy of the same rule.
  - **Gates (22.09).** `npm run build` passes, with vitest at 2035 passed and 7 skipped. `logic-identity.mjs`: all checks pass. `flows-batch4.mjs`: 55/55. **A trap on a Windows machine with python3 installed:** the seven python-backed tests in `tests/deploy/google-signin-guards.test.ts` then run instead of skipping. Two of them fail, because Windows python ends its lines with `\r\n` and the tests compare deploy.sh's output byte for byte. That is an environment effect, not a regression: the host is Linux. Run the gates with python off `PATH` to get the usual 7 skips.
  - **Ratchets.** Style: 6607 → 6133 inline styles, 3774 → 3228 colours, 107 → 82 files; three files keep a count on purpose (Google's own colours in GoogleButton, RoleModal's scroll lock, SettingsPage's logo fallback and default brand colours in its form data), pinned by `style-ratchet.test.ts`. Typecheck 226 → 224 (two unused icon imports went with the old Einstellungen markup).
  - **Contrast.** 202 → 227 cases. New: the chosen plan card and the price box's tones; the one-time-code block on Mein Konto's legacy card (`--bg-surface`); the staging banner's label on its orange and Google's button in its light and dark colours, both read from the component source. The list caught one real miss before it shipped: the plan card's price unit was `fg-subtle` on `brand-soft`, 4.38:1 in dark; it is `fg-muted`.
  - **Left legacy, and why.** App.tsx's `AuthLoadingScreen` and `ProfileUnavailableScreen` (the brief: App.tsx untouched). `AccountPage` (Mein Konto; not in this batch) — it renders the converted one-time-code block inside its legacy card. `EmphasizedText` keeps its `style` prop, which Mein Konto still passes. The landing page and `LandingPage`'s own language switcher. A failed Benutzer list load still leaves an empty list and zero counts behind its error dialog, and a failed Abonnement read an empty history: neither screen keeps an error state, so the look cannot tell "failed" from "none" without a logic change.
- **Phase 5, the frame: `AppLayout` on the design system (PR #116, branch `phase5/applayout`), what to know:**
  - **What moved, and how little it shows.** Seven files: `AppLayout` itself, `Sidebar`, `UserMenu`, `TrialBanner`, `AnnouncementBanner`, `SupportSessionBar` and `SupportReasonModal`. The frame paints `bg-canvas` on `min-h-dvh` (never `h-screen`) where it painted the legacy `--bg-base`. **Those two are the same colour in light** (#f4f6fa / #F4F6FA) and differ by a hair in dark (#0b0f17 / #0A0F1C), so nothing under the frame moves: every converted screen's header, tabs and notes keep the ratios they were measured at. The sidebar's active row is the design system's navigation fill (`bg-brand-soft` / `text-brand-text`) instead of the old filled accent pill, and the trial banner's seven-day CTA is the brand fill because the design system has no `caution` fill for a white label.
  - **Look only, proved and controlled.** `node design-harness/identity-proof.mjs . <base> <the seven files>`: **every logic region IDENTICAL** except two declared moves — AppLayout's new docblock (14 comment lines) and `AnnouncementBanner`'s `TONE_STYLE`, which crossed the presentation marker to become class names. All 12 "missing" inline handlers are imperative hover painters (`el.style.background = 'var(--bg-muted)'` and friends), now `hover:` classes; every other handler and every logic call is present verbatim. **Negative control run:** changing `NAV_ITEMS`' werkstatt `feature` to `overview` and deleting one `onClick` made the tool report both, and it reported neither again after the revert.
  - **The contrast blocks: one re-aimed, one untouched, none deleted.** `ON_LEGACY_PAGE` measures Phase-2 foregrounds on `--bg-base`, which was AppLayout's. It stays, because **`AdminLayout` still paints `--bg-base`** (line 89) under `PlatformTeamPage`, `PlatformSettingsPage` and the settings editors, which are built from `components/ui` — so all six pairs are still rendered, in the panel instead of the tenant shell. Its docblock and two examples now say so; it retires when AdminLayout moves. `ON_LEGACY_SURFACE` is **not** the frame's and was not touched: its background is the card `AccountPage` paints itself, and Mein Konto is still legacy. One pair added: `critical` on `field` at 3:1 — the support-session reason framed critical when it is refused, which is `Input`'s own `aria-[invalid=true]:border-critical` reaching a third state.
  - **What is deliberately still legacy.** `AdminLayout` and `AdminSidebar` (out of scope, and they own the panel's own frame). `LanguageSwitcher`: it is shared by both shells, so its `default` variant is untouched and AppLayout asks for the `variant="ui"` that already existed — only its docblock changed. `EnvironmentBanner`: the staging banner is not a child of this frame at all (it is fixed to the viewport, mounted above the router) and its two hex colours are read out of its source by `tokens.contrast.test.ts`, so touching it would fail that test on purpose. **`SupportSessionBar` and `SupportReasonModal` could not be left behind** — AdminLayout renders the bar too, so the panel's copy changes with the tenant shell's; the change is small because legacy `--warning` and Phase-2 `--caution` are the same colour (#8F5200).
  - **Six screens inside the frame were never converted** and now sit on `--canvas`: `OverviewPage` (169 inline styles), `NotesPage` (50), `WerkstattPage` (39), `NotificationsPage` (26), `AccountPage` (19), `LockedFeaturePage` (13). Three are registered in this batch's harness so the seam could be looked at rather than assumed.
  - **Rendered.** `design-harness/applayout/` plus `shots-applayout.mjs`; `node design-harness/capture.mjs --set before|after --batch applayout`, shots in `Docs/design/phase5/applayout/{before,after}/`. **`--root` swaps the whole harness, not just `src/`**, because `configFile` is read from that root — so the before set was taken from a base checkout extracted with `git archive`, with this batch's harness copied in and a `node_modules` junction. Also worth knowing: `scenario.ts` falls back to Fahrzeuge for a screen name it does not know (hence this batch reading `screen` from the query string itself), and `settle()` waits for `window.__harness.answered > 0`, so a screen that issues no query hangs.
  - **Gates.** Style **5002 → 4939 inline styles, 2784 → 2780 colours, 68 → 63 files**; the seven files are at zero inline styles, and `Sidebar` and `SupportReasonModal` keep one colour each — the scrim literal `bg-[rgb(5_8_16/0.55)]`, copied from `Dialog.tsx`, which carries the same one. Typecheck **213, unmoved**. Vitest 2315 tests, all passing; the `tests/deploy/google-signin-guards.test.ts` failures on this Windows box are batch 4's recorded python/`\r\n` effect — 8 with python on `PATH`, 0 with it off, and this branch changes nothing under `deploy/`.
  - **For whoever moves `AdminLayout`:** it is the last legacy frame, and moving it is what retires `ON_LEGACY_PAGE` and frees `LanguageSwitcher`'s `default` variant to be deleted. Both are noted where they live.
- **Bargeldbestand on the cash ledger (PR #43, plan 4.3b), what to know:**
  - `BargeldbestandPage` and the Overview cash panel read `cash_movements` through `utils/cashLedger.ts`. Both establish scope before making any claim: `cash_movements` and `salary_cash_receipts` are readable by the owning company only, so platform staff inside a customer read that customer's Bargeld and Belege and none of its movements. An empty set there is indistinguishable from "no payouts", so out of scope the payout card is dropped, the older "Bargeld − Belege" label kept, and the caveat carried into the Excel dump. Printing 0,00 € would have turned a missing read into a false claim.
  - Five defects from the review, all fixed before merge: a literal minus prefixed to an already-signed number (a receipt paid in one month and voided in the next rendered "−-300,00 €"); none of the three queries paginated, and the largest staging company already holds 836 receipts against PostgREST's silent 1000-row cap, so the balance would have under-reported cash with no failure signal; `calcCashSummary` had no test; an unknown `source_type` made real cash vanish from the balance rather than merely be miscategorised; and the two new cards rendered a decimal point instead of the German comma.
  - **What the merge gate proved and what it did not.** Staging holds zero cash movements, zero receipts and zero Gehaltsquittung payments across 19 companies, so every comparison in the parity script had a third term of exactly zero: it proved the two formulas equivalent and never exercised the divergence.
  - **The seeded check that does exercise it has now run on staging**, inside a transaction that ended in `ROLLBACK`, acting as the e2e test company's own admin under RLS rather than as a superuser: a driver, a salary of 300,00 € for March 2026, `confirm_salary_cash_payment` for 300,00 € dated 15.03.2026, then `void_salary_cash_receipt`. The payment produced receipt `GQ-2026-0001` and exactly one outgoing movement of 300,00 € in 2026-03; the void marked the receipt `voided` and produced one incoming movement of 300,00 € in 2026-09 — today's month, because a storno is booked on the day of the void. Net per month: 2026-03 is −300,00 €, 2026-09 is +300,00 €. The documented cash-book rule therefore holds against the live RPCs, not only in theory.
  - **Its limit:** that verified the database side — the RPCs write the movements with the right month attribution. It did not verify the screen, which rests on the four `calcCashSummary` tests PR #43 added, covering the same month boundary and the void landing in the next month. Nothing was left behind on staging, so a check against the rendered Bargeldbestand figures would still need data seeded and kept.
- **A cash salary payment is now a Gehaltsquittung (PR #69, plan 4.3d), what to know:**
  - **The defect it fixes is a ledger one, not a missing button.** `SalaryDetailsModal` inserted into `salary_payments` directly, so a payment added with `payment_type: 'cash'` wrote **no `cash_movements` row** — the money left the till and the Bargeldbestand did not move. The three Gehaltsquittung RPCs are the only writers of that ledger. The row also had no receipt, so `paymentRowActions` (which keys on `source_type = 'gehaltsquittung'`) had nothing to hang PR #49's preview button on. Adding a cash payment now hands the salary, the amount and the date to the existing `GehaltsquittungModal`, preselected and opening on its **amount** step, where the balance and the *Ort der Zahlung* are; the confirmation writes all three rows in one transaction and the lock, the badge and the preview appear on the new row by themselves.
  - **The existing dialog is opened, not copied.** `useGehaltsquittungFlow` holds the request id kept until the database answers, its resume out of `sessionStorage` after a reload, the pre-send balance re-read, and the `overlapping_salary` / `overpayment` / `request_id_conflict` refusals. A second client for `confirm_salary_cash_payment` would have to keep all of that identical, and the failure mode of drift is a payment booked twice. The one new option is `preselect`; **an unanswered confirmation still wins over it**, because that one may already be booked.
  - **The rule is keyed on the origin of the write, never on `payment_type` alone**, and that is the part not to touch. The weekly "Bar zurück" (`weekly_cash_difference`) is also stored as cash, and CLAUDE.md is explicit that it counts as paid and never moves cash — a rule of "type is cash → receipt" would book a payout from a till that was never opened. `src/utils/salaryPaymentWrite.ts` is that decision, pure and tested without a database; `handleWeeklyCashPayment` asks it too and throws if it ever answers anything but `insert`.
  - **A stored cash row is frozen in its amount and its payment type**, and the form disables both fields rather than refusing on submit. The review of this PR found the hole: `paymentRowActions` gives the pencil to every non-`gehaltsquittung` row, so a legacy cash payment of 100,00 € could be edited to 5.000,00 € — 4.900,00 € more asserted as paid in cash with no ledger row anywhere, the same defect through a different door, reachable on any legacy row by anyone with salaries-edit. Date and description carry no cash claim and stay editable. The way to correct such a row is to delete it and record it again, which now creates the receipt and moves the till, and the message says exactly that (`sal.payment.cashRowLocked`) — there is nothing to void, because the row predates receipts.
  - **Nothing is backfilled, and what that leaves standing is worth stating plainly: every cash payment recorded before 17.09.2026 still asserts money that left the till with no `cash_movements` row behind it, and no migration corrects them.** The Bargeldbestand of any company that recorded cash salary payments before this release is therefore high by the sum of those rows, permanently. They keep their row and their delete button; they get no retroactive receipt. The other door into an untracked cash payment — create it as a bank transfer, then edit its type to cash — was open before this PR and is now refused by name (`sal.payment.cashNeedsReceipt`), because `confirm_salary_cash_payment` writes its own row and cannot adopt one.
  - **A salary whose period overlaps another row of the same driver can now take no cash payment at all, and that is a deliberate capability regression.** Before this PR such a payment was a plain INSERT and succeeded; now it is a Gehaltsquittung, and `confirm_salary_cash_payment` refuses an overlapping salary (`overlapping_salary`). **The owner decided on 17.09.2026 that the refusal stays**: he merges the two rows by hand before paying, and the system keeps refusing until he has. So there is no fallback path and none should be added. What the PR does instead is make the refusal actionable — it is shown in the payment form **before the form closes**, beside the type select while a bank transfer is still one click away, and it **names the conflicting row**: its period and its due amount, because one staging company holds 16 overlapping pairs and another holds 25 salary rows in one company, where "these rows overlap" is not something anyone can act on. A bank transfer is unaffected.
  - **The overlap rule is spelled once, and both screens read it.** PR #71 merged the same day with `src/utils/salaryPeriodOverlap.ts`, the browser's mirror of the RPC's condition, and marks overlapping rows on the salaries list; its own commit message noted that `findOverlappingSalary` in `salaryCashReceiptFlow.ts` made the same comparison and that folding the two together was a follow-up. This PR is that follow-up: `findOverlappingSalary` is now the single-row form of `findOverlappingSalaryPeriods` and holds no comparison of its own, so **the row the payment form names is the first row the salaries badge lists**, decided by one function. Both agree that a **shared boundary day is an overlap** — the RPC compares with `<=` and `>=` — and that two consecutive periods (one ending the day before the next begins) are not; `salaryCashReceiptFlow.test.ts` pins both, because two spellings of one rule is how the two screens come to tell the user different things about what the database will accept.
  - **No SQL.** Nothing under `supabase/migrations/` or `tests/rls/`, so the kit needs no run for this branch. Typecheck ratchet unchanged at 228; vitest 1460 passed.
  - **Not verified:** nothing was run against staging or any database. The end-to-end behaviour is the code path PR #34 and PR #43 already exercise, but this entry point into it has not been clicked through.
- **Merging overlapping salary rows (PR #73), what to know:**
  - The other half of #71. The owner merges the rows himself, so the Gehälter screen now **deletes a salary row**; before this no screen did, and "by hand" meant a direct database edit while `confirm_salary_cash_payment` went on refusing every cash payment for the 5 drivers of the one production company that holds 16 overlapping pairs. The pair is a hand-entered calendar month beside the business month the app computes: **deleting the hand-entered one is the whole merge.**
  - **Editing a period was built and then removed, and it must not come back without reading why.** The full reasoning is in the header of `src/utils/salaryRowEdit.ts`; the first reason alone is fatal. `SalariesManagement` matches a driver's stored row to the selected period by **exact equality of both ends**, so a row widened to `01.03.–31.03.` no longer matches the computed business range `02.03.–29.03.`: `calc.salary_id` goes undefined, the table falls back to a recomputed `amount_due`, and the stored `netto_salary`, `total_paid`, `paid_at` and `status` vanish from the screen — a part-paid driver is redrawn as *unpaid, 0,00 paid*. The badge then **stays** (the widened row overlaps the computed range with no id to exclude) and the next click on *Zahlungen verwalten* runs `ensureSalaryExists` and inserts a **third** row. The widen would have undone itself and left a fresh overlap, sixteen times over. Three more: a widened start crossing a week boundary re-files issued Gehaltsquittungen under a different `business_month` while the Overview still filters by calendar month; the due figure is never recomputed, so the receipt the driver signs would state a period the amount was never computed for; and nothing stops a widen storing a *new* overlap, there being no CHECK and no exclusion constraint.
  - **A false warning was removed with it.** The delete confirmation used to say "no other period covers this one — those days will be in no salary record" whenever no single remaining row contained the deleted period. That is false for exactly the case it appeared in: a week belongs to the month containing its Monday, so `01.03.2026` belongs to business **February** and `30.–31.03.` to business **April**, and the adjacent rows already hold them. That warning is what would have pushed the owner into widening. The confirmation now names the periods that remain and claims nothing about coverage.
  - **No migration, and that was established before any UI was written.** `20260917130000` already gives `salaries` all four policies with `current_role_permits('salaries', true)` gating DELETE as well as INSERT, and the RLS kit already pins it: `matrix.sql:1210` has `salaries` `D` at `'Y.'` for the editor, supervisor and company administrator and `'..'` for anon, the viewer and a platform account. Grants are argued from those cells plus the `salaries` UPDATE `SalaryDetailsModal.tsx:273` already runs in production — **not** from a live database, which nothing in this PR touched.
  - **Where it lives and why.** A new `SalaryPeriodsModal`, on the Gehälter screen, reached from the amber badge and from the actions cell — not in `SalaryDetailsModal`. The table shows one line per driver for the *selected* period and the overlapping row is usually outside it, so a merge needs both sides at once; the dialog lists every row of the one driver out of the rows the screen already loaded (the `groupSalaryPeriodsByDriver` index #71 built is now its own `useMemo`, read by both). And opening `SalaryDetailsModal` calls `ensureSalaryExists`, which *creates* a row.
  - **Deleting refuses two ways, and the second is the one the database does not make for us.** A `salary_cash_receipts` row blocks it for good (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`, pinned as 23503 by `tests/rls/salary-cash.sql:361`) — and the check reads the **receipts**, not the payments, because a *voided* receipt keeps its row while its payment is gone, so reading payments alone would offer a delete the database answers with a bare 23503. Ordinary `salary_payments` block it too, and there the application is the only guard: that foreign key is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so the database would take them away silently.
  - **The decision is re-taken against a fresh read of that one row's children immediately before the DELETE**, not against the map loaded when the dialog opened — otherwise a payment recorded in another tab while the dialog sat open would be cascaded away and reported as a clean success. **This narrows the window, it does not close it:** a gap remains between that read and the DELETE, and only a `SECURITY DEFINER` function checking and deleting in one transaction would remove it. That is a migration and an owner decision, deliberately not slipped into a screen change. Both child reads go through `fetchAllPaginated`, because a truncated payment list on a destructive path reads as "fewer payments hang off this row than really do".
  - **Zero rows back from the DELETE is read, not trusted**, because a refused DELETE reports no error — and the row is looked for before the caller is blamed, so "someone else already deleted it" is not reported as a permission problem. The gate is `salaryRowWriteRefusal(receiptScope(...), can('salaries','edit'))`, the two terms the policy carries; the scope term is **not** redundant, because `AuthContext.can` returns true for every platform account while `get_active_company_id()` is NULL for one.
  - **The payments refusal names the row's own period and says to select it first**, because *Zahlungen verwalten* opens the payments of the period selected on the screen behind the dialog, not of the row named in the message.
  - `src/utils/salaryRowEdit.ts` is the pure half, 19 tests. Rebased onto `2aa0795` (after #72). Ratchet unchanged at 228; vitest 58 files, 1689 passed, 7 skipped. **No SQL, so the kit needs no run for this branch.**
  - **Not verified: the screen was never rendered.** There is no component test harness (`vitest.config.ts` is `environment: 'node'` and includes `.test.ts` only), and the branch was built under an instruction not to touch the VPS or any database, so neither a staging click-through nor a local run against real data happened. **Clicking it through on staging, on the e2e test company and not a real customer, is the first thing to do after the deploy** — including the 23503 message mapping, which has never seen a real PostgreSQL response.
- **A Gehaltsquittung's number carries the salary's year (owner decision 17.09.2026, PR #75), what to know:**
  - It answers the open question above. `confirm_salary_cash_payment` keyed its counter on `EXTRACT(year FROM p_payment_date)`; migration `20260919160000` keys it on `v_byear` instead — **one assignment**, and `v_byear` is already sitting there two statements above, from `get_business_month(v_salary.period_start)`.
  - **Which year, and the evidence.** The business year of `period_start`, not its calendar year. Rule 7 of `20260914140000` already says "The receipt's month is `get_business_month(period_start)`"; the row already stores that answer as `business_month` / `business_year` and indexes the archive on `(company_id, driver_id, business_year, business_month)`. The business month is this product's month everywhere — `get_business_month()` in SQL, `utils/weekCalculations.ts` in the browser, `abrechnung_weekly`, the Stundennachweis, the Monatsplanung. Reusing it means the number's year and `business_year` are the same integer **by construction**, so they can never disagree; `period_start`'s calendar year would have been a second convention for one field, differing whenever a period starts in a week that began in the previous year.
  - **Existing receipts are not renumbered.** No UPDATE, no backfill, no counter touched. A Gehaltsquittung is a ledger document that has been printed and signed. So **both conventions live in the history**: before this release the payment's year, after it the salary's. They differ only for a salary paid in a year other than the one it belongs to. `business_year` on the row is correct either way and is what a report should group by. Production holds very few receipts and staging one test receipt, but say it rather than let it be found.
  - **What the counter's uniqueness means now.** `document_counters` is unchanged — `PRIMARY KEY (company_id, doc_type, year)`, incremented under its own row lock inside the confirming transaction, unreachable from every API role. Only the integer handed to `year` changes meaning. Gaplessness is now per (company, **salary** year): two receipts issued on one day for salaries of different years draw from different counters, that being the point. `salary_cash_receipts_number_unique UNIQUE (company_id, receipt_number)` and the `^GQ-[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{4,}$` CHECK need no change, the year being inside the number. **Two consequences the decision carries:** a company's numbers are no longer issued in the chronological order of its payments, and a closed year can be reopened by a late payment, still gaplessly.
  - **Nothing else reads `GQ-YYYY` expecting the payment year.** The browser never predicts a number — `salaryCashReceiptDocument.ts` prints *Nr. wird bei der Bestätigung vergeben* until the database answers — and no list, filter or export parses the year out of one. The one near miss is `signedCopyPath()` (`utils/salaryCashReceiptFlow.ts`), which files an uploaded signed copy under `{company}/{payment year}/{number}_…`; a `GQ-2025-…` copy may now sit in a `2026/` folder. **Left alone on purpose** — it is a storage folder, not a number, existing `signed_copy_path` values are absolute, and moving it would change where uploads land for nothing.
  - **The body is rewritten from the catalogue, not retyped**, the precedent `20260917130000` set for these three functions: the regexp must match exactly once or the migration fails without changing anything, and `CREATE OR REPLACE` keeps the owner, the pinned `search_path`, `SECURITY DEFINER` and the ACL. The owner is deliberately **not** moved to `CURRENT_USER`: on the live databases these belong to `postgres`, 3.3 chose a grant over an ownership change for exactly these three, and this adds no call `postgres` cannot already reach. The self-check asserts the year comes from the salary, that the assignment is read before the number is built, and that thirteen load-bearing fragments of the function (the 3.3 state gate, the role gate, both advisory locks, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, idempotency, the overlap refusal, …) are still there — restating a function here has already lost a rule twice.
  - Backward compatible, **no `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump**: a rollback leaves the new function in place and the previous release reads `receipt_number` as an opaque string.
  - Kit files: `supabase/migrations/20260919160000_gehaltsquittung_number_salary_year.sql` and `tests/rls/salary-cash.sql`. **The kit was not run from this branch** (no Docker here, and the branch was built under an instruction not to touch the VPS or any database) — run `npm run test:rls` before merging.
- **The Gehaltsquittung prints its Abrechnungsmonat (owner decision 18.09.2026, PR #78), what to know:**
  - **Why it exists.** With #75 the number's `GQ-YYYY` is the salary's business year. The sheet printed only `periodStart`, `periodEnd` and `paymentDate`, all three of which read 2026 for a salary whose period starts Thursday 01.01.2026 — so `GQ-2025-…` was the only "2025" on a paper going to the Steuerberater, and read as a mistake rather than as a rule. The receipt now carries **Abrechnungsmonat: Dezember 2025** beside its Zeitraum, and the number explains itself. No SQL, no migration, no change to how the number is built.
  - **The rule it preserves: a confirmed receipt prints the row the database stored.** `salary_cash_receipts.business_month` / `.business_year` were written by `confirm_salary_cash_payment` since `20260914140000` and were simply never selected; `RECEIPT_COLUMNS` (`src/lib/salaryCashReceipts.ts`) now reads them and `storedReceiptDocumentInput` is the only thing that fills `ReceiptDocumentInput.businessMonth` for a confirmed or voided receipt. Nothing recomputes it — a value derived in the browser would let a later change to the calendar rule reprint an issued document under a month other than the one it was numbered by. `salaryPaymentReceiptRow.test.ts` pins that with a row whose stored pair (12/2025) is one the period alone (01.01.2026–25.01.2026) would not obviously produce.
  - **The preview is the one place it is computed**, because no row exists yet, and it computes it from the same rule the RPC uses: `businessMonthOfPeriod()` → `getBusinessMonth()` (`utils/weekCalculations.ts`), the browser twin of `get_business_month()`. **`weekCalculations` counts months from 0 and the stored column from 1**; the `+ 1` happens once, in that one function, and a test asserts the two agree for five dates including 01.01.2026. The date is read as local calendar parts, never `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")`, which is UTC midnight and falls back a day west of Greenwich — that would move the week, and with it the month, for every period starting on a Monday.
  - **Wording and placement, and what they cost.** The label is `Abrechnungsmonat`, the product's own German for this (nothing in `src/` named the concept before), the value a German month name and year — hard-coded German like the rest of the sheet, since the Gehaltsquittung is on the closed statutory list in `src/i18n/index.ts`. It sits in the fact row **between Zeitraum and Zahlungsdatum**, the two dates it is derived from and paid against. That row is four equal flex boxes now instead of three, so **`Zeitraum` wraps onto two lines** at its en-dash (682 px of content cannot hold a date range and three more boxes on one line, at any split). Rendered and looked at; it reads cleanly and the sheet has room to spare.
  - **The list has an `Abrechnungsmonat` column too** (`GehaltsquittungList`), `hideBelow: 'lg'`, after the period and before *Gezahlt am*, sorted by `business_year * 12 + business_month`. It reads the stored columns like the sheet does. It is a screen, not the document, so its header follows the UI language — German `Abrechnungsmonat`, English `Business month` — and the month name comes from the existing `doc.month.*` keys. Only the sheet is German in both.
  - **Not verified: the list was never rendered.** Same reason as #73 above — no component harness, and no VPS or database. The *document* was rendered: `GehaltsquittungSheet` through `react-dom/server` and headless Chrome, for a January 2026 period numbered `GQ-2025-0004` and an August one numbered `GQ-2026-0012`. Click the list through on staging after the deploy.
  - Ratchet unchanged at 228; `npx vitest run` 58 files, 1749 passed, 7 skipped; `npm run build` clean. **No SQL, so the kit needs no run for this branch.**
- **Follow-ups from 3.9 (PR #38), none blocking:**
  - ~~`platform_settings_get` writes an audit row per call with no rate limit~~ — closed by PR #44 (migration `20260917120000`): settings audit rows are capped at 120 per actor per minute, and the rate-limit helper now fails closed on a NULL actor instead of answering "not limited".
  - ~~The `plan_features` validator accepts a partial map~~ — closed by PR #44: an incomplete map is refused.
  - `AppConfigContext` reads the price book once per session, so a long session quotes stale prices.
  - The fail-closed rule (a feature the map does not name needs premium) and both parsers have no vitest coverage. PR #44 added a provider test that demonstrably fails against the unfixed code, but it covers the reload path, not this rule.
  - Two migrations share the prefix `20260916150000` (3.5's allowlist, 3.9's price book). Ordering is deterministic by filename and nothing is load-bearing; give each later slice its own prefix.
- **Audit allowlist, before merging 3.9 (PR #38):**
  - PR #39 ships `supabase/migrations/20260916150000_platform_audit_record_allowlist.sql`, which sets the allowed audit namespaces to `platform_staff|legacy|auth|platform_read`.
  - 3.9 adds `platform_settings`. Its migration must extend that allowlist to all five namespaces, never redefine the function from an older body, or it silently reopens forged audit rows.
  - 3.9 also has to move off the `20260916150000` prefix, which PR #39 now uses.
  - **Checked again after PR #44 merged**, read-only on staging: all three rules of the 3.5 audit guard are intact — the action allow-list, the reserved namespaces, and the 120-per-minute cap. Keep checking this after any migration that touches `platform_audit_record`; PR #38 came within one review of silently reverting the guard by redefining the function from an older body.
- **Settings hardening (PR #44, plan slice 3.9), what to know:**
  - Migration `20260917120000` bounds the settings audit rows at 120 per actor per minute, refuses an incomplete `plan_features` map, and makes the rate-limit helper fail closed on a NULL actor.
  - Four defects the review found, all fixed before merge: a change of signed-in account did not reload the configuration, because the effect watched a two-valued derivation of the user id, so switching between two signed-in accounts never re-ran it; `refresh()` could strand the loading spinner forever; a migration self-check demanded exactly 16 feature keys while the check below it asserted that an extra key is valid, which could abort a release for no reason; and the rate-limit helper returned "not limited" for a NULL actor, a fail-open default in a schema whose rule is the opposite. A provider test was added that demonstrably fails against the unfixed code.
  - Verified after the merge, read-only on staging: the ledger row is present, the NULL-actor branch really does return true, `plan_features` still holds 16 keys, anon can call neither settings RPC, and prices are unchanged and exact (advanced with 8 cars monthly 304,00 €, premium with 12 cars yearly 8.164,80 €). The RLS kit was re-run against the branch after the fixes and passed. e2e 12 passed.
- **Google sign-in screens (PR #45, plan 1.6 PR C), what to know:**
  - The screens ship with the provider **switched off**; `/auth/v1/settings` reports `google:false` in both environments and stays that way.
  - Fixed before merge: the Google error mapping was consulted before the intent check, so any GoTrue failure carrying `error=access_denied` — the shape it sends for a dead mail link — displayed "Die Anmeldung mit Google wurde abgebrochen" and hid the "Neuen Link anfordern" button; a failed profile read skipped the reconcile, the platform-staff refusal and the new registration gate at once, letting a company-less account reach the tenant router; signing out from the waiting page could land on a dead `/auth/callback`; reconcile ran on every page load forever for every Google customer; the button's wrapper still added 20px with the provider off; and an unread route value was dropped. A follow-up replaced the indefinite spinner a failed profile load produced with a recovery screen offering retry and sign-out, behind a bounded retry policy (3 attempts, 400 then 800 ms) that is unit-tested. 126 new vitest cases. e2e 13 passed, 1 skipped — the skip is the Google spec skipping itself while the provider is off, which is the intended assertion.
  - **Recorded honestly, as the PR body now does: the platform-staff refusal is enforced in the browser, not the server.** The database refuses platform staff in the three `oauth-account` actions and nowhere else, so a staff account that completed a Google sign-in would still hold a valid GoTrue JWT until our own JavaScript ended the session. The server-side rule — requiring the session JWT's `amr` claim to name the password method before any platform capability — is deliberately deferred until after slice 3.3 rewrites `is_user_super_admin`, and **Google stays switched off in both environments until it exists.**
- **One Google OAuth client for both environments (owner decision G3, 17.09.2026), what to know:**
  - The owner answered G3: staging and production share one Google client instead of one each. `deploy.sh`'s `google_signin_guard` used to refuse any release where both `.env` files carried the same `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, so with the decision as it stands neither side could release at all.
  - The check is kept and given an explicit opt-in: `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SHARED=true` in **both** `.env` files, so a shared client is a written decision rather than an accident of editing. What it catches is the **partial** mistake: the other environment's id pasted into `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` while no opt-in was written. The opt-in on one side only is refused too, naming that side and whether the key is missing there or merely not `true`, because half a decision is a misconfiguration. Empty or `false` is "not shared", and any other value is refused like the switch — on either side — so a typo never quietly reads as "not shared". The opt-in is read whenever `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` is non-empty, switch or no switch, exactly as the comparison it governs already was; the ids are still compared by hash and nothing is printed.
  - **What it does not catch, and the comment now says so.** Once both files carry the opt-in, this check can no longer tell a deliberate share from a wholesale copy of the other environment's `.env` — the copy brings the opt-in with it and passes. That is not this guard's job: a copied `.env` is refused by the `PROJECT_NAME` guard, which runs before it, and again by `JWT_SECRET`, `SMTP_HOST`, `COMPOSE_PROFILES` and `OTP_PEPPER`. A fourth comparison here would be redundancy in the release path, and the first version of this entry claimed a property the guard does not have.
  - **The risk the owner accepted, written down because it does not show up anywhere else:** staging runs a copy of production's accounts, so one client means one consent screen and one token audience across both environments, and a change to the client's redirect URIs, branding or verification state hits both stacks at once.
  - **This is a code change only.** Nothing was written on the host. Before the first release that uses it, both `.env` files need `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SHARED=true`, and the Google client needs production's `PUBLIC_URL + /auth/v1/callback` added as a second authorised redirect URI. Until both `.env` files carry the key, a shared id refuses the release — which is the guard working, not a regression.
  - **Edit both `.env` files in the same sitting.** Writing the shared id and the opt-in into production's `.env` alone leaves staging holding that id with no opt-in on the peer side, so **staging's** next push-triggered release fails — on an unrelated commit, with a message about a Google client nobody touched. Staging deploys on every push, so the window is however long the second edit is left undone.
  - `deploy/ci-release.sh` is untouched and needed no change: its `compose_fingerprint` reports `GOTRUE_EXTERNAL_*` client ids as "(name only)", so a shared id never moved it, and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SHARED` is a deploy-time guard key that reaches no container, so it is not in the fingerprint at all. Rollback behaviour across this release is unchanged.
- **One policy shape for every tenant table (PR #46, plan slice 3.3), what to know:**
  - Migration `20260917130000` rewrites the RLS policies on every tenant table onto capabilities. It **raises `SCHEMA_EPOCH` from 1 to 2**, so a rollback across this release is refused by design; the way back is the `pg_dump` snapshot deploy.sh takes before any migration runs.
  - `platform_writes_company_data()` returns false, so platform staff cannot write tenant data outside a support session — and support sessions do not exist yet, they are slice 3.4. `current_role_permits` no longer reads `is_user_super_admin`.
  - Two billing tables gained a trigger pinning `company_id`, so an invoice or a subscription row can no longer be re-pointed at another company by anyone.
  - An independent review returned changes_required with one critical and five major findings; all were fixed. A second stream independently found a sixth: three Gehaltsquittung RPCs still read `get_user_company_id()`, so a suspended or lapsed company could still write a receipt and move cash in the ledger while its reads returned nothing. Fixed in `989572a` before the merge.
  - **Verified after the merge:** the RLS kit passed; the per-company parity gate on staging reported 30 profiles and zero mismatches; and a probe on a company with real data showed its admin seeing 10 drivers, 836 receipts and 48 salaries, all from that one company and zero rows from the other 28 drivers' companies, while the platform owner saw all 38.
- **The post-3.3 cleanup (PR #53), what to know:**
  - **The Bargeldbestand ledger guard was widened, not deleted.** 3.3 gave `cash_movements` and `salary_cash_receipts` the SELECT shape every business table carries, which reads the ledger for a company user in their own company **or** for platform staff holding `business.read`. That capability is held by `owner`, `support` and `read_only` — **not by `billing`** — so the read is granted by capability, not by staff status, and deleting the guard outright would have reintroduced exactly the false `0,00 €` that PR #43 shipped it to prevent. The in-scope test is now one pure predicate, named after the database seam, so slice 3.4 changes the same single term on both sides.
  - The four `oauth_*` RPCs that the matrix reported as `UNCOVERED` now have their lines, all recording a refusal for all seven principals: `20260916100020` grants EXECUTE to `service_role` alone, and no matrix principal is the service role.
  - **The RLS kit was run against it before the merge:** all assertions passed, and the four `UNCOVERED` warnings are gone.
  - It changed no function, grant or policy — a browser predicate and test coverage only.
- **A platform account changes its own email without a code (owner's decision, 17.09.2026), what to know:**
  - **Why it is acceptable.** The `email_change` code goes to the **new** address, so it only ever proved control of the new mailbox. An attacker at an unattended signed-in tab picks that address and reads their own code, so the code never was the control against account takeover. That control is the *other* half of the flow: `otp-request` refuses `email_change` unless the session token's `amr` claim names a **password** sign-in no older than `PASSWORD_PROOF_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (600 s, `supabase/functions/_shared/otp-core.ts:45`). **It is enforced, not assumed** — `otp-request/index.ts:104-105` — and the new route enforces the same constant with the same helper. Company users keep the code exactly as it was; `otp-request` and `otp-verify` were not touched.
  - **What is given up, and it is not nothing.** Nothing now proves the new address exists or belongs to anyone. A typo locks the account out of its own sign-in address. The "your address changed" notice still goes to the **old** address, which is the only thing that makes such a mistake visible, and another owner can move the account back through `manage-users`.
  - **Where it lives.** `POST /platform-staff/email-change`, a route on an existing authenticated function rather than a new function: a new function costs an entry in `PUBLIC_FUNCTIONS`/`FUNCTION_ENV` in `supabase/functions/main/index.ts` and one in `deploy/docker-compose.yml`, and `platform-staff` already receives exactly the environment this needs (service role, and SMTP for the notice). It is matched **before** `resolvePlatformStaffRoute` and is deliberately not a row in `PLATFORM_STAFF_ROUTES`, because that table pairs a route with a capability and an action `app_private.platform_edge_audit_actions()` must list — and this route has neither.
  - **The audit row.** `20260919140000` adds `public.platform_self_email_change_check` and `..._record`, both `service_role` only. The row is written by the database through `app_private.audit_write` with category `auth` and action `auth.platform_email_change`, a namespace `public.platform_audit_record` already refuses — so neither rule of that function was weakened, and no staff role can forge the row through the edge functions' door. The migration's self-check proves the refusal by calling it. **It fails closed**, in `auditedPlatformWrite()`'s shape: an `attempt` row before GoTrue is asked to move the address, and 500 `audit_unavailable` without moving it when that row cannot be written; the `ok`/`error` row after is best-effort, because by then the change has happened or failed.
  - **Two guards that look optional and are not** (the review of PR #66 found both missing). `account_blocked`: GoTrue does **not** revoke an issued access token when an account is banned, so a responder who bans a compromised platform account without also disabling its `platform_staff` row would otherwise leave the attacker able to move the address for the rest of the ten-minute window. `rate_limited`: five completed changes per account per hour, because each one mails the notice from the product's Resend domain and an insider could loop `victim@… → throwaway@… → victim@…` at request rate on production's quota. A cooldown was rejected — the owner is the only platform account on production and must be able to correct a typo immediately — and only `ok` rows count, so a failing GoTrue cannot lock him out. The migration's self-check fails a release that loses either.
  - **One eligibility test is deliberately dropped:** `otp_issue` also requires `user_profiles.status = 'active'`, and this route does not. For a platform account the staff row is the authority (`platform_can` never reads the profile, and `platform-staff` writes a company-less `viewer`), so the consequence — a `suspended`-profile platform account can now move its address — is intended and written into the migration header. `platform_staff.disabled_at` is the lever that stops it.
