```bash
         WHERE source = 'invoice' AND status = 'void' AND void_reason = 'replaced')  AS replaced_after;

-- 5. The gap the fix does not close: a first row, granting now, for an invoice
--    older than the company's last manual edit. The sweep grants it whatever
--    that edit said. Every company listed here needs a person's look before
--    the cron line goes in.
SELECT co.name, i.invoice_number, i.status AS invoice_status, i.created_at AS invoice_created,
       m.created_at AS last_manual_edit, m.new_status AS manual_status, c.period_end AS grants_until
  FROM public.subscription_coverage c
  JOIN public.subscription_invoices i ON i.id = c.source_id
  JOIN public.companies co            ON co.id = c.company_id
  JOIN LATERAL (SELECT sa.created_at, sa.new_status
                  FROM public.subscription_admin_actions sa
                 WHERE sa.company_id = c.company_id AND sa.action_type = 'manual_edit'
                 ORDER BY sa.created_at DESC
                 LIMIT 1) m ON true
 WHERE c.source = 'invoice'
   AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM cov_before b WHERE b.id = c.id)
   AND c.status = 'active' AND c.grants_access AND c.period_end > now()
   AND m.created_at > i.created_at
 ORDER BY co.name, i.invoice_number;

ROLLBACK;
SQL
```
- **`OTP_PEPPER`**: provisioned in staging's `.env` on 15.09.2026 (64 hex characters, never printed). Production needs its own, different one before 0.6, or its deploy is refused. That waits for the owner's go. All three OTP streams follow the contract kept at `C:\Users\ahmad\.claude\plans\otp-contract-wf_ce583f7b-774.md`, outside the repo.
- **Staging mail reaches real recipients** (owner's decision, 14.09.2026). Mailpit keeps a copy of every message and relays it through production's Resend account, except mail to `@example.com`. Live on staging since PR #8 (`45ff313`). Resend lists the relayed test mails to `delivered@resend.dev` as `delivered`.
- **Staging mail stays as it is (owner's decision, 16.09.2026).** He asked to "use Resend for both environments", and after the trade was laid out he decided to leave staging alone, explicitly because it supports the tests. The reasoning is written down so this is not re-litigated:
  - **The product never depended on a test tool.** Production already sends through Resend directly (`GOTRUE_SMTP_HOST=smtp.resend.com`). It is staging's GoTrue and edge functions that point at Mailpit, which relays everything to `smtp.resend.com:2587` and blocks only `@example.com`.
  - **The e2e suite has no other way to obtain a one-time code.** `app_private.otp_challenges` stores only `code_hash`, `otp_send_events` keeps metadata alone, and no `otp_*` function returns a code. Take Mailpit away and the suite has nothing to read.
  - **It would also have meant changing `deploy.sh`,** which fails a staging release when `mailpit-relay.yml` is missing, or when relaying runs without the `example.com` block.
  - **~~Still outstanding, and owner-side~~ — done on 16.09.2026.** The `mail-staging.elprofessor.eu` record was added and the mailbox is live: it resolves to `159.195.205.30` through public DNS and answers `401` behind its basic-auth login. The SSH tunnel is no longer the only way in. (A local resolver may still refuse the name — see the gotcha below; the record itself is fine.)
- **Security fix, 15.09.2026.** Anyone holding the public anon key could call `list_users_for_admin()` and read every account's email, name and role.
  - **Why the guard failed:** it reads `IF auth.email() != 'support@…' THEN RAISE`. For an anonymous caller `auth.email()` is NULL, so the comparison is NULL and the RAISE never runs. Confirmed on staging: 15 rows came back.
  - **Closed by hand, with the owner's approval.** On production and staging, EXECUTE was revoked from anon and PUBLIC on `list_users_for_admin`, `create_user_account`, `redeem_discount_code`, `preview_activation_code` and `redeem_activation_code`. Signed-in users keep the activation functions and service_role keeps discount redemption. anon now gets 401.
  - **No sign of use:** Kong's production log starts 13.09.2026 and shows no call to these functions.
  - **Made permanent (PR #15):** migration `20260915110000_revoke_anon_from_unguarded_functions.sql`, which also closes `get_user_status` and `is_user_active` and fails the release if anon can still run any of them.
- **Receipt foundations (PR #13), for whoever builds step 4.3.** The screens wait for the owner's design approval. Carry these review findings into 4.3:
  - `payment_date` has no lower bound and no relation to the salary period.
  - A signed copy can be replaced without keeping the previous path.
  - The receipt month comes from `get_business_month(period_start)`, which mislabels a salary row that does not start on a Monday.
  - The RPCs and storage policies must adopt PLATFORM's server-side block once it exists.
  - When 4.3 ships, add the Gehaltsquittung to the closed list of German-only documents in `src/i18n/index.ts`.
- **Phase 1 on staging (PR #11, #16, #14).** Codes replace the links for signup, "Passwort vergessen", invitations and email changes.
  - GoTrue's public `/signup` is closed and answers 422 `signup_disabled`.
  - Each function receives only its allowlisted variables, and none gets `SUPABASE_DB_URL`.
  - `AuthCallbackPage` and GoTrue's link templates stay for one release, so a link mailed just before still works.
  - A staging rollback across #16 is refused on purpose, because it would reopen signup.
  - Phase 1 is not done until two things happen: the code flows pass on staging (with `@example.com` addresses only), and a real code reaches an owner's mailbox from production.
- **Support sessions (3.4a) leave a work list for 3.4b.** `platform_reads_company_data()` is now a constant `false`: a platform account reaches a company's operational data only through an open context, answered by `get_active_company_id()`. Every screen that read a tenant table with the caller's own JWT therefore reads `[]` — with `error === null`, so nothing throws and nothing warns.
  - **Already handled in 3.4a.** `SuperAdminDashboard` and `CompaniesPage` (list badge and detail drawer) now read the audited models `platform_companies_list` / `platform_users_list`; `AbnahmeDiagnosticPage` reads its run with `maybeSingle()` and reports one warning instead of a cascade of false criticals; `OverviewPage` and `BargeldbestandPage` take `platformReadsCompanyData` from `SuperAdminContext` (`business.read` **and** a live context on that company) instead of the capability alone, which had become a rubber stamp.
  - **The dashboard's counts cost an audit row a page.** `collectCompanyCounts` (`platformReadModels.ts`) reads them on opening the page and on the refresh button only, never on the minute tick or after an action, and stops at the offset ceiling. A company created since the last read shows a dash in its row until the next refresh.
  - **Made unreachable rather than patched.** `enterCompany` (`SuperAdminContext.tsx`) requires a live context on that company and returns whether it opened; the six call sites branch on it. That is why ~22 tenant screens are not individually guarded — none of them can be reached without a session. The rule is `platformCanEnterCompany` in `platformAccess.ts`, and the gate has a clock: the provider asks `platform_whoami()` again when the context runs out (timed from `seconds_remaining`, so a wrong laptop clock does not matter), when the tab comes back, and on every `enterCompany`, and drops the company at the context's end whether or not an answer arrives. A re-ask never shows the panel's loading state, and one that fails closes the company but keeps the panel. 3.4b builds the screens that open a session, and calls `reloadPlatformAccess()` afterwards so the context is seen.
  - **Still open: a platform account whose own company the database refuses.** App.tsx skips the approval and subscription gates for `isSuperAdmin`, but `get_active_company_id()` still applies them to a staff member's own company and answers NULL — relevant in production, where a platform owner may sit in an unsubscribed company. The cash ledger is fixed: `SuperAdminContext` asks `get_active_company_id()` itself and `cashLedgerInScope` requires it (`ownCompanyReadable`), because the browser holds no copy of those gates for staff (`AuthContext.tsx:328` loads approval and subscription only for non-staff, and no company status is kept at all). The other tenant screens still read empty for such an account. Not routed away in 3.4a on purpose: sending it to `/admin` would also take it off `/abo-aktivieren` and `/billing`, the way out of exactly that state. 3.4b should decide it with the owner.
  - **3.4b's list, to re-check once a session can be opened:** Overview (money and margin), Bargeldbestand, Abrechnung, Drivers, Cars, Income, Salaries, Roles, Documents, Werkstatt, Notes, Planner, Belege, Notifications, Stundennachweis.
  - **Two of those are worse than empty, and are the ones to fix first.** `NotificationsPage.tsx:141-151` reads `[]` as "no settings saved yet" and renders `DEFAULT_ENABLED`, so an admin toggling from that screen would overwrite a company's real settings. `StundennachweisPage.tsx:3707-3723` generates and downloads an Excel file containing zero drivers — a wrong artifact leaving the building, with only a `console.error` at `:3724`.
  - **Independent of the seam, and its own defect:** `useDatabase.ts:10` reads `companyId` from `useAuth()`, which is NULL for a platform account even inside a company. The query raises `22P02` and the catch blocks at `:140-146` and `:331-338` swallow it into a `localStorage` fallback — empty, with no toast.
- **A platform capability requires a password-backed session (migration `20260919130000`), what to know:**
  - This is the server-side rule that PR #45 recorded as missing and that the plan deferred until after 3.3: *platform staff never sign in with Google* (owner decision, 16.09.2026) is no longer a screen. Until now `sessionCameFromGoogle()` in `AuthContext` ended the session in the browser, but the JWT was valid and passed every `platform_can()` check used directly against PostgREST or an edge function. It is urgent because the owner wants Google switched on in production.
  - **Where it lives:** `app_private.platform_staff_role_of(user_id)` answers NULL when the request carries an end user's token and the session's `amr` does not name `password` — a condition about the session, so it holds for every account the caller asks about. That one function is what `platform_can`, `platform_staff_role`, `platform_whoami`, `platform_can_manage_account`, `platform_audit_record`, `is_user_super_admin`, the five audited read models, both settings RPCs, every platform write RPC's capability check and the profile privilege guard all already reach — so nothing had to be changed twice, and there is nowhere left to forget.
  - **The rule is an allow-list:** `amr` must name `password` and must not name `oauth`, `sso/saml` or `anonymous`. Missing, empty or malformed `amr`, and no JWT at all, are false — never NULL, never an error.
  - **The evidence, from GoTrue's own source at v2.170.0:** the method strings are `internal/models/factor.go:40-91`; password grant → `password` (`token.go:220`), Google in both flows and One Tap → `oauth` (`external.go:82,232`, `token_oidc.go:235`), SAML → `sso/saml` (`samlacs.go:299`), and `POST /verify` → **`otp` whatever the `type` is** (`verify.go:279`). A refresh rebuilds `amr` from the session and appends nothing (`token_refresh.go:217`; there is no `AddClaimToSession` in that file). Measured read-only on staging with the e2e account: `[{"method":"password","timestamp":1789635130}]`, and the same array and timestamp after one refresh.
  - **The one cost, deliberate:** a session minted by `issueSession()` after a one-time code carries `otp`, so a newly invited platform member and one who used "Passwort vergessen" hold no capability until they sign in with the password they have just chosen. `SetPasswordPage` ends such a session and sends it to `/admin/login`, instead of letting it land on the panel's "no platform role" screen, which would say the wrong thing. **It asks `platform_whoami()`, not the profile flag**: keying on "is this account staff" would sign out a colleague who arrived from `must_change_password` after a real password sign-in, whose panel works fine. An error answers "nothing to do" — an unanswered question is not a reason to end a session — and a `signOut` that rejects puts the address back and falls through to the ordinary redirect.
  - **The audit log keeps the actor's role.** `app_private.audit_write` now reads `platform_staff_role_of_account()` for `actor_role`. On the gated helper it would have written NULL on the one event the rule exists to catch — the `denied` row `platform_rpc_authorize` writes for any refused actor — leaving a platform account probing the write RPCs from a Google session indistinguishable in the log from a company user. The body is `20260916110000`'s with that one call renamed; the diff was checked line by line and the nine rules it carries are listed in the migration header, because this repository has already shipped a defect by restating a function and dropping one.
  - **`amr` comes from the same document as `sub`** (`app_private.jwt_subject_source()`, singular claim first, which is `auth.uid()`'s order). Otherwise, wherever both claim forms are set — only ever the RLS kit, but that is where the first defect lived too — the subject could name one session and the `amr` another.
  - **It does not ask `auth.uid()`, and that is the whole lesson of the first round.** The gate first read `p_user_id = auth.uid()`, and the RLS kit failed on it: `auth.uid()` is not ours, its body differs between the supabase/postgres image (which reads only `request.jwt.claim.sub`) and the live databases, so in the kit the comparison was NULL, the `AND` was NULL, the `IF` never fired, and a Google session kept its platform role **while every pure-rule assertion around it passed**. It now asks `app_private.jwt_has_subject()`, which reads both claim forms itself, can only be true or false, and treats a subject that is not a uuid as a session rather than as no session. Same defect class as the two in CLAUDE.md, *Known defects*.
  - **What was NOT gated, and why it matters:** `app_private.platform_staff_role_of_account(user_id)` is the ungated account fact, and two callers read it because they ask about a *row*, not a session — `platform_staff_sync_profile()` and `enforce_user_profile_privilege_guard()`'s `staff_flag`. Rebuild either from the gated helper and a platform account signing in with Google reads its own staff row as absent and can clear its own `is_super_admin`. `tests/rls/platform-amr.sql` section 8 is what catches that.
  - **No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump.** No table, column, signature or grant changed; a company user is untouched; a password session answers exactly as before. The previous release keeps working.
  - **Coordination with 3.4a, re-checked on the merged tree after PR #56 landed.** The two migrations define nine and twenty-two functions and **share none**. Every place 3.4a asks about platform rights passes `auth.uid()`, so all six inherit the gate: `platform_context_row()` (`:272`), the new platform branches in `current_role_permits()` (`:535`) and `is_company_admin()` (`:576`), `platform_support_company_id()` (`:688`) and `platform_whoami()` (`:1242`). That is the outcome to want — a session Google minted can neither open a support context nor gain edit rights through one. `platform_reads_company_data()` is now a constant `false`, so `tests/rls/platform-amr.sql` section 6 takes its documented "seam already closed" branch and says so in a NOTICE instead of comparing.
  - **3.4a also added `tests/rls/platform-contexts.sql`**, whose `_pc_as()` sets a subject and a `session_id`; it needed the `amr` claim like every other principal, or every check in it would have failed on this rule rather than on what it means to test. That is the one file the rebase could have hidden.
- **A paying customer locked out of its own product (class 3 of the subscription decision sheet, approved 17.09.2026), what to know:**
  - **The defect was on the normal path and hit every new customer eventually.** `app_private.signup_insert_company` writes `status = 'trial'` with `subscription_status = 'inactive'`; approving a company changes only `approval_status`; `redeem_activation_code` writes the five `subscription_*` columns and **never** `companies.status`. Nothing else moves `status` off `trial` but the platform's own status button. So a paying customer keeps `status = 'trial'` for ever — and on the day `trial_end` passed, `PlanContext` called it `trial_expired`, dropped it out of `isActive`, and refused every feature but the overview. The server went on answering: `get_active_company_id()` accepts `status IN ('active','trial')` and never reads `trial_end`. Only the browser locked, so the sidebar emptied and ten routes became `LockedFeaturePage` while every query behind them still worked.
  - **The fix is that the browser asks the server's question.** The plan state machine moved out of the provider into `src/utils/planState.ts` and now reads `subscription_status` and `subscription_expires_at` as well: a live subscription makes a company active on its stored plan whatever `status` still says. The subscription term is part of the `active` branch too, so a company `get_active_company_id()` refuses is not shown as active — the browser is nowhere more permissive than the server, with **one carve-out the owner kept**: a *running* trial stays unlocked on premium, although a fresh company's subscription is `inactive` and the server does refuse it. Nothing is reachable through that gap — `SubscriptionGuard` holds such a company on `/abo-aktivieren`, `/aktivierung` and `/billing`.
  - **`isTrialExpired` changed meaning with it**: "the trial ended and nothing replaced it". Its only two readers, `TrialBanner` and the overview's plan card, print *Testphase abgelaufen · Nur Basic-Features verfügbar*, which is the wrong sentence for someone who is paying — and they printed it to every converted customer, whose `trial_end` stays in the past for ever.
  - **One predicate, two contexts.** `subscriptionIsActive()` is the only place the browser spells `subscription_status = 'active' AND (expiry IS NULL OR expiry > now())`; `AuthContext.isSubscriptionActive` now calls it. `PlanContext` still fetches its own row rather than reading AuthContext's copy, because AuthContext sets the profile one round trip *before* it has the company, so a plan state assembled from the two would spend that window calling a paying customer unsubscribed.
  - **`ActivationPage` refreshes the plan as well as the subscription**, or the customer lands on `/billing` with the row from before the code and stays locked until the tab is reloaded.
  - **The operator's screen reads the same way, or it contradicts the customer's.** `SuperAdminDashboard` judged a trial from `status === 'trial'` and the date alone while already fetching the two subscription columns and never looking at them, so after the customer-side fix a paying company would have read *Aktiv* on its own overview while the platform counted it in `expiredTrials` behind a red tile and raised a **critical** *Testphase abgelaufen* against it — a number growing by one with every customer who pays, on the screen the owner opens daily. One helper, `readsAsTrial()`, now feeds all seven readings (the three KPI counts, the whole attention chain, `expiringSoon`, the trial countdown cell and both extend-trial buttons) and calls the same `subscriptionIsActive()`. The **whole** attention chain, not only its expired branch: gating that branch alone drops a paying company into the next one, whose `daysLeft` is negative and therefore "≤ 2", trading one wrong critical item for another. A *running* trial is unchanged, paid or not, exactly as `isInTrial` ignores the subscription — so no company falls out of the trial KPI.
  - **An unknown `companies.status` now locks instead of opening.** Unreachable today (`companies_status_check` pins six values), but the direction was backwards: an unknown *plan* already locks, and a seventh status added later would be refused by `get_active_company_id()` while an opening browser showed empty screens. A row with **no** status at all still reads as `active`, as it always did.
  - **Not fixed, and deliberately so: the trial is not a usable trial.** A newly approved company has `subscription_status = 'inactive'`, which `get_active_company_id()` refuses, so it reads and writes nothing until an activation code is redeemed — `SubscriptionGuard` sends it to `/abo-aktivieren` and that is the intended flow. The database is the same as before; only the browser changed. The other half of the family — that `redeem_activation_code` never writes `status = 'active'` — is a migration, not a browser fix, and is left for the subscription slice.
  - 154 vitest cases in `src/utils/planState.test.ts`, including the full status × subscription × expiry × trial table asserted against a transcription of the server's predicate. **Read that table for what it is:** its oracle calls `subscriptionIsActive()` for the server's last two terms, so for that half it asserts `f(x) === f(x)` and pins the state machine's *shape*, not the subscription rule. The rule is pinned by the five hardcoded assertions in the `subscriptionIsActive` block; the test file says so.
- **The Team roster from its own table (PR #87, slice 3.12, migration `20260920140000`), what to know:**
  - **What it closes.** The Team page folded `app_private.platform_audit_log` into a roster, because `platform_staff` is not served by PostgREST and `platform_users_list` leaves staff out. So the roster lived only as long as the log, showed user ids instead of people, and could not say who granted a membership. `public.platform_staff_list` is the sixth audited read model, in exactly the shape of `20260916140000` — authenticated only, `VOLATILE`, arguments first (22023), `not_authorized` as a value, one `platform_read.staff_list` row per call through `platform_read_record`, no row for a caller without a staff role — with its SQL in `app_private.platform_staff_list_statement()`. The page reads it and the fold is deleted.
  - **The capability is `staff.manage`, owners only**, not `audit.read` and not `companies.read`. The roster is the list of addresses that hold platform rights, and every one of them signs in on the one public screen with a per-IP (not per-account) rate limit — so it is a target list, and a leaked `support`/`billing`/`read_only` password should not hand over the owners' addresses. Tying it to `audit.read` would let decision D3 widen it by accident. Every staff member still learns its own role from `platform_whoami()`.
  - **Columns** (a data protection decision, pinned key by key in `tests/rls/platform-readmodels.sql` section 11 and by the migration's self-check): `user_id`, `email` from `auth.users`, `role`, `status`, `disabled_at`, `note`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `created_by_email`, `invitation_pending`. Nothing else of `auth.users` — the self-check fails a release whose statement reads any column of it but `id` and `email` — and no name: the address identifies the account.
  - **The NULL actor was not a defect, and nothing was redefined for it.** Traced end to end: `platform_staff_add` writes `created_by = auth.uid()`, and both rows a call produces — its own `platform_staff.add` and the trigger's `platform_staff.insert` — reach `audit_write` without an actor argument, which takes `auth.uid()`; PostgREST v12 sets the claims for the whole transaction, triggers included. Disable and enable are the same. **Every NULL measured on staging came from a row written with no session at all:** the backfill of `20260916110000` (a migration) and the two statements run by hand as `supabase_admin` on 16.09.2026 (the `super@professor.com` insert and the `admin@yamobility.de` disable). Section 11f of the kit file now pins that an RPC-written row carries the caller on both rows and in `created_by`, and that a session-less row carries neither. **What stays true by construction:** a migration, `psql` or a host script that writes `platform_staff` records no actor and no creator. The page says *Nicht erfasst* for such a row and explains it once above the list. If host scripts should leave a trace, make them call the RPC with a signed-in owner's token, or set `created_by` explicitly.
  - **Role changes and non-owner roles stayed refused here, and were enabled on 23.09.2026** by the slice above (`20260923110000`). The two capability-based residues this entry pointed at were the right thing to check first: 3.13a (`20260921110000`) closed `user_profiles` and `company_settings`, and the role-enable slice found and closed the two it had missed, `roles` and `role_permissions`. There is still no way to change an existing member's role; a member who needs a different one is disabled and added again.
  - **The last active owner** is shown as *Letzter aktiver Inhaber* (a label with a lock, not a button that can only fail), with a warning above the list — and only when the whole roster was read; from a partial one the page draws no conclusion and leaves it to the database.
  - **Resend** is offered only while the member's profile is still `pending_invite`, the one state `otp_issue` will send an invitation in; it used to be offered for every active member with a tooltip saying it might not work.
  - **Rendered, not only reasoned:** the real page on invented data, through a Vite harness that replaces only the Supabase client, `SuperAdminContext` and `platformStaffClient` (the parser, the roster logic and both dictionaries are the real ones), in light and dark at 1280 and 375 wide, plus the single-owner case and English. No horizontal scroll at either width; below 1280 px the roster is one card per member. **Not rendered against a database**: the kit was not run (no Docker here, no VPS by instruction). Run `npm run test:rls` before merging; the files are the migration, `tests/rls/platform-readmodels.sql` (section 11, new) and one line in `tests/rls/matrix.sql`.
  - ~~**D4 is unblocked from 3.12's side once this is live on production** — not merely merged. It still needs the owner's retention periods and a cleanup job with a deliberate way past the append-only trigger.~~ — 3.12 went live on production on 21.09.2026, the periods were decided the same day, and the job is built: see the D4 entry below.
- **Audit log retention (decision D4, PR #94, branch `feat/audit-retention`, migration `20260921100000`), what to know:**
  - **The decision (21.09.2026, delegated by the owner):** rows of category `read` are kept **12 months**, every other category **36 months**, then deleted by a daily job. Counted in Europe/Berlin wall-clock months, strictly older than the cut-off (a row stamped exactly on it stays a day longer). The numbers are `CONSTANT`s in `app_private.platform_audit_purge()` and nowhere else.
  - **Nothing is deleted before September 2027, and that is why this is safe to merge.** `read` rows exist since 3.1/3.6b (16.09.2026 on staging, later on production) and `audit_write` stamps every category but `legacy` with `now()`. Every other category waits 36 months. `legacy` rows carry the `created_at` of `subscription_payment_audit` and `subscription_admin_actions`, created by `20260607125649` and `20260621114828` with `DEFAULT now()`, so the first is reached in June 2029 at the earliest. **This was reasoned from the repository, not measured** — the branch was built under an instruction not to touch any product database — so the dry run below prints the oldest row per category; run it before installing anything.
  - **Nothing else reads history out of the log**, checked in `src/`, `supabase/functions/`, every migration and `deploy/`: the Protokoll screen (`platform_audit_list`), and four rate limits whose longest window is an hour — 30 `denied` rows a minute (`platform_rpc_authorize`), 120 edge rows a minute (`platform_audit_record`), 120 settings rows a minute (`platform_settings_rate_limited`), 5 completed platform email changes an hour (`platform_self_email_change_check`). The company lifecycle deliberately does not read a prior status back out of the log (the 3.5 reactivate entry), and 3.4c's "wrote in a support session earlier in this transaction" is a setting.
  - **What it does.** One DELETE per run removes expired rows; the function returns one row per category (`category`, `cut_off`, `deleted`, all seven, zeros included) and writes **one** audit row of its own per run, also when nothing went: category `settings`, action `platform_audit.purge`, outcome `ok`, target_type `platform_audit_log`, no company, no actor (a cron has no JWT), and in `after_value` the two periods, the zone, both cut-offs, the count per category and the total. That row is non-`read`, so the record that read rows were removed outlives them by two years. Runs are serialised with an advisory lock. A partial index on the read rows' `occurred_at` keeps the daily DELETE off the rows it keeps.
  - **Why `settings` for its row:** the periods are how the platform is run, and `settings` rows are readable in full only with `audit.read` (owner). Not `read`/`write` — the categories `platform_audit_record` accepts from the edge functions. Not `legacy` — it means "copied from before 3.1" and is the one category allowed its own timestamp. `staff`, `auth` and `support` belong to their triggers and flows. `platform_audit_record` refuses the action in every category (its allow-list names only what the functions send), asserted by the migration and the kit rather than by redefining that function.
  - **The one way past the append-only trigger.** `platform_audit_log_refuse_change()` is restated with every refusal it had. It lets a DELETE of a row through only when **(1)** the setting `app_private.audit_purge_marker` holds exactly `purge:<this transaction's id>` **and (2)** the statement runs as a member of the log's owner (`pg_has_role(relowner, 'MEMBER')`; a superuser always is). The purge sets the marker immediately before its one DELETE and clears it immediately after, so a DELETE later in the same transaction is refused again, and a value left in a session, a role default or a database default can never match a transaction that did not exist yet. UPDATE and TRUNCATE are refused whatever the marker says. Both triggers are now `ENABLE ALWAYS`, so `SET session_replication_role = replica` — the quiet way a superuser skips ordinary triggers — no longer does.
  - **Why a client cannot reach it.** No API role can issue the DELETE: `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` have no USAGE on `app_private` and no privilege on the table, so the kit sees 42501 *permission denied for schema* even after each of them sets the exact marker itself (placeholder settings are user-settable in SQL, which is exactly why the marker is never trusted alone). Condition (2) is the second wall against a grant that leaks — the kit creates a role holding DELETE on the log, sets the exact marker for it, and the trigger still refuses. No function a client can call deletes from the log: the migration's self-check and kit section 8 read every function in `public` and `app_private` and fail when anything but the purge names a DELETE, UPDATE or TRUNCATE of the table, or anything but the purge and the trigger names the marker; the table has no foreign key, so no cascade reaches it. And nobody can call the purge: it is SECURITY INVOKER with EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`, so even a leaked grant would run it as a caller who holds no DELETE. PostgREST sets only `request.*` settings, the role and the search path. The shape is 3.4c's (a transaction-local setting one function writes and one trigger judges) plus U3's (a fact about the role the statement runs as).
  - **What it cannot do, stated rather than hidden:** hold a superuser who means it. `supabase_admin` at psql is refused a plain DELETE, UPDATE or TRUNCATE, with or without replica mode; but a superuser can disable or drop the trigger, and one who types the marker by hand passes as the purge does. The kit pins that last case as `RESIDUAL`, which also serves as the control that the other refusals are the marker's doing. The same limit U3 states: an operator is not someone a trigger can hold. **An operator removes rows only by running the purge.**
  - **Tests.** `tests/rls/audit-retention.sql` (new, 114 checks in 9 sections): shape and grants; a first run on the kit's young log (deletes nothing, still records itself); 28 boundary rows — per category, one a microsecond past the cut-off (goes), one on it and one a microsecond inside (stay), and one a day past twelve months (goes for `read` only), `legacy` through `audit_write` with its own timestamp; the purge's own rows; a second run in a session on `Pacific/Auckland` (same cut-offs, deletes nothing, records itself); the owner refused outside the purge, also in replica mode, after a purge in the same transaction and with a stale marker; four API principals refused by privilege after setting the marker; the leaked-grant role; the catalogue; and `platform_audit_record`. **Proved to fail:** ten deliberate mutations of the migration (marker not bound to the transaction, no owner condition, triggers not ALWAYS, `<` → `<=`, marker left set, the purge SECURITY DEFINER, 13 months for `read`, a second deleting function, no row when nothing went, `legacy` kept like `read`) were each caught, five by the self-check and five by the kit file.
  - **Verified in a stand-in, not in the kit.** A throwaway PostgreSQL 16.6 cluster from laragon in the scratchpad (session TimeZone UTC, like the kit), deleted afterwards: the schema fixture, the storage shim, all 45 migrations from `20260908`, both seeds and every `tests/rls` file except `salary-cash.sql` (psql `\gset`, which the stand-in cannot run) passed, on this branch and on the base. It is not `supabase/postgres:17` — **run `npm run test:rls` before merging.**
  - **Kit files:** `supabase/migrations/20260921100000_platform_audit_retention.sql` (new) and `tests/rls/audit-retention.sql` (new). No other file under `supabase/migrations` or `tests/rls` changed.
  - **Gates, from Git Bash:** `npx vitest run` 65 files, 1969 passed, 7 skipped (the `python3` checks in `tests/deploy/`, as before); `npm run build` passes with the typecheck ratchet at its baseline of 226 and the style ratchet unchanged. No TypeScript was touched.
  - **No `SCHEMA_EPOCH` bump.** A new function, one trigger function restated with every refusal and one path more, the triggers' enable mode, an index. No column, policy or API-role grant changes; the previous release never deletes from the log.
  - **Out of scope, on purpose:** D4 is the platform log only. `subscription_payment_audit` and `subscription_admin_actions` (the legacy sources, public tables) keep their rows; the tenant audit tables (`fahrtenbuch_assignment_audit`, `monthly_planning_manual_exceptions`) are business records with their own obligations; the OTP tables have `deploy/otp-housekeeping.sh`.
  - **How to run it. Staging: installed on 21.09.2026, after #94 merged and went live there. Production: not installed yet, and only once the release carrying `20260921100000` is live there.** The same steps for each. First the dry run, which changes nothing (the ROLLBACK takes the purge's own row with it):

    ```bash
    cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && docker compose exec -T db psql -U supabase_admin -d postgres -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 <<'SQL'
    -- The oldest row per category. Nothing but legacy may predate 16.09.2026.
    SELECT l.category, min(l.occurred_at) AS oldest, count(*) AS rows
      FROM app_private.platform_audit_log l
     GROUP BY l.category
     ORDER BY l.category;
    -- What a run would do today; every deleted count is 0 until September 2027.
    BEGIN;
    SELECT * FROM app_private.platform_audit_purge();
    ROLLBACK;
    SQL
    ```

    For production, the same with `cd /srv/PRO/deploy`. Then the cron file `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-audit-retention` (root, mode 0644) — the staging line first; add the production line once `journalctl -t el-professor-audit-purge` shows a staging run:

    ```
    20 2 * * *  root cd /srv/PRO-staging/deploy && tr -d '\r' < .env | grep -qx 'PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility-staging' && flock -n /run/lock/el-professor-audit-purge-staging.lock docker compose exec -T db psql -U supabase_admin -d postgres -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -qtA -c "SELECT 'staging ' || string_agg(p.category || '=' || p.deleted, ' ' ORDER BY p.category) FROM app_private.platform_audit_purge() AS p" < /dev/null 2>&1 | logger -t el-professor-audit-purge
    50 2 * * *  root cd /srv/PRO/deploy && tr -d '\r' < .env | grep -qx 'PROJECT_NAME=ya-mobility' && flock -n /run/lock/el-professor-audit-purge-production.lock docker compose exec -T db psql -U supabase_admin -d postgres -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -qtA -c "SELECT 'production ' || string_agg(p.category || '=' || p.deleted, ' ' ORDER BY p.category) FROM app_private.platform_audit_purge() AS p" < /dev/null 2>&1 | logger -t el-professor-audit-purge
    ```

    Each run logs one line, e.g. `staging auth=0 legacy=0 read=0 settings=0 staff=0 support=0 write=0`, and leaves one `platform_audit.purge` row in the Protokoll (category *Einstellungen*). Once installed, list the file under *Installed on the host by hand*.
  - **For the privacy policy, which does not exist yet — the sentence it must carry about these periods** (German, as the policy will be; lift it as is):

    > Protokolleinträge über Lesezugriffe von Mitarbeitenden der Plattform auf Kundendaten werden nach zwölf Monaten gelöscht, alle übrigen Einträge des Plattformprotokolls (insbesondere Änderungen, Support-Zugriffe, Anmeldevorgänge, Einstellungen und Teamverwaltung) nach 36 Monaten; die Löschung erfolgt täglich automatisch.

    (In English: log entries recording platform staff reading customer data are deleted after twelve months, all other platform-log entries after 36 months, automatically every day.) **One fact the lawyer will need beside it, which only the host knows:** a deleted entry survives in backups until they rotate out — the nightly `/etc/cron.daily/ya-mobility-backup` (its rotation is not in the repository) and the ten newest pre-migration snapshots `deploy.sh` keeps.
- **The legal pages, `/impressum` and `/datenschutz` (PR #97, in review, branch `feat/legal-pages`, migration `20260921120000`), what to know:**
  - **Why.** Production had neither an Impressum (§ 5 DDG requires one on a German commercial site) nor a privacy policy, and Google's consent screen needs a privacy-policy URL before Google sign-in can go live there. The slice builds the place for both and **invents nothing**: no company fact, no legal text. The owner enters the facts, the lawyer supplies the text.
  - **Two new settings keys, `legal.impressum` and `legal.privacy`** — thirteen keys now. Both seeded `{}` ("nothing published"), written with `settings.manage` (owner only), read in the panel with `settings.read` (owner, read_only; not billing, not support), version-checked and audited with the value before and after like every key.
  - **`seller` is untouched, on purpose (coordinator's decision, 22.09.2026).** The first draft stored the Impressum's fields inside `seller`; the previous release reads a seller only when it names exactly its eleven fields, so a rollback after the owner's first save would have broken the invoice PDF and the seller tab. The Impressum is therefore a key of its own. The invoice's name, address and VAT id reach it only through the editor's **"Aus Verkäuferdaten übernehmen"** button, which fills the empty fields for the owner to read and save — never behind his back. `get_legal_pages()` reads the two legal keys and nothing else, and the migration's self-check fails a release whose body so much as names `seller` or `invoice`.
  - **What is public: fourteen fields, pinned key by key** in `tests/rls/legal-pages.sql` and against `IMPRESSUM_FIELDS` in vitest: `legal_name`, `legal_form`, `street`, `house_number`, `postal_code`, `city`, `represented_by`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone`, `register_court`, `register_number`, `vat_id`, `content_responsible_name`, `content_responsible_address` — each justified in the migration header by its paragraph of § 5 DDG or § 18 Abs. 2 MStV. No bank detail, no Steuernummer, nothing of the invoice numbering. Every field is a string of at most 200 characters without control characters; a non-empty email and phone must be linkable. The page renders an Impressum only once `legal_name` is filled; before that it says it is not provided yet.
  - **The privacy text** is `{}` or `{as_of, paragraphs}`: a real date, 1–300 paragraphs of at most 4000 characters, **plain text only** (no `<` or `>`, no control character but a line break inside a paragraph). It may take **32000 bytes**, the one exception to the shared 16384 — dispatched ahead of the size check — because `audit_write` refuses a before/after value above 32768. A longer text needs its own decision (it cannot be had by editing a number).
  - **`platform_settings_problem` restated** from `20260920130000` with three changes (the key gate; `legal.privacy` before the size check; `legal.impressum` after it) and every other branch carried over character for character; the self-check restates what each earlier branch refuses, the seller's eleven-field rule included.
  - **The pages** (`src/pages/LegalPage.tsx`) are matched in `App.tsx` ahead of every gate, like `/design-preview`, so a signed-out, blocked, pending or signed-in visitor reaches them; the links are plain page loads. On the design system, German in both languages (on the closed list in `src/i18n/index.ts`), with one English line in the English UI saying so. Links: the landing page's footer, `/login`, `/signup` and the app sidebar's footer (new tab, so nothing typed is lost). No other change to those screens.
  - **The editors** are two new panel tabs, *Impressum* and *Datenschutz*, each with a preview that is the public page's own component.
  - **Screenshots** in `Docs/design/legal-pages/` (harness entry `design-harness/legal.html`, script `design-harness/capture-legal.mjs`): filled and empty at 1280 light and 375 dark, the English note and the failed-load state. All facts shown there are invented Muster/example.com data.
  - **Not verified:** the kit (the coordinator runs it), and the pages against a real database — the harness renders the production view on supplied state, not through `get_legal_pages()`.
  - **Google on production, once the owner has entered both pages there:**
    1. Google Cloud Console → *APIs & Services* → *OAuth consent screen* (*Branding*): set **Privacy policy link** to `https://elprofessor.eu/datenschutz` (and the home page to `https://elprofessor.eu`). Open the URL first and check the lawyer's text is on it.
    2. On the host, in `/srv/PRO/deploy/.env`: `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_ENABLED=true`. `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SHARED=true` must already be there (G3); check them without printing them.
    3. **Actions → Deploy → Run workflow on `main`, confirm `production`.** A switch only: no code changes, and `deploy.sh` refuses a malformed switch or client and checks `/auth/v1/settings` against it after the release. The kill switch is `false` plus the same button, never a rollback.
  - **For the lawyer:** the D4 entry above carries the German sentence the privacy policy must hold about the audit log's retention, and the backup fact to put beside it. The policy is pasted into *Einstellungen → Datenschutz*: paragraphs separated by a blank line, plain text.
- **A platform account writes a company's accounts and settings only inside a support session (PR #93, slice 3.13a, migrations `20260921110000` and `20260921110010`), what to know:**
  - **What it closes — four paths that reached any company with no session, no reason and no company binding.** (1) `user_profiles_update` / `_delete` carried `OR platform_can('company_users.manage')` with no company term (`20260917130000:646-659`): a view context changed the profiles of the company it read (the context made them visible, the capability writable). (2) `company_settings_insert` / `_update` / `_delete` carried `companies.lifecycle` / `companies.hard_delete` with no company term (`:611-626`): `super` edited, deleted and re-pointed any company's settings with no context, and a view context inserted them. (3) `manage-users` ignored the context entirely: `list` returned every account of every company to a `business.read` holder, `decideAccountTarget()` returned for a platform caller before comparing companies, and `create` took `company_id` from the body unvalidated — and interpolated it into the role lookup's PostgREST filter. The function runs as the service role, so 3.4a's confinement never reached it. (4) The three Gehaltsquittung RPCs succeeded inside a support session; the refusal was `receiptScope()`'s alone.
  - **The rule, and the helper reused.** The platform branch of the five policies is `company_id = (SELECT public.platform_support_company_id())` — 3.4a's own helper, the same term the storage write policies use; nothing new defined. No capability term beside it on the four member-shaped policies (inside a support session `is_company_admin()` already answers true, so a capability there would gate nothing); `company_settings_delete` keeps `companies.hard_delete` as a conjunct because it has no member branch. Every member term is carried over unchanged. One edge is stated in the migration header rather than hidden: the branch reaches the session's company even for a staff account that also sits in a company (3.7b's state), which needs an operator to write a profile by hand during an open session — and is recorded.
  - **Audit: they joined 3.4c's set, with their own arming.** Same function (`record_support_write()`), same row shape and redaction (`iban`/`bic` on `company_settings` are markers). **Not** 3.4c's statement trigger: its view refusal and `none:` arm would refuse a platform account's own profile row and the audited RPCs that write these tables for the company in their argument (`platform_company_lifecycle`, `platform_subscription_assign`, `platform_staff_sync_profile`) — exactly why 3.4a took `company_settings` off it. Instead `app_private.arm_support_shell_write()` (statement level) clears `app_private.support_shell_company` on every statement and sets it only inside a support session, and three row triggers call `record_support_write()` gated on the row's `company_id` equalling it. So the own row, another staff profile and an RPC's write elsewhere never reach the audit function (which would refuse a row of another company and roll the RPC back). Kit section 6b of `support-write-audit.sql` pins every case.
  - **`manage-users` (edge function, Deno glue plus pure `_shared/manage-users-routes.ts`).** `resolveManageUsersScope()`: authority unchanged (a platform capability or profile role `admin`); company = the membership, else for a company-less platform account `platformSupportCompanyId()` asked with the caller's token, else refused with `support_session_required` and a `denied` audit row. Every route compares the target's company with the scope (`decideAccountTarget()`, now for platform callers too; an other-company target is audited as `denied`); `list` is scoped by company for everyone; `create` refuses a `company_id` that is not a uuid (400) or names another company (403). Also fixed on the way, because they are the same company binding: the `update` route's role lookup was unscoped (`.eq('name', role).maybeSingle()` could hand back another company's role id) and now asks the target's company or the system roles; the shared lookup sorts `company_id desc nulls last`, since plain `desc` put the system role first in PostgreSQL, the opposite of what `create`'s comment said. `deno check manage-users/index.ts` passes (denoland/deno 2.1.4, local throwaway container); the function itself was not run.
  - **The owner's two panel flows open their own session (coordinator's decision, same PR).** `CompaniesPage`'s first administrator (end of the company wizard) and "Benutzer hinzufügen" call `manage-users/create` for a company from the list, with no session open, and the server rule would refuse both. They now run that one call through `withCompanyWriteSession()` (`src/lib/companyWriteSession.ts`): it asks `platform_whoami()` what is open now and then — **nothing open:** opens a support session with a fixed reason (`Erster Administrator nach Anlage der Firma` / `Benutzer anlegen aus der Firmenliste`, audit data, deliberately untranslated), creates the account, closes the session in every case; **a support session on that company:** reuses it and leaves it open; **a view context on that company, or any context on another:** refuses with a translated sentence (`sa.companies.session.viewOpen` / `.otherCompanyOpen`) and closes nothing. The session is opened with the raw client (no bar, `effectiveCompanyId` never moves) and closed through `SuperAdminContext.closeCompany` (no "session ended" notice, and `whoami` is re-asked). A failed close after a successful create says so (`sa.companies.session.closeFailed`). `platform_context_open`'s refusals are shown as sentences through `contextRefusalMessage`; `manage-users`' `support_session_required` cannot arise on these paths any more and would still show its German sentence. `src/lib/companyWriteSession.test.ts` pins every state. `UserManagement` is unaffected: platform accounts reach it only through a context, a view context is refused by the screen before any call, and a support session is exactly the scope the function now asks for.
  - **Gehaltsquittung.** Each RPC raises 42501 `platform_session_not_allowed` inside a platform context, either mode, before reading an argument, taking a lock or drawing a number. Condition: `v_company IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (membership)` — `get_active_company_id()` answers a company to an account without one only from an open context, so no new helper and no grant for the functions' `postgres` owner. Spliced from the catalogue in front of the authorization check (must match exactly once), as 3.3 and the GQ-year change did; the self-check re-asserts every load-bearing fragment of each old body and that each still answers a caller with no subject with `not_authorized`. The PR body lists each old behaviour and where it is now. The view mode now gets the new reason instead of `not_authorized`. The browser maps the reason to `company_mismatch`'s sentence.
  - **Kit.** Passed locally in full, on the branch as it stood on `7203776` (before #94 merged), on a throwaway `supabase/postgres:17.6.1.170` (Docker Desktop on the dev machine; container deleted). Matrix: 10 lines moved, each with its reason in the file — `company_settings` I (view `YR`→`RR`), U (`super` and view `YY`→`..`, support `YY`→`Y.`), D (`super` and view `YY`→`..`, support `YY`→`Y.`), M (`super` and view `YY`→`..`, support `YY`→`R.`); `user_profiles` U (view `Y.`→`..`), D (view `T.`→`..`), M (view `T.`→`..`); the three receipt RPCs, staff `TY`→`TT`. The residual tags on `company_settings` are gone; `companies` U/D keep theirs (not in this slice). Mutation run: the same kit without the two migrations fails on exactly those 10 lines. Also changed: `platform-contexts.sql` (5b, 6), `tenant-state.sql` (the owner line), `support-write-audit.sql` (section 1, new 6b), `salary-cash.sql` (new rolled-back block).
  - **Every other platform-side writer was checked for the same regression, and none has it.** `UserManagement` (tenant `/users`): reached by a platform account only inside a context; a view context is refused by the screen before any call, a support session is the scope the function asks for. `AccountPage` (`/admin/konto`) and `SetPasswordPage` write the account's own profile row, which every mode still admits (kit: 6b). `SettingsPage` / `CompanySettingsContext` (tenant `/settings`): a support session writes and is recorded; a **view** context is now refused — correctly, it writes nothing — but the save and the logo removal show the raw RLS error rather than a sentence (the logo upload already had one). The panel pages (`SuperAdminDashboard`, `SubscriptionBillingPage` with the seller, `PlatformSettingsPage`, `PlatformTeamPage`, the directories) write neither table: the dashboard and the invoice only *read* `company_settings`, the seller is `platform_invoice_seller()`, the team goes through `platform-staff`. The audited RPCs that write the two tables (`platform_subscription_assign`, `platform_company_lifecycle`, `platform_staff_sync_profile`) are SECURITY DEFINER and unaffected.
  - **Pre-existing, found here, not fixed:** `manage-users/list` reads `auth.admin.listUsers()` with no page arguments, which is GoTrue's first page of 50 accounts across the whole platform, and then keeps the ones in the caller's company. Once the platform holds more than 50 accounts, a company whose users are not all on that first page gets an incomplete Benutzer list — for its own admin as much as for support. The fix is to read the company's profiles first and page `listUsers` (or fetch the few accounts by id); it changes what the screen shows, so it was left for its own change.
  - **Not verified:** the two CompaniesPage flows clicked through (no harness covers the panel yet; the decision is pinned in vitest, the page wiring is not), the edge function end to end (no Deno runtime with a database here), the screens against staging, and production-owned function ownership (the kit has no owners; the splice keeps whatever owner a function has, and the new block calls nothing the owner lacks).
- **A support session's writes leave an audit row each (PR #86, migration `20260920110000`, slice 3.4c), what to know:**
  - **What it closes.** Opening and closing a context were audited; what the session wrote in between was not. Now every row a platform account writes inside a **support** session leaves one row in `app_private.platform_audit_log`: category `support`, action `support_write.<table>.<insert|update|delete>`, the row's company, `target_id` the row's `id`, the actor with its role and the sign-in session, `before_value {"values": …}` (NULL on insert) and `after_value {"context_id", "reason", "values"}` (values null on delete).
  - **Where.** `app_private.record_support_write()`, an AFTER … FOR EACH ROW trigger, on exactly the tables that carry 3.4a's `refuse_read_only_context_write` — derived from that trigger, not listed, so today the same 39 and none excluded. `tests/rls/support-write-audit.sql` section 1 fails a table that carries one and not the other: **a tenant table added later needs both triggers.**
  - **How a company member stays out of it.** The trigger's `WHEN (current_setting('app_private.support_write_context', true) <> '')` is a GUC read evaluated before anything is queued; only the statement trigger sets that flag, and only for a company-less account with a `platform_staff` row. A member's row never calls the function; a member's statement pays one `current_setting` and one primary-key lookup on `platform_staff`.
  - **What the flag holds, and the three answers.** `<context id>:<account>` while the account holds a valid support session, `none:<account>` while it holds none. The audit function records the first against the context it looks up again (it must exist, be this account's and be `support`), **refuses** the second (`support_session_required`) except for the D13 residual below, and refuses a flag that names another account or is malformed (`support_audit_unavailable`) — so a forged flag makes the write **fail** rather than record it in someone else's name or drop it.
  - **The defect the kit found, and the rule that closes it: nothing clears the flag for its own account.** The first version held only the context id and cleared the flag whenever that context was no longer valid. `platform_company_contexts.company_id` is ON DELETE CASCADE, and PostgreSQL runs a delete's foreign-key cascades one after another, in the order of their constraint triggers' names — which differs between databases. So in a company's hard delete by a support session, every audited cascade that ran **after** the contexts cascade found no session, cleared the flag and wrote its rows with the gate closed: **nothing recorded, nothing refused**. The kit's run refused the delete only because one audited cascade (`company_planning_runs`) sorted first there and queued an event while the flag was still armed; with the other order the delete would have gone through unrecorded. Now the statement trigger clears the flag only for another account, by comparing the account, and never looks up whether the session still exists. A session that lapses mid-call keeps its writes recorded (a planning RPC that resolved its company before the sixty minutes ran out); one ended or deleted before the first write is refused. Kit section 10 pins both, with a SECURITY DEFINER write after the session row is deleted and after it is closed.
  - **A company is not hard-deleted while a context is open — decided up front.** `app_private.guard_company_hard_delete`, a BEFORE DELETE row trigger on `companies`, refuses the delete for a company-less platform account that holds a context **in either mode**, or wrote in a support session earlier in the transaction, before any cascade runs: *Solange ein Unternehmenszugriff offen ist, kann kein Unternehmen gelöscht werden. Bitte zuerst den Zugriff beenden.* (`company_delete_context_open`). The answer no longer depends on the cascades' order or on which tables have rows. Recording the cascade instead would copy a whole company into an append-only log under a session that ends in the same statement, and put the delete at risk of the statement timeout. **End the session first**; with no context open the guard marks the transaction (`app_private.company_hard_delete`) and the audit lets exactly that delete's cascades through unrecorded — the D13 residual it always was, and `super` still deletes both companies in the matrix. `matrix.sql`'s `companies D` is `TT` for both context principals, now for one reason. No other cascade is affected: deleting a driver, vehicle, salary, import, run, reconciliation, document or role inside a session cascades only into audited tables of the same company and is recorded row by row (kit section 10). The audit also refuses a row of another company, as a second wall.
  - **`refuse_read_only_context_write()` was restated** to arm the flag. The header lists the eight rules carried over from 3.4a; its refusal sentence, SQLSTATE and HINT are unchanged, and it now reads `platform_context_row()` once instead of twice. It does **not** restate `jwt_amr()`, `jwt_subject_source()`, `session_amr_permits_platform()` or `platform_staff_role_of()`, so PR #84's `jwt_amr()` fallback is untouched. **Independent of the five migrations that sort after it** (`20260920120000`, U3; `20260920130000` and `20260920130010`, 3.10/3.11; `20260920140000`, 3.12; `20260920150000`, #88's recompute fix): none restates anything this one uses, and it touches nothing of theirs — they call `audit_write` unchanged, 3.11's new table lives in `app_private`, so it has no tenant shape and needs neither trigger, and #88 restates only `recompute_company_subscription()`, which writes `companies`, not an audited table. U3's `refuse_direct_subscription_write` is a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE row trigger on `companies`, which carries no audit trigger; this PR's only trigger there is `guard_company_hard_delete`, BEFORE DELETE, so on a company write only U3's fires and on a company delete only this one. The order they apply in — before them in the kit and on production, after them on staging — therefore does not matter.
  - **What the log copies — a data-protection decision, like the read models' columns.** UPDATE: changed columns only. INSERT/DELETE: the whole row. Always: pay and money owed to a person, identifiers beyond the name, gender and pay/absence notes as `{"redacted": true}` (the column is named, the value is not), and JSON documents and any value over 1 KiB as `{"omitted": true, "bytes": n}`, because `totals_json`, `driver_summary_json`, `rows_json` carry per-driver pay that no column name reveals. No hash of anything. The rule is one function, `app_private.support_write_redaction_pattern()`; widening what is copied is an owner decision. ~~Retention (D4) is still open.~~ D4 was decided on 21.09.2026: `support` rows, like every category but `read`, are kept 36 months (the D4 entry above).
  - **fahrtenbuch_trips.** `tg_protect_fahrtenbuch_immutable` is BEFORE ROW, the audit AFTER ROW, so a change to an immutable column is refused before any audit row exists, and a trip's row carries only the assignment columns. No UPDATE policy was added.
  - **The planning path, and what has to be measured.** apply writes `T + E + 2` rows (run insert, T trips, E exceptions, run update), replace `D + T + E + 3` (plus the old run and the D detached trips, recorded row by row). Both RPCs already write each trip in its own statement, so statement level would not have been cheaper. **The added time was reasoned, not measured** (~0.1 ms per trip); **time a support-session apply of a real month on staging before a support agent applies a large one.** If it is too slow, the fix is per-call rows inside the two RPCs, which means changing payroll functions.
  - **No rate limit, on purpose.** Every row is a real change to a customer's row; a cap would either stop support work mid-month or make the log lossy exactly when someone bulk-edits. Each value is capped at 16 KiB so `audit_write`'s 32 KiB limit can never fail a support write.
  - **Still not audited, and why:** `company_settings` and `user_profiles` — the two residues #80 and #84 named for 3.4c. Their platform writes take no company from the context (`companies.lifecycle`, `company_users.manage`), so they happen with or without a session; recording them only when a session happens to be open would log half of them under a category that is not theirs. They need a company term in the policy and an audit of capability writes, which is 3.13's. `storage.objects` has no row history to diff. The billing tables' writes are the audited 3.5 RPCs. — **Closed by 3.13a (PR #93, entry above):** both tables now take their platform writes' company from the support session, and those writes are recorded.
  - **Still open, not built here:** the Gehaltsquittung RPCs' server-side refusal inside a support session (the #81 follow-up below). — **Built by 3.13a (PR #93).** A support session's `confirm_salary_cash_payment` now leaves audit rows for the receipt, the payment and the cash movement — **which makes it traceable, not permitted**; the rule is attestation and does not change.
  - **Vitest skips 48 in PowerShell and 7 in Git Bash, and neither is this PR.** `tests/deploy/` skips its shell-driven checks when `bash`, `sh`, `sha256sum` or `python3` is not usable from Node: from PowerShell `bash` resolves to Windows' WSL launcher, `sh` and `sha256sum` are missing and `python3` is the Store stub, so all 48 skip; from Git Bash only the 7 `python3` ones do. Run the suite from Git Bash to compare counts with other runs.
  - **Kit files:** `supabase/migrations/20260920110000_support_write_audit.sql`, `tests/rls/support-write-audit.sql` (new, 13 sections), and `tests/rls/matrix.sql` (`companies D`, staff_support `YY` → `TT`, with the reason). The first kit run stopped on that matrix cell; the second (head `67d50a8`) passed the matrix and stopped in the new file on the own-company hard delete, which is how the defect above was found. The fix has not been through the kit yet. All three files parse with libpg_query (PostgreSQL's own parser, PG 18 grammar), every PL/pgSQL body and DO block included — syntax, not behaviour.
- **Storage in a support session (PR #84, migration `20260920100000`, the file half of plan 3.C), what to know:**
  - **Built against production's storage, not the chain.** The chain does not describe it: production was captured read-only on 19.09.2026 (7 buckets, 22 policies on `storage.objects`, objects only in `company-logos` (7) and `document-templates` (2)), and one of the policies replaced here — `Company members can read own company logo` — appears in no migration at all. Every DROP names a policy production carries, by its exact name, and the migration's self-check fails a release where any **other** policy still names one of the four buckets, or where any permissive storage policy names no bucket — a policy nobody knew about would OR its grant onto the new ones.
  - **The two defects were one.** `documents` asked only `folder = get_user_company_id()`, which since 3.4a answers the opened company in **either** mode — so a `view` context could insert, overwrite and delete that company's documents. PostgREST serves the `storage` schema, so that was reachable directly. The other three (`fahrtenbuch-files`, `company-logos` writes, `stundennachweise-pdfs`) read `user_profiles.company_id`, NULL for a platform account, so a support session reached none of them. Moving those onto `get_user_company_id()` alone would have spread the first defect to all four.
  - **The rule.** Read: `folder = get_user_company_id()` — a member's own company in whatever state, as before, and the opened company in either mode. Write: `folder = COALESCE(membership, platform_support_company_id())` — 3.4a's own name for "the company open in support mode", in `get_user_company_id()`'s order (membership first), so a staff account that still sits in a company is judged by its profile. `company-logos` keeps its members' `role IN ('admin','super_admin')` verbatim and adds `OR is_company_admin()`, 3.4a's administrator gate, which is true for a company-less staff account only inside a support session and adds nothing for a member. `fahrtenbuch-files` still has no UPDATE policy. **No member gains or loses anything**; `tests/rls/storage.sql` pins each bucket's member rule, including that a suspended company's member still reaches its own files.
  - **Not SQL alone, and this is the part to know: storage-api's claims never carried an `amr` the database could read.** storage-api v1.19.3 (`src/internal/database/connection.ts`, `setScope`) sets `request.jwt.claim.sub` and the whole payload as `request.jwt.claims`, and no singular `amr`. Since `20260919130000`, `jwt_subject_source()` answers `claim` there and `jwt_amr()` read only the singular amr, so **every platform account held no role on every storage-api request** — no context, no file, whatever the policies said. `jwt_amr()` now falls back to the document's amr when the singular subject carries no singular amr **and the document names the same subject**. A singular amr still wins; a malformed one does not fall back; a document naming anyone else is never read. Without this the policies were right and a support session still could not upload anything through the browser. **Any later migration that restates `jwt_amr()` must keep the fallback.**
  - **`salary-receipts` was already right and is untouched:** its writes ask `current_role_permits('salaries', true)`, false in a view context, and it has no DELETE policy. The self-check pins that. Whether a support session may attach a Gehaltsquittung's signed copy is the permanent owner rule (it may not); its enforcement belongs with the three RPCs, the open follow-up below — the uploaded file alone attaches nothing.
  - **`document-templates`** (public, owner-keyed, unused by `src/`) and **`Y & A Mobility`** (private, named by no policy, a customer's name) are not touched; the second is under *Waiting on the owner*.
  - **In the browser:** `support.storage.membershipOnly` is gone. A refused upload in a `view` context (`useFahrtenbuch.createImport`, `SettingsPage.handleLogoUpload`) says `support.storage.readOnly`; a support session's failure shows the raw error like a member's.
  - **Unblocked, and now done:** lifting `role_requires_3_3` in `platform_staff_add` / `_enable`, for which this was the named prerequisite — a `read_only` account's view context now writes no company file, where before it could write the `documents` bucket. It was not done in that PR on purpose, and landed on 23.09.2026 as the slice above. The two capability-based residues (`user_profiles`, `company_settings`, above) are unchanged by this and are 3.4c's. — 3.4c (PR #86) left them out on purpose and says why in its entry above: they are capability writes, not context writes, and belong to 3.13.
  - **Kit files:** `supabase/migrations/20260920100000_storage_support_sessions.sql`, `tests/rls/storage.sql` (new), `tests/rls/platform-amr.sql` (storage-api shape added to 2b), and comment-only edits to `tests/rls/matrix.sql` and `tests/rls/platform-contexts.sql`. **The kit was not run from this branch** — no Docker here and no VPS by instruction; run `npm run test:rls` before merging. **Not verified at all:** a real upload through storage-api on staging, which is the only proof that the claim shape above is what the live service sends.
- **A reload threw a support session out of its company (PR #82), what to know:**
  - **The first time anyone rendered 3.4b**, on staging on 18.09.2026, with a throwaway platform owner on the e2e test company. Opening a company worked and the bar read exactly what the plan specifies. Then an ordinary reload of `/overview` landed on `/admin` — the platform overview on screen, with the read-only bar above it still naming the company.
  - **It was not a lost session.** No `platform_context_close` was ever sent and `platform_whoami` went on being asked afterwards, so the context survived the reload as designed. It was a race in the routing: on a fresh load `PlatformAccountGuard` ran before the first whoami answered, read "no company" out of an unanswered question, and redirected; the answer arrived too late to matter.
  - **The fix is a third answer, not a permissive one.** `platformAccountRoute()` in `adminGate.ts` returns `tenant`, `panel` or `wait`, and `wait` is a spinner. It reads `platformAccessStatus`, which is `loading` for the **first** question only — a re-ask keeps the previous answer on screen — so a fresh load waits and a tab regaining focus never does. A *failed* whoami is `panel`, not `wait`: an unanswered question is not permission, and a failed one is not a reason to spin for ever; `/admin` is where the failure and its retry are shown.
  - **Do not make the guard permissive while loading.** Letting the tenant router mount before the answer is in is the shape that put a company-less account into it with every query asking for `company_id = null`. The function's comment says so; leave it there.
  - **Where a reload lands:** back on the tenant screen whose address the browser still holds — that comes free once the guard stops redirecting, and the address bar is the only record of where the person was. For a reload of `/admin` itself, or a deliberate visit with a session open, the bar there now offers `Zur Unternehmensansicht`.
  - The regression test is `platformAccountRoute`'s first case, proved by transcribing the pre-fix rule into the function (`expected 'panel' to be 'wait'`). Three more in `SuperAdminContext.test.ts` pin the distinction at the source: a whoami **left unresolved** reports `loading` with no company, one answered with no context reports `ready` with no company. Those two look identical to a guard that only asks whether there is a company.
- **Support sessions in the browser (PR #80, slice 3.4b), what to know:**
  - **What it closes.** 3.4a (`20260917140000`) made `get_active_company_id()` answer the open context's company and nothing else, and left the browser with no way to open one — `platformCanEnterCompany` required a live context, so every "Als Unternehmen öffnen" click returned false and the panel's own comment said 3.4b would build the screens. This is those screens: open a read (`view`) or support context, see it in a bar, end it. **No SQL was added; every RPC already existed.**
  - **The seam is `SuperAdminContext.effectiveCompanyId`, and it now comes from the server.** It was React state a click left behind — lost on every reload, invisible to any screen that read `AuthContext.companyId`. It is derived from `platform_whoami().company_context` through the pure `companyScope()` (`src/lib/companyScope.ts`), which resolves a member's own company **first** and the context only for an account that has none, the order `get_active_company_id()` itself uses.
  - **`AuthContext.companyId` deliberately keeps meaning the membership**, although the plan's wording suggests otherwise. Three rules read the difference between the two values: `receiptScope()` tells `own_company` from `other_company` by comparing exactly them, `ownCompanyDataReadable()` judges the account's own company by what the database answered for it, and AuthContext's own company/approval/subscription queries are about the membership. And AuthProvider sits **above** SuperAdminProvider, so resolving the context there would mean a second `platform_whoami()` poll and a second expiry timer — the opposite of "one place".
  - **`SuperAdminProvider` moved above `CompanySettingsProvider`** so both it and `PlanProvider` can ask for the session's company. SuperAdminProvider reads only AuthContext, so nothing is inverted.
  - **What was actually broken, verified rather than taken from the plan** (the list is 15.09 and two migrations have landed since): `useDatabase` (Belege, Fahrer, and the receipt/driver halves of Overview and Umsätze), `useFahrtenbuch`, `CompanySettingsContext`, `PlanContext` and `CompanyBillingPage` all read the membership. **Abrechnung was already correct** — it passes no company at all, `abrechnung_weekly` is `security_invoker`, and `matrix.sql` pins both the isolation and that a context shows the context's company. **Rollen was already correct too**, except that its `useEffect` had `[]` deps, so entering a company left the previous list on screen. **Benutzer is the one that is only half fixable here:** `manage-users/list` (`index.ts:207`) answers a platform caller with every account of every company, including platform staff, and that is a server change the plan assigns to `app_company_scope()`. The browser filters what it *shows* and says in the code that this is display, not security.
  - **`MonatsplanungStundennachweisPreview.tsx` never used `effectiveCompanyId`** — its variable of that name is a local off the planning run. CLAUDE.md's claim that it is the only consumer was wrong in both directions; 24 files use the real one, and the claim is corrected in this PR.
  - **A `view` context does not offer writes.** `companyScope.readOnly`, `useCompanyScope()` (`can`, `canCreate`, `canEdit`, `canDelete` all false) and the four screens that ask those questions; Rollen's buttons; and **Benutzer is refused outright** with a sentence naming what is missing, because every control on it writes. **The residue is stated and not hidden:** the other ~59 files that call `supabase.from(...)` still render their save buttons, and the database refuses each write with a German sentence the bar explains how to fix. Disabling a button was never what stops the write.
  - **Entering a company opens a `view` context, not a support session** — least privilege, and it avoids demanding a justification before anyone knows one is needed. The bar offers the upgrade, which asks for the reason `platform_context_open` requires.
  - **A context expiring mid-screen needs no answer from the server:** the provider's timer fires at `expiresAtMs`, the scope becomes `none`, the bar raises one toast (suppressed when the person pressed *Beenden*), and `PlatformAccountGuard` sends the tab to `/admin`. A test advances fake timers with every RPC hanging and asserts it.
  - **`platform_context_current()` is deliberately never called.** It is `VOLATILE` and writes an audit row per call; the browser asks for its context on every tab focus, and `platform_whoami()` answers the same `company_context` and writes nothing.
  - **The open call installs its own answer.** `platform_context_open` returns the row it wrote; the provider parses it with the same parser `whoami` goes through. Without that, a caller navigating on success lands in the window before the confirming `whoami`, where the browser holds no context and `PlatformAccountGuard` bounces it back to `/admin`.
  - **The company can now move under a mounted screen, which it never could before, so the tenant shell is keyed on it.** `AppLayout` carries `key={effectiveCompanyId}` (`TenantShell` in App.tsx). Two tabs on one session, tab 2 switches company, tab 1 regains focus: `CompanySettingsContext` follows the company but `useDatabase`'s four screens key their load on `dbLoading` alone, so the sidebar name, the logo and every document header would become company B over company A's figures. No leak — the rows were read under A's own policy — but a payroll figure under the wrong customer's name. A key covers every screen of that shape, including the ones nobody has looked at; four dependency arrays would have covered four.
  - **`isAdmin` was the wrong gate for four routes.** `App.tsx` registered `users`, `roles`, `billing` and `settings` on `user_profiles.role === 'admin'`, and `platform-staff` writes `viewer` on every invited member's profile — so the RolesManagement and CompanyBillingPage work in this very PR was unreachable for the accounts it is for. They are now registered unconditionally and decided by `CompanyAdminRoute` (an administrator of this company, **or** a platform session), and the Sidebar's management section follows the same rule. `UserManagement`'s own test was wrong in the other direction too: `isAdmin || kind === 'support'` gave a platform account whose profile happens to carry `admin` a fully editable screen inside a **read** context, and every button there calls `manage-users`, which never looks at the context. The profile role now decides only when the scope is the account's own company.
  - **`companyScope.readOnly` is what the screens should offer, not proof that the database refuses.** Two paths are outside the 39 tables `refuse_read_only_context_write` covers, both 3.3/3.4a residue that this slice makes *reachable* rather than creates: `user_profiles` (`user_profiles_update`/`_delete`, `20260917130000`, a `company_users.manage` branch with no company term) and `company_settings` (same shape under `companies.lifecycle`, deliberately left off the trigger's list). **Follow-ups, for 3.4c:** put the write-audit triggers on both, and give the two policies a company term. — 3.4c (PR #86) put the triggers on the 39 context-write tables only and gives its reasons for leaving these two to 3.13 in its own entry above. **Both closed by 3.13a (PR #93):** company term and audit, see its entry. ~~`storage.objects` (no trigger at all)~~ was the third — **closed by `20260920100000`** (see the storage entry above): a view context now reads a company's files and writes none.
  - ~~**The storage half of two "fixed" screens still is not fixed, and the screens now say so.**~~ — **fixed by `20260920100000`.** `fahrtenbuch-files` and `company-logos` (and `stundennachweise-pdfs`, which had the same shape) now let a support session write the opened company's files; `support.storage.membershipOnly` is gone, and a refused upload in a *view* context says `support.storage.readOnly` instead.
