See the storage entry above for the one part that was not SQL.
  - **`manage-users` ignores the context completely, and the write half is wider than the list route.** `_shared/manage-users-routes.ts:68-76` returns for a platform caller before `targetCompanyId` and `callerCompanyId` are ever compared, so `update`, `activate-deactivate`, `resend-invite`, `reset-password` and `delete` act on **any account in any company** for a holder of `company_users.manage` — no context, no reason, no company term; and `create` (`index.ts:253-255`) takes `company_id` from the request body unvalidated. All pre-existing: nothing in this PR touches `supabase/`. The plan's answer is `app_company_scope()` plus paging and no `body.company_id`. — **Closed by 3.13a (PR #93):** the function resolves one company per caller (membership, or the support session's company) and every route, `list` and `create` included, is held to it; a `body.company_id` is accepted only when it repeats that company. Paging `listUsers()` is still not done — see the 3.13a entry.
  - **A context ended at the server is noticed within a minute.** The wake-up and the expiry timer between them miss one case — a session closed from another tab, or a staff row disabled, while this tab keeps the focus — and in that case every screen queries a company the database no longer answers for and prints confident zeros. A focused tab with a company open now re-asks `platform_whoami()` every 60 s (STABLE, no audit row, nothing when no company is open or the tab is hidden).
  - **The Gehaltsquittung screens refuse inside a support session, and it is now a decided rule** — see the entry below, which corrects the reason PR #80 wrote here.
  - 41 new tests (12 `companyScope`, 19 `supportSession`, 8 added to `SuperAdminContext`, minus `enterCompany`'s two, which went with it). **No screen was rendered** — every claim is from source, the migration, `tests/rls/matrix.sql` or a test.
- **A support session never issues a Gehaltsquittung (owner decision, 18.09.2026, PR #81), what to know:**
  - **The rule is permanent and waits on nothing.** PR #80 left `receiptScope()` answering `other_company` inside a support context, so `GehaltsquittungModal` and `GehaltsquittungList` refuse there, and recorded it as provisional pending the owner and 3.4c. The owner delegated the decision and it is now made: the refusal stays, for good. The code did not change — only the reason written beside it. `receiptScope()` behaves exactly as it did.
  - **The reason is attestation, not traceability.** A Gehaltsquittung attests to a physical handover of cash: the company counts banknotes into a driver's hand and the driver signs the paper for having received them. A platform support agent was not in the room. Letting one issue a receipt means recording a physical event nobody at the platform witnessed, and no audit log changes that. This is why the rule cannot expire.
  - **PR #80's reason was factually wrong, and that is the hazard this PR removes.** It said the receipt "records no actor". It records three: `salary_cash_receipts.confirmed_by` (`20260914140000:174`, written at `:517`), `.voided_by` (`:176`, written at `:618`) and `cash_movements.created_by` (`:117`, written at `:535` and `:629`), all from `auth.uid()`. A reason that expires is worse than no reason: the first person to ship 3.4c's write-audit triggers would have read "we only blocked this because nobody was recorded" and reopened the rule.
  - **Two facts support it without being the reason.** A platform account inside a context has no company of its own (`platform_context_open` refuses one that has, `account_in_company`), so the customer cannot resolve `confirmed_by` to a name through its own `user_profiles` read, and nothing on these screens selects the column anyway. And the act is close to irreversible on a customer's books: the receipt consumes a gapless number and moves cash, and the only undo is a Storno, which is itself a permanent row — one mistake leaves two marks.
  - **Follow-up, not built here: the rule is unenforced server-side.** `confirm_salary_cash_payment`, `void_salary_cash_receipt` and `attach_salary_cash_receipt_signed_copy` all resolve the caller with `get_active_company_id()` (`20260917130000`, and `20260919160000` for the first), which answers the open context's company, so **all three still succeed inside a support session**. The browser is the only thing refusing, and a staff member who can open a context can call them straight through PostgREST with their own token and the anon key that ships in the bundle. A permanent product rule belongs in the RPCs: refuse when the caller's `user_profiles.company_id` is not the company the write is for — the condition the three functions had before 3.3, kept only for these three. Sized as SQL work for 3.4c, beside the write-audit triggers. **Not built by 3.4c (PR #86). Built by 3.13a (PR #93, `20260921110010`)** — with the condition "a company, and no membership" rather than the pre-3.3 read of `user_profiles.company_id`, so the state gate of 3.3 stays in force; see the 3.13a entry.
- **Slices 3.10 (second half) and 3.11 — mail texts, the invoice's seller, announcements, feature flags (PR #89, `20260920130000` and `20260920130010`), what to know:**
  - **Built together on purpose.** Both extend `app_private.platform_settings_problem`, the validator that must be restated to learn a key, and two branches restating it in parallel is how this repository lost rules before. It is restated **once**, in `20260920130000`, with the `price_book`, `plan_features` and 3.10-first-half branches carried over character for character and self-check 4 restating what each must still refuse. `platform_settings_get`, `platform_settings_put` and `platform_audit_record` are **not** redefined, and neither is `jwt_amr()`, `jwt_subject_source()`, `session_amr_permits_platform()` or `platform_staff_role_of()` (so PR #84's storage-api fallback is untouched). `platform_settings_rate_limited` is restated to count the new actions in the same 120-a-minute budget, through `app_private.platform_settings_audit_actions()`; its constant and its fail-closed NULL answer are carried over. `get_app_config()` is restated with the price book, the feature map and the trial length read exactly as before.
  - **Six new keys, all `settings.manage` (owner only), all readable with `settings.read` (owner, read_only — not billing, which holds it for prices alone):** `mail.sender` (`{"name": …}`, seeded `El-Professor`, the `SMTP_SENDER_NAME` both hosts set), `mail.template.signup_verify|password_reset|invite_activate|email_change` (each seeded `{}` — an override, so every mail is byte-identical until the owner changes a field), and `feature_flags` (seeded `{}`). `platform_login` has no template: nobody receives that mail since the owner's decision of 16.09.2026.
  - **The code never reaches a subject**, three times over: the subject takes no variable, `{code}` is not a variable anywhere (the layout draws it in its box), and six digits in a row are refused as `…subject.code`; at send time `safeSubject()` replaces a subject that contains the code with the default. **An unreadable template sends the default wording**, never no mail: `loadMailDeliverySettings()` never rejects, and `buildOtpMail` ignores a template it cannot read whole. The pure rules are `supabase/functions/_shared/mail-template.ts`, shared by the functions, the settings screen's preview and vitest; `mail-template.test.ts` reads the migration's bounds and seeds and fails when they drift, and a scratch comparison against HEAD's `otp-mail.ts` showed 90 of 90 default mails byte-identical (5 purposes × 3 variants × 6 template values including `{}`, `null` and garbage).
  - **The functions read the settings through `public.mail_delivery_settings(purpose)`, service role only and not audited** (the caller is the system sending a code). OTP mails, the address-changed notice and the activation-code mails all take the sender name from it. **The sender address is not a setting** (it must sit on Resend's verified domain), and GoTrue's own templates keep GoTrue's `SMTP_SENDER_NAME`, which the database cannot reach. **No function was added**, so `FUNCTION_ENV`, `PUBLIC_FUNCTIONS` and `deploy/docker-compose.yml` are unchanged.
  - **`platform-mail-test` (a test send) is scoped out.** It would need a function directory, a `FUNCTION_ENV` line, a compose line, and an action in `app_private.platform_edge_audit_actions()` (a restatement `platform-access-actions.test.ts` watches) — and a mail sent to the caller only, so it cannot relay. The preview on the settings screen renders through `buildOtpMail` itself, so what it shows is what goes out; what it cannot show is delivery.
  - **The invoice PDF now prints the `seller` setting**, through `public.platform_invoice_seller()` (`companies.read`, the capability that reads an invoice, so billing and support — who download invoices but may not read the settings — get it too; one audited `read` row per PDF; inside the settings budget; `not_found` for a stored document the validator would refuse). It replaced a `company_settings` read of whichever company the signed-in platform account sat in — none, on both real databases — so **every invoice printed "El-Professor" with nothing under it, and still does until the owner fills the seller in**. `invoiceSellerFields()` keeps the old composition character for character, except that an all-blank address prints `''` instead of the old `"\n "` — exactly what production printed with no row. A failed read builds no PDF. This is what unblocks 3.7b (the platform account losing `company_id`). The `invoice` key (prefix, digits, payment method) is **still not read** by `generate_invoice_number` or `GenerateInvoicesModal`; its editor still says so.
  - **Announcements are rows, `app_private.platform_announcements`**, each with its own version: tone, German title and body (required), English (optional, both or neither), an audience (all, plans, or named companies — ids checked to exist at save), and a window `[starts_at, ends_at)` **of two timestamptz, never dates**; the validator refuses a time without an explicit offset, so no cast depends on the session's zone (the kit runs in UTC). `get_app_config()` returns those that apply to `get_active_company_id()` now — a blocked company and a platform account outside a session get none — newest start first, at most five, and **never the audience** (company ids would name other customers). A plan audience matches the stored `companies.plan`, not a running trial's premium. `AnnouncementBanner` sits under the trial banner in `AppLayout`, takes an announcement down at its end without waiting for the next config read, and remembers a dismissal per browser keyed by id **and version**.
  - **Feature flags: `useFlag(key)` lives in `AppConfigContext.tsx`**, off for any flag the map does not name. **Nothing reads a flag yet**, which is why it is not a file of its own (a module nothing imports is dead code by CLAUDE.md's rule). `parseAppConfig` now takes flags and announcements from the answer; an answer that lacks them (`get_public_config()`, after a sign-out) means none, so one account's announcements never linger on the next.
  - **Order-independent of `20260920120000` (#85) and `20260920140000` (#87)**, which is why the files keep their reserved `130000` numbers although staging applies them after `140000`: neither of mine defines or calls anything those two define or call (`platform_staff_list*`, `platform_read_record`, `platform_audit_record`, the `companies` write wall), neither writes `companies` or `platform_staff`, and no self-check on either side counts rows the other creates. The wall does not touch this slice: nothing here writes a subscription column or `trial_end`, the trial still goes through `signup_insert_company` (SECURITY DEFINER, untouched), and the test files that set `companies.plan` or `.status` do it as `supabase_admin`, not as an API role.
  - **`InvoiceDetailModal` lost its unused `sellerSettings` prop**, which took one `TS6133` with it: the ratchet baseline is **227**.
  - **Tests.** SQL: `tests/rls/settings.sql` (sections 7–9 new; the key count and the unknown-key examples updated), `tests/rls/pricing.sql` (key count; `mail.sender` → `a_key_nobody_seeds` as the unseeded example; the `get_app_config` comment), new `tests/rls/announcements.sql`, and five lines in `tests/rls/matrix.sql` plus one fixture announcement. **The real kit was not run** (no Docker here, the VPS was off limits); a stand-in was: PostgreSQL 16 (laragon's binaries) in a throwaway cluster in the session's scratchpad, loading `tests/fixtures/schema.sql`, the storage shim, every migration from `20260908`, both seeds and every `tests/rls` file — **all passed except `salary-cash.sql`, which the stand-in cannot run because it uses psql's `\gset`**, on this branch and on the base alike. It is not the supabase/postgres:17 image — run `npm run test:rls` before merging.
  - **Rendered.** The settings tabs (E-Mails, Ankündigungen, Feature-Flags, Verkäufer, a billing account's single tab), the tenant banner, light and dark, desktop and 390 px, through a local Vite harness on invented data with the Supabase client and two contexts replaced. What rendering found and fixed: the mail preview frame came up blank when narrow with `sandbox=""` and renders with `sandbox="allow-same-origin"` (still no scripts, no navigation); the preview was too short for the whole mail; a save button's label was a fixed white. **Not fixed, and not ours:** `datetime-local` shows the browser's own date format (US in the harness's Chrome), like every other date input in the product. Nothing was clicked through against a real database.
- **`main` is behind `staging`** by the Phase 0 work. Production still runs a bundle deployed before CI releases existed.

## What is live where

| | production | staging |
|---|---|---|
| web | `https://elprofessor.eu` — pre-CI bundle | `https://app.elprofessor.eu` — behind Basic Auth |
| API | `https://api.elprofessor.eu` | `https://api-staging.elprofessor.eu` |
| release record | none yet; the first CI release is step 0.6 | `/srv/PRO-staging/releases/`, `current` → the live one (`status staging`) |
| login rate limits | not yet; they arrive with 0.6 | on |
| mail | Resend | Mailpit, relaying to Resend except `@example.com`; `mail-staging.elprofessor.eu` resolves and the mailbox is live since 16.09.2026 |
| edge functions | the pre-pipeline bundle: no `otp-request`, `otp-verify`, `oauth-account` or `platform-staff` | all of them, since the OTP and Google releases |
| platform panel | — | `/admin` with its own sign-in since PR #57; `/super-admin/*` still rewrites onto it until slice 3.13 |
| GoTrue signup | closed since 16.09.2026 (`disable_signup:true`) | closed; Kong answers 403 on signup, otp and magiclink |
| Google sign-in | off, and stays off until the password-session rule exists | on since 16.09.2026 |
| schema epoch | 1 (its release predates 3.3) | 2, since PR #46 |

## Installed on the host by hand, not by CI

- **`super@professor.com`'s production password was set to the same one staging uses, on 27.09.2026, at the owner's explicit instruction and after the risk was put to him.** The account was never missing: it exists in both environments, `email_confirmed_at` set, not banned, an **active `owner` row** in `app_private.platform_staff`, and it had signed in on 23.09 in both. What differed was the password — staging accepted it, production answered `400 invalid_credentials` — which is why it read as "not on production". It was changed through GoTrue's admin endpoint (`PUT /auth/v1/admin/users/<id>`) with the service role key, so the hash is GoTrue's own, and verified immediately by a password grant that returned 200 with a token. Nothing else about the account was touched; no `.env`, no container, no migration.

  **The risk the owner accepted, written down because it does not expire:** this is a platform `owner` account on a live product with real customers' data, and the password is the single most-guessed string there is. `/login` is the one public sign-in screen since 17.09.2026, and the only limit in front of it is GoTrue's `RATE_LIMIT_TOKEN` — 60 requests per 5 minutes **per source IP**, shared with token refresh — so there is **no per-account lockout anywhere in the product** and a guesser with rotating addresses is not bounded. An owner session reads and writes every company's rows through the support-session and audited-RPC paths. Changing it later is one admin call; the exposure until then is real. The alternative offered and declined was a generated strong password handed over once.


- `/usr/local/sbin/el-professor-release`: `deploy/ci-release.sh`. It is the only thing the CI key can run: `release`, `rollback`, `status`. Reinstall it before merging a workflow change that relies on it.
- `/usr/local/sbin/el-professor-caddy-guard` plus its systemd `.path`/`.timer`/`.service` units: `deploy/caddy/guard.sh`.
- `/srv/caddy-sites/elprofessor.caddy`: the published routes. Change them only through `deploy/caddy/publish.sh`.
- `/srv/PRO-staging/deploy/set-staging-password.sh`, `set-mailpit-password.sh` and `set-staging-mail-relay.sh`.
- `/srv/PRO-staging/deploy/otp-housekeeping.sh`, scheduled by `/etc/cron.d/el-professor-otp` to run hourly and daily on staging. The production lines are added at 0.6.
- Previous versions are kept in `/root/el-professor-caddy-backups/` and `/root/el-professor-release.before-rollback-*`.

Secrets never enter git:

- `/srv/PRO*/deploy/.env`
- `/root/el-professor-secrets/staging-web-auth` (current staging user: `admon`)
- `/srv/PRO-staging/deploy/mailpit-ui-auth` (placeholder user `locked` until the owner sets a login)
- `/srv/PRO-staging/deploy/mailpit-relay.yml` (holds production's Resend key; `MAILPIT_RELAY_ALL` in staging's `.env` switches relaying on)

## Common operations

| Task | How |
|---|---|
| Deploy staging | Merge a PR into `staging`. |
| Deploy production | Merge `staging` into `main`, then Actions → Deploy → Run workflow on `main` and confirm `production`. The job refuses unless `main`'s tree is exactly what staging runs. |
| See what runs | On the host: `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND="status staging" /usr/local/sbin/el-professor-release` |
| Roll back | Actions → Rollback, which is visible once `rollback.yml` is on `main`. Or on the host: `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND="rollback staging" /usr/local/sbin/el-professor-release`. Undo it by deploying again. |
| Change routes | Edit `deploy/caddy/elprofessor.caddy`, then `EL_PROFESSOR_SSH=elprofessor bash deploy/caddy/publish.sh` |
| Change the staging login | `ssh -t root@159.195.205.30 /srv/PRO-staging/deploy/set-staging-password.sh` (without `-t` the prompts cannot read the password) |
| Staging mail relay | `ssh root@159.195.205.30 /srv/PRO-staging/deploy/set-staging-mail-relay.sh` turns it on; `--test` sends one mail to Resend's sink address; `--off` keeps mail on the host |
| Reach the host from the dev machine | `ssh elprofessor` (dedicated key; see memory) |
| Run the end-to-end tests | `cd e2e && npm install && npm run setup && npm test` (details in `e2e/README.md`) |

## Waiting on the owner

- **Two customers' logos are SVG files already sitting in a public bucket, and stopping the next upload does not remove them.** Measured on production, 28.09.2026: `company-logos` holds 7 objects — `image/jpeg`, `image/png` and **two `image/svg+xml`**, 117,755 and 101,117 bytes, belonging to two different companies. The bucket is public by design (`CompanySettingsContext` builds every logo URL with `getPublicUrl`; the Mietwagenauftrag prints it), and it is served from the API origin.

  **Why an SVG is different from the other six.** It is a document, not a picture: served inline with `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` it executes whatever script it carries. Uploading is gated on `is_company_admin()`, so until `20260928100000` the type list in `SettingsPage` was the only thing between a company administrator and a script running on `api.elprofessor.eu`.

  **The bound, as narrowly as it was established.** The application keeps its Supabase session in the *app* origin's localStorage, so a script on the API origin does not read it simply by being there. What it certainly gets is execution on a trusted domain — a phishing surface and same-origin footing against whatever that origin does hold. Whether it can be carried further **was not established**, and was not the reason for the change.

  **What was done, and what was deliberately not.** `20260928100000` gives the bucket an allow-list of `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/webp` and a 2 MiB limit, and `SettingsPage` drops `image/svg+xml` from its own list. `allowed_mime_types` gates **uploads only** — it does not retro-reject what is stored. So the next SVG is refused and **the two existing ones are untouched and still served**.

  **The decision is yours**, because the alternatives all cost a customer something: leave them (they are presumably the logos those two companies chose, and no evidence says otherwise); ask the two companies to re-upload a PNG and delete the SVGs; or convert them server-side, which changes a file a customer supplied without asking. Deleting a customer's file is not ours to do.


- **The fix plan of 28.09.2026 is `Docs/audit/FIX_PLAN_2026-09-28.md`, and it is where the next session starts.** 44 agents produced it: one read-only verifier per open P0, two adversarial lenses per confirmation, four triage agents over the 53 untriaged P1s, one synthesis. Every severity in it was measured against production or staging, read-only — which is the point: **of the thirteen findings the 26.09 review filed as P0, ten affect zero rows today**, two survived as live and one turned out to be a decision. It carries fourteen ordered items, ten disjoint file groups that can be worked in parallel, the four that need SQL and one kit run, ten owner decisions, twelve findings it refuses to act on with the reason, and a section naming every claim in it that is still reasoned rather than measured.

  **The three worth knowing before anything else:**
  1. **Schicht compression appears to price the absorbed gap as worked time** — `MonatsplanungStundennachweisPreview.tsx:471` → `seg_minutes` → `calcExpectedBrutto` → `calculated_salary`, over **1,090 of 1,178 production driver-days**. If that holds it contradicts CLAUDE.md's own written invariant, that compression is display- and export-only. The euro magnitude was **not** computed; the plan says exactly what settles it.
  2. **Every premium window is a fixed millisecond offset from local midnight**, so both DST days misclassify an hour. 26 production trips already sit on 29.03.2026 and **the next transition is 25.10.2026**.
  3. **`document-templates` is a public storage bucket holding two PDFs that name a private individual**, live on production, with no authenticated route to them at all — its four policies key on `owner = auth.uid()` and every object has `owner IS NULL`. Flipping the bucket private is one migration; deleting the files is the owner's.


- **Google sign-in is LIVE on production while the Impressum and the privacy page are both empty.** Measured 28.09.2026, read-only. `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_ENABLED=true` in `/srv/PRO/deploy/.env`, and GoTrue's own `/auth/v1/settings` on `api.elprofessor.eu` answers `external.google: true`, so the switch is not merely set — the provider is serving. But `app_private.platform_settings` holds `legal.impressum` and `legal.privacy` as `{}` on production, two bytes each. `App.tsx` renders the Impressum only once its own `legal_name` is filled, so `/impressum` and `/datenschutz` show nothing.

  **Why this is written here rather than fixed:** the content is the owner's to write, and every earlier note in this file recorded the production Google switch as *waiting* on exactly these two texts. So either the decision changed and this is intended, or the switch went on ahead of the texts by accident. Only the owner knows which. For a German SaaS an Impressum is required by §5 DDG and a privacy notice by DSGVO Art. 13, and the Art. 13 duty is at its sharpest for an OAuth flow that hands an identity provider a request on the visitor's behalf.

  **What was checked at the same time and is fine:** GoTrue reports `disable_signup: false`, which is expected — its entrypoint opens signup exactly when the Google provider is on — and Kong's `auth-v1-closed` block still answers **403** on `/auth/v1/signup`, `/auth/v1/otp` and `/auth/v1/magiclink` on production, so open signup stays contained the way the design intends. Nothing about the wall has drifted.


- **Which Bundesland gets which holiday — the table itself, not the dates.** Raised 27.09.2026 while fixing Buß- und Bettag's date, and deliberately left alone, because each line of it decides whether a Feiertagszuschlag of +125% is owed. Three things are wrong or doubtful in the two tables, and none of them is a calendar calculation:
  - **`utils/germanHolidays.ts` grants Buß- und Bettag to `SN` and `TH`.** Since 1995 it has been a statutory public holiday in **Sachsen only**; in Thüringen it is not. So a Thüringen company would be paid a premium that is not owed. `pages/StundennachweisPage.tsx` has it for `SN` alone, so the two tables already disagree on this.
  - **`germanHolidays.ts` has no Reformationstag at all**, and omits Allerheiligen for `BW` — both from review finding H28, both unverified by me.
  - **The two Bundesland parsers resolve the same `company_settings.state` differently** — H27 reports `'Sachsen-Anhalt'` reading as `ST` in one and `SN` in the other. Since `SN` is the one Land that has Buß- und Bettag, that mis-read grants a premium in the wrong state.

  **Who this reaches today: nobody, measured.** `company_settings.state` on 27.09.2026 holds exactly one non-empty value across both databases — `Nordrhein-Westfalen`, one company — with 16 empty rows on production and 23 on staging. So the whole holiday-table cluster (H27, H28, M16/M26) is **latent, not live**, which is a correction to the review's P0 classification of all three. It becomes live with the first customer in a Land whose table line is wrong.

  What I did fix, because it is arithmetic and not a table: the **date** of Buß- und Bettag in both files. `germanHolidays.ts` returned 23 November itself whenever that day was a Wednesday (2022 and 2033 of 2020..2035), and `StundennachweisPage.tsx` returned a **Tuesday in every single year**. `src/utils/bussUndBettag.test.ts` pins the definition across sixteen years and keeps the shipped offsets as negative controls.


- **Ausgleichsstunden are in the expected Brutto and on the screen, but not in the Stundennachweis PDF — nor in what is stored.** Found 27.09.2026. `MonatsplanungStundennachweisPreview.tsx:1553-1565` adds each driver's adjustment total to `expected_brutto` and attaches `ausgleich_hours` / `ausgleich_amount`; the screen prints them (`:2250-2260`) and the Excel dump gives them a column of their own (`:1110`, `:1159`). But `buildPdfRows(g, …)` (`:887`, called at `:1653` and by the download button) builds its rows from the group's shifts and **never reads `ausgleich_hours`**, and `rowsForStorage` is built from those same rows — so the hours are absent from the PDF the Steuerberater receives and from the saved record. `adjustmentMap` reaches exactly three places (`:1555` the Brutto, `:2170` the deviation panel, `:3388` inside it) and the document builder is not one of them.

  **Why this needs a decision and not a patch:** CLAUDE.md states the rule as *"Ausgleichsstunden exist only in the Stundennachweis, always as Lohnart 1000 with no premium,"* which reads as "the Stundennachweis is where they belong." If that is right, the PDF is missing hours whose money it already claims, and the document does not reconcile with its own Brutto. But putting them in means **choosing a day and a time span for hours nobody worked**, in a statutory record — which cannot be invented here. An `AdjustmentRecord` carries `hours`, `amount` and a reason (`Gehaltsausgleich: Transfer von <driver>`) and **no date**.

  Two readings, and the owner picks:
  1. **They belong in this PDF.** Then the owner says on which day and at what times an Ausgleich appears — and whether it is one row or spread.
  2. **They belong in the other Stundennachweis** — `StundennachweisPage`'s generator, which synthesises hours to hit a Brutto target and would absorb them by construction. Then this preview's Brutto is the thing that is wrong to include them in, and the fix is on the money side, not the document side.

  Until then the mismatch is visible on screen (the card prints worked hours and Ausgleich separately) but silent in the PDF.


- **A night shift that does not cross midnight is paid no night premium at all.** Found 27.09.2026 while checking the review's claim that `nightMinsForSeg` returns 0 for such a shift. It does, and the measurement below is the product's own two functions copied verbatim (`StundennachweisPage.tsx:339-362`) against the window their own comment claims, 20:00–06:00:

  | shift | `nachtarbeit` | `nachtarbeit04` | true 20:00–06:00 | paid at +40% | night minutes lost |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 22:00–02:00 | 240 | 120 | 240 | 240 | 0 |
  | 20:00–23:00 | 180 | 0 | 180 | 180 | 0 |
  | 01:00–05:00 | **0** | 180 | 240 | **0** | **240** |
  | 03:00–08:00 | **0** | 60 | 180 | **0** | **180** |
  | 00:00–06:00 | **0** | 240 | 360 | **0** | **360** |
  | 04:00–06:00 | **0** | **0** | 120 | **0** | **120** |
  | 05:00–09:00 | **0** | **0** | 60 | **0** | **60** |

  The cause is exact: `seg1` takes `max(f, 20*60)` so it is empty for a shift starting before 20:00, and `seg2` needs `absTo > 24*60` so it is empty for a shift that does not cross midnight. **There is no term for the part of a shift that lies in 00:00–06:00 of its own day.** Only `nachtarbeit` feeds money (`:585`, the 1501 +40% line); `nachtarbeit04` is a display column and enters no total, so the review's second claim — that the two overlapping columns double-count — **is wrong, and is recorded as wrong.** The error is one-directional: night work is **under**-credited, never over.

  **This is a decision and not a fix, because three contracts for this one column contradict each other and only §3b can choose:**
  1. the function's own comment — "night minutes (20:00–06:00)", one flat +40%, "there is no separate 00–04 tier";
  2. the generator's — `nachtarbeit` is 20:00–23:59 and `nacht04` is 00:00–04:00, disjoint, which it enforces by writing `nachtarbeit04` and **clearing** `nachtarbeit` on those rows (`:1689-1690`, `:1744-1745`) — and its comment says `calcRow` "would count 00:00–06:00 as nachtarbeit", which the measurement above shows it does not, so that override was written against an assumption that is already false;
  3. CLAUDE.md's — `1500 = nightSec − night0to4Sec` at 25% and `1501 = night0to4Sec` at 40%, two mutually exclusive wage types, which is a third model again.

  Fixing the arithmetic to match any one of the three changes what a Stundennachweis pays, and picking one is the §3b judgement `Docs/audit/REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` says the payroll work is blocked on. The measurement is here so the decision starts from numbers. **Note the interaction:** correcting (1) makes the generator's clearing load-bearing for the first time, so the two must be decided together.


- **Which of the three treatments of 4:30 and 8:30 is the rule?** Found while fixing the dead max-3-Schichten check (PR for `fix/shift-limits-honest`). The same two constants, 270 and 510 minutes, are used three different ways in the product, and only one of them can be what the Stundennachweis is supposed to say:
  - **A block.** `StundennachweisPage.tsx:411-418` states the rule outright — each Schicht at most 4:30, the day at most 8:30 — and `validateRowDurations` enforces it: `hasDurationViolations` disables Speichern and PDF-Export and returns early from both handlers (`:3562`, `:3584`, `:3718`, `:3727`). A day over the limit cannot be saved or exported, with no override.
  - **A silent cap.** `AbnahmeDiagnosticPage.tsx:116-125` and the Stundennachweis generator (`StundennachweisPage.tsx:933`, `:962`, `:1458`, `:1574`, `:1797`) clamp with `Math.min(..., MAX_SHIFT, MAX_DAY - dayTotal)`. Under that treatment a driver who worked 9:00 is recorded as 8:30 and nothing is refused.
  - **Nothing at all.** Both planning screens declared the constants and never reached the condition that used them. That half is what this PR fixes.

  **The question for the owner is which treatment is correct for a real day over 8:30**: refuse the document, or record the capped figure. It decides whether a pre-flight gate in `FinalizePlanningModal` is possible — a gate is only correct under the first treatment, and only if it measures the same number `validateRowDurations` does, which is the three built rows and not the raw merged windows. Measured on staging 26.09.2026 the blast radius is small and known: **2 of 1178 driver-days** exceed 8:30 (2026-08-23 at 575 min and 2026-08-24 at 520 min), and **none** has a single merged shift over 4:30. Until it is answered both screens warn and name the day, and neither blocks. This is a §3b-adjacent judgement about what a statutory record may say, not a code decision.

The loss list is 15 rows, **three addresses, all `viewer`, all in Y&A Mobility**: `ahmedjaberdev@gmail.com` (active), `ahmedkhhemaid@gmail.com` and `yassin@yamobility.de`, each losing `cars`, `cash-balance` and `documents`. That is the hole the slice exists to close, not a regression. `supervisor` appears nowhere in the list and keeps everything. **Separately noticed and not slice C's:** `yassin@yamobility.de` has duplicate `user_profiles` rows.

- ~~**The Belege week filter drops the week's Sunday**~~ — **fixed 27.09.2026** (`fix/receipt-date-range`). It was not only the Sunday: every receipt on the last day of the selected week **or business month** was dropped — from the list, the total, the Excel export and the summary PDF the Steuerberater receives. The comparison was made on instants while the two range paths built them with different meanings: a custom range parses `YYYY-MM-DD` as **UTC** midnight, a week or business month is **local** midnight, and a receipt's date is UTC too, so it sat later than a local-midnight end bound. Measured on production: **93 of 915** receipts fall on a Sunday and **31** on a month's last day. It now compares calendar days as strings, so no timezone is assumed, and the custom range — whose bounds were both UTC and which therefore always worked — is unchanged and pinned by a test.

## Gotchas learned the hard way

- **`tests/deploy/google-signin-guards.test.ts` fails under parallel load, and passes alone.** Seen 27.09.2026: in a full `npx vitest run` on a loaded machine, 8 of its 22 tests failed with the guard's output arriving as an empty string; the same file run on its own passed 15 with 7 skipped. The file shells out to `deploy.sh`'s guard for every case and spends ~156 s in subprocesses, so under contention a spawn returns nothing and the assertion compares `''`. **The failures are not a regression and not a flaky guard** — the guard is fine; the test's way of invoking it is. Worth making it robust (assert on a non-empty output first, or serialise the file) before it wastes someone's afternoon, since an empty answer currently reads as "the guard refused nothing".


- **ridy is off limits.** Its deploys reset its Caddyfile. The guard restored our section on both of ridy's deploys on 14.09.2026 (10:57 and 11:11 CEST).
- **`gh pr merge --delete-branch` switches the local checkout to the local base branch,** which may be stale. Pull right after.
- **`workflow_dispatch` workflows only become runnable once the file is on the default branch** (`main`).
- **Quoting through `ssh '…'` turns `$[` into bash arithmetic expansion.** Send scripts with `ssh host bash -s <<'EOF'` instead.
- **Caddy accepts any `@name` as a named matcher,** so a template placeholder must be an invalid directive to fail closed.
- **The `Enter passphrase for key … id_ed25519` prompt is local to your machine.** Pressing Enter falls back to the server password.
- **GoTrue v2.170.0 only rate-limits when `GOTRUE_RATE_LIMIT_HEADER` is set;** otherwise it silently does nothing.
- **Playwright applies the config's `httpCredentials` to every request context, including `request.newContext()`.** A "visitor without a login" built that way silently sends the test login. Use Node's `fetch` for anonymous checks.
- **supabase-js strips the URL hash while it creates a session from a link.** Anything that needs `type=` or `error=` must read it before the client is created; see `authRedirect` in `src/lib/supabase.ts`.
- **A single-file bind mount whose file is missing becomes an empty directory.** Mailpit then cannot read `mailpit-relay.yml` and does not start, or starts without its UI login. `deploy.sh` checks both files before a staging release.
- **"No buffer space available" / `ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE` on the dev machine means it ran out of sockets,** not that the host is down. On 14.09.2026 the MyPublicWiFi service held 16,000 bound sockets, and e2e, git and ssh all failed until it let them go. It came back the same day, and six agents died after five hours with `ENOTFOUND`. Stopping the service is the only real fix.
- **Mailpit logs a relay only when it fails;** success is a debug line, so a quiet log is the good case. To see what Resend did with a mail, list `GET https://api.resend.com/emails` with production's key (pass the header on stdin with `curl -H @-`) and read `last_event`. Print only the fields you need: the list holds customers' addresses.
- **A `grep` that matches nothing ends a `set -euo pipefail` script silently** — the same trap as the `.env` reads in `deploy.sh`. It hid the result of the first relay test, which had in fact been delivered.
- **On Windows, vitest rewrites the golden `.snap` files with LF line endings.** The content is byte-identical and `git diff` is empty, but with `core.autocrlf=true` git lists them as modified. Restore them with `git checkout -- src/utils/__snapshots__/` and never commit them. A `.gitattributes` line `src/utils/__snapshots__/*.snap text eol=lf` would end the churn.
- **A release copies only part of `deploy/`.** `install_release` copies the compose file, `kong.yml`, `nginx-spa.conf`, `deploy.sh`, `init-db`, the functions, the migrations and `dist`. Any other script the host runs (`set-*.sh`, `otp-housekeeping.sh`) stays at the copy installed by hand, so reinstall it when it changes.
- **Supabase grants EXECUTE on every new function to PUBLIC, anon and authenticated.** A function meant for service_role only needs all three revoked; `20260911120000` missed anon. A guard written as `auth.email() != '…'` or `auth.uid() <> …` lets an anonymous caller through, because the comparison is NULL. For every SECURITY DEFINER function a PR adds, check `has_function_privilege('anon', …)`.
- **Never delete a PR's branch before `gh pr view N --json state` says MERGED.** On 15.09.2026 a merge failed on a GitHub TLS timeout, the script deleted the branch anyway, and GitHub closed PR #11 unmerged. Pushing the head commit back and `gh pr reopen` recovered it.
- **An inline `style` beats a Tailwind class.** Most screens set their layout in `style={{…}}`, which cannot change with the screen width. A class such as `hidden md:flex` next to an inline `display` hides nothing. On 15.09.2026 that kept the landing page two to four columns wide on phones. Anything that has to follow the viewport (grid columns, padding, display) belongs in classes, not in the style object.
- **The Workflow tool does not save an agent's worktree.** When a run is cut off, commit what is on disk (as `wip:`) before relaunching; a resumed run only replays agents that had finished.
- **Nothing creates a `user_profiles` row by trigger.** `auth.users` carries only `mark_pending_oauth_signup` and `sync_user_profile_email`; `public.handle_new_user` exists and nothing invokes it. Every profile is written by a signup RPC. Any code or test that assumes a profile appears on its own is wrong — and `tests/rls/oauth-signup.sql` creates that trigger in its own prelude, so the RLS kit cannot catch the assumption.
- **Production's compose reads the GoTrue signup switch as `DISABLE_SIGNUP`, not `GOTRUE_DISABLE_SIGNUP`.** Setting the prefixed name there changes nothing and reads as if it had.
- **A platform account cannot be hard-deleted once it has issued activation codes.** The delete fails with `23503` on `activation_codes_created_by_fkey`, and forcing it would destroy real history. Disable the staff row instead; that is what was done to `admin@yamobility.de` on staging.
- **`otp-request` answers 202 for the public purposes whatever happened**, including when the address has spent its five codes for the hour. That is deliberate — the endpoint must not reveal whether an address has an account — but it means "the mail never arrived" and "we refused to send it" look identical from outside. When a code does not turn up, count the hour's sends in `app_private.otp_send_events` before suspecting the mailer.
- **A local resolver that refuses a name is not a missing DNS record.** On the dev machine `nslookup mail-staging.elprofessor.eu` answered `Query refused` from a local resolver while the record was perfectly fine — it resolves through `1.1.1.1` and the site answers. Same family as the MyPublicWiFi socket problem below: check against a public resolver, or with `curl -w '%{remote_ip}'`, before reporting DNS as missing.
- **A PostgreSQL regular expression takes its greediness from its first quantifier** (manual 9.7.3.5), not per atom as in Perl or JavaScript. `update\s+companies\s+set(.*?);` is therefore greedy as a whole, because `\s+` comes first, and it runs to the *last* semicolon of a function body rather than the first. U1's `app_private.companies_set_clauses` has that shape. It is harmless for the checks that use it today, but it is not a pattern to copy. Bound a capture with a negated class instead, as `[^;]*` in `app_private.subscription_column_writes()` does.
- **An engine never calls `t(...)`; it names a key beside its German text.** The pure modules under `src/utils/` are golden-mastered and feed exports and audit rows, so a message keeps its German string and gains `messageKey` / `messageVars` (or `<field>Key` / `<field>Vars`); the screen renders `engineText(t, ...)` from `src/i18n/engineMessage.ts` and falls back to the German. Two traps: a German dictionary entry must render **exactly** what the engine already produced, or turning a key on rewords the German UI (which is why `engine.phase3.repair.noValidMove*` is English in `de.ts`); and a German string that something **matches on** gets no key at all — `warningCategories.classify()`, `evaluateManualCandidate`'s `blockingChecks`, and the persisted `manual_exception.reason`. `src/i18n/engineMessages.test.ts` reads the engines' source and fails the build on a key missing from either dictionary.
- **A Recharts `dataKey` is also the key on the datum, so translating one without the other empties the chart.** The Overview's 6-month trend and driver-performance charts read `dataKey={t('overview.kpi.umsatz')}` off objects keyed `Umsatz`, `Kosten`, `Gewinn`, `Gehälter` — correct in German and, in the English UI, four series resolving to keys no datum had, so **both charts drew nothing and reported no error**. Both sides now come from the same `t(...)` call, and `TREND_SERIES` in `overviewHelpers.ts` is the one place naming the trend series. The same shape is worth checking on any chart whose series label is translated. Note this is *not* the engine-message pattern next door: a series name has no second life — nothing matches, classifies or audits it — so it is a plain `t(...)`, not a German string carrying a key.
- **A screen label follows the UI language; a string something else reads does not.** The two are easy to confuse, and the test is what *else* consumes the string. Left German deliberately and worth knowing before "finishing" the conversion: `wageLabel` in `HistoricalReconciliationWizard` (drawn into the Stundennachweis PDF); `MONTH_NAMES` / `MONTHS_DE` in Abrechnung, Dokumente and `InvoiceDetailModal` (date formatting per `src/i18n/index.ts`, and the Abrechnung copy also builds the exported image's filename); `Woche N` in `IncomeManagement` (written into `income.description` and matched back with a regex); `ReceiptModal`'s category values and the `'Unsicher'` OCR prefixes; `SalaryDetailsModal`'s `description` / `payment_category`; `'Deutschland'` as a stored default. Each carries a comment saying so at its site.
- **The refusal sentence a platform screen shows is the database's, not the browser's.** `callPlatformRpc` throws `result.error`, a German sentence the RPC wrote; only the "answer was unrecognisable" fallback belongs to us. Translating that fallback alone would thread `t` through a dozen call sites to change the one message a user almost never sees, while every real refusal stayed German. If platform refusals should read in English, that is a server-side change to the RPCs' `error` text, not a screen change.
- **The platform panel moved to `/admin`.** `/super-admin/*` is a rewrite, not a second route table, and the in-app links still point at the old path on purpose. Slice 3.13 removes the rewrite and those links **together**.

## Keeping this file honest

**One deliberate exception, recorded on 16.09.2026.** While several agents worked in parallel, the feature PRs of that afternoon (#55 and #57, and the panel work around them) deliberately left the four status documents alone, because every one of them would have collided on the same four files and cost a force-push. That was an explicit decision, not an oversight, and this batch is how it was paid back — in one PR, naming what each skipped change was. The rule below still stands: skipping it is a decision someone has to take on purpose and settle immediately afterwards, never something that quietly becomes the habit.

Every fix or feature updates, in the same PR:

- this file
- `PROGRESS.md`
- the "Project status" section of `README.md`
- the progress table in `el-professor-plan.html`
- `CLAUDE.md`, when a rule or mechanism changed
